# Engine backlog inbox

Content agents append their engine and canvas limitations here, under `Raised later (<course> level <n>):`, as bullets. An integration pass numbers them into `ENGINE-BACKLOG.md` and clears this file.


Raised later (cpp-pricing level 1):

- C++ runtime (libc++): a range-for over a braced list (`for (double x : {1.0, 2.0})`) fails to compile unless `<initializer_list>` is included directly or by another header; worth a line in AUTHORING section 3's C++ addendum.
- code widget (C++ and Python): `isclose` accepts any two numbers within 1e-12 of each other, so tiny values such as Φ(−10) = 7.6e-24 or 1 − cos(1e-8) cannot be tested directly. A per-case `rel` or `abs` tolerance on code tests is needed; authors currently write `f(x) / exact - 1` with expect 0, or scale the value up.
- `grade.js` `fmtNum`: rounds the test hook's and Show solution's numeric answer to 6 decimal places, so an answer like 9.5e-7 is filled in as 0.000001 and fails its own tolerance. Format with significant figures instead.
- plane canvas derive: a single `area` object cannot be targeted by its id (`A.to`); only `area.to` works. Either support it or document it in README-B.
- bars `sim`: it draws from the app's JavaScript generator, so a histogram cannot show C++ (libc++) draws live. Allowing `data` per control stop, or a seeded C++ source, would avoid precomputed derive tables.

Raised later (stat-learning level 2):

- plane canvas: the x-axis is always drawn at y = 0 when the y range spans 0, with its tick labels inside the plot, so signed coefficient paths (ridge and lasso paths against λ) run over the ticks. An `xAxis: "bottom"` option, the twin of `yAxis: "left"`, is needed; stat-learning l7 and l8 plot |β̂_j| instead.
- validator vs README-B: plane `functions` entries of the documented contour family (`{ "contour": "...", "levels": [...] }`, integration pass 5) are rejected ("a function needs expr, or x and y, or implicit"). stat-learning l8 draws each error ellipse as its own `implicit` curve. Either the validator should accept `contour`, or README-B should drop it.
- series canvas: the x axis is the array index only, so a curve against a non-integer or log-spaced grid (test MSE against λ = 0.01, 0.1, 1, 10, ...) cannot carry its real x values. Needed: numeric x values per series (or `xValues`), with `xScale: "log"` honouring them.
- derive: there is `matmul` but no linear solve or inverse, so ridge coefficients for correlated features cannot follow a λ slider live. stat-learning l7 and l8 use a design with uncorrelated features so the paths are closed-form; a `solve(A, b)` (small matrices) would allow real data.
- pair of bars side by side shrinks both charts until category labels and values are unreadable at the 540px column; stat-learning l9 sets `stack: true`. The pair could stack bars automatically below a minimum scale, as it already does for matrices.

Raised later (cpp level 2):

- C++ runtime (WebAssembly): undefined behaviour does not trap, so no lesson can show a memory bug failing in the browser. Probed: `*nullptr` read returns whatever is at address 0 (printed 1 and 0) and a null write succeeds; a pointer or reference to a dead local reads the stale value (101.25), or a later frame's value (7) once another call reuses the slot; use-after-free reads stale bytes; double `delete`, `delete[]`/`delete` mismatch and `delete` of a stack object all run silently with exit status 0; a reference into a vector after a reallocating `push_back` read 2.93699e-306. Only clang's compile-time warnings (`-Wreturn-stack-address`, `-Wmismatched-new-delete`) appear. Lessons teach these through `console` canvases of real g++ `-fsanitize=address` output from a Linux build. Needed: an AddressSanitizer-like heap and stack checker in the sysroot (even a guard-page allocator with free-list poisoning and a null page) so `compare: "raises"` could cover use-after-free, double free and null reads.
- C++ runtime: when clang can see the UB (a null pointer inlined into a dereference, `*std::unique_ptr` known empty), it emits `unreachable` and the case crashes, but the same code behind a non-inlined call returns 0. Crash or no crash depends on inlining, so authors must never use `compare: "raises"` for UB; worth a sentence in AUTHORING's C++ addendum.
- C++ runtime: pointers are 4 bytes (wasm32), so `sizeof(void*)` is 4 and `sizeof(std::vector<double>)` is 12, against 8 and 24 on a 64-bit desk; lessons that print sizes or addresses have to say so. Printed stack addresses are near 0x7fffe0 because of `--stack-first`.
- C++ code widget: there is no built-in leak check. Level 2 hides a counting `operator new[]`/`operator delete[]` replacement in the `prelude`, whose static destructor exits with status 1 when a block was never `delete[]`d (this also catches `delete` on `new[]` memory). A widget option such as `"leakCheck": true` that does this for `new`, `new[]` and `malloc` would save authors copying 25 lines.
- C++ code widget: function-mode tests cannot check a learner's signature (by value against `const&`) except by a `static_assert` in the `postlude`; clang's message then quotes the whole `std::is_same<...>` before the author's text. A `"signature": "double total_value(const std::vector<double>&, const std::vector<double>&)"` field that the harness checks with its own readable message would help.
- graph canvas: `nodeRadius` sets both the circle size and the frame padding (`r + 26` horizontally, `r + 22` vertically), so a graph with one long rect label and `height` near 200 squashes the nodes into a thin band; level 2 sets `nodeRadius: 20` and `height: 220` to 240 everywhere. Padding should follow the actual node extents.
- manipulate widget: it is not counted as a canvas-bound widget for `explore` screens, so a stepped call stack (array canvas in stack mode, patched by each state) has to be a `problem` screen.
- console canvas: lines wider than about 54 characters scroll sideways with no visual cue, which hides the end of compiler and sanitiser messages; real clang and AddressSanitizer lines are 60 to 90 characters, so authors trim them by hand. A `wrap: true` option (with a hanging indent) would let real tool output be shown verbatim.

Raised later (cpp-pricing level 2):

- C++ code widget: `isclose` is fixed at a relative 1e-9 and cases take no `tol`, so "to 1e-6" or "within 0.005 of 10.45" tests must be written as `std::abs(x - v) < tol` with `expect: true`, whose failure reads only "Expected true, got false". Needed: `tol: { "abs": x }` / `{ "rel": r }` on C++ (and Python) cases, shown in the row.
- series canvas sim: `running: "mean"` has no error band, so a Monte Carlo convergence chart cannot show ±2 SE live; cpp-pricing l8 draws the band from precomputed C++ values as two extra dashed series, and the explore's live line uses the browser RNG, not the C++ stream. Needed: `band: 2` on a running-mean sim (mean ± k·sd/√i, shaded).
- graph canvas: a recombining lattice (binomial tree) has to be hand-placed node by node, and two-line rect labels ("£112.24\n17.71") crowd at n = 3 even with `height: 370`; the `tree` canvas cannot recombine. Needed: a lattice layout (`layout: "lattice"`, `steps: n`) or a `tree` option to merge equal nodes.
- playthrough tool: in one run, `--set cpp-pricing/review-2` straight after four C++ lessons reported "the set has no items" and the homework timed out in `waitForFunction`; the same sets passed when rerun alone. Possibly a load race (other agents writing the course folder) or state left by the C++ worker; worth a retry or a clearer message.

Raised later (stat-learning level 1):

- plane: no way to let a control choose between precomputed curves (one fitted polynomial per degree). Worked round with a ternary chain in one `expr` (`d < 0.5 ? (...) : d < 1.5 ? (...) : ...`). It works, but each function's formula grows to kilobytes: `stat-learning/l3-bias-variance.json` (20 curves × 10 degrees) is 140 KB. Wanted: a function entry with `exprs: [...]` indexed by a param (`"index": "d"`), or `hide`/`show` bindable to a param.
- plane: point sets cannot be shown or hidden by a control. Revealing the held-out set in a second colour had to wait for an `explain.canvas` patch. Wanted: a `hide` list a control or `derive` can set, or a point `visible` expression.
- plane: function labels avoid curves and axes but not points, so "fitted polynomial" and "true line" are drawn over scattered data in l1, l2 and l3. Wanted: labels that also keep clear of points.
- series: a marker label at the last index collides with that series' direct end label (l2 "More data": "80.15" was drawn over "test MSE, 30 days"). Worked round by dropping the marker. Wanted: marker labels placed clear of the series end labels.
- validator: an `explain.canvas` holding a whole canvas (`{ kind, params, state }`) instead of a state patch is ignored without a warning, so the Why? sheet silently shows the unchanged canvas. Wanted: a validator warning when `explain.canvas` (or a `wrong[].canvas`) has a `kind` key.

Raised later (cpp-pricing level 3):

- C++ code widget, script mode: a `stdout` case compares the whole output and stderr is shown only when a run fails, so a graded program cannot also print timings for the learner to read. cpp-pricing l9 uses function mode instead. Needed: a stdout case that checks only matching lines (`match` regex or `lines: [..]`), or stderr shown under passing rows.
- C++ runtime: the optimisation level is fixed at `-O1` and cannot be set per widget or case (the worker's `init` already takes an `opt` knob the page never sends). A widget `flags`/`opt` field (`-O0`, `-O2`, `-DNDEBUG`, `-ffast-math`) would let l11 show debug against release, `NDEBUG` and `-ffast-math` live in the browser instead of through desk consoles.
- C++ runtime clocks: in Chromium `steady_clock` moves in 100 µs steps (`performance.now()` is coarsened because the app is not cross-origin isolated) and `system_clock` in 1 ms steps (`Date.now()`). Serving the app with COOP/COEP headers would give about 5 µs; until then timing lessons must batch work (l9 teaches it as the edge case).
- C++ code widget: `prelude` is per widget, not per case, so "the learner's test must fail against version A and pass against version B" needs every hidden pricer behind a function pointer that each case's `setup` swaps. A per-case `prelude` (or `defines: { "BUGGY": 1 }` compiled once per distinct set) would make planted-bug tests simpler and closer to real code.
- C++ code widget: there is no shared test kit. l12 and homework-3 copy a 25-line `TEST`/`CHECK`/`CHECK_CLOSE` registry into each prelude and a `run_tests()` into each postlude; `cpp` l18 will need the same. A built-in option (`"testkit": true`) or a shared include file would remove the copies.
- series canvas: x is the array index only, so time against n = 100, 200, 400, 800 is drawn on an "n ÷ 100" axis with markers placed by `y` (same need as stat-learning's `xValues` bullet).
- grid canvas: `state.paths` draw their dots and lines through the cell centres, over the cell text, so a route across numbered cells hides the numbers it passes. l10 uses shaded sets instead. Paths could offset below the text, or the text could get a halo.
- boxes canvas: with four `box` containers of three `card` items, card labels shrink until "→ row 0" shows as "row"; l10 uses "→0". A minimum label size (wrapping containers to a second row) would keep labels legible.

Raised later (stat-learning level 3):

- validator vs README-B: plane `heat` function entries (integration pass 5) are rejected like `contour` ("a function needs expr, ..."). l11 wanted the model's default probability as a heat map behind the moving decision boundary; it was dropped. Either accept `heat` (and `contour`) or remove them from README-B.
- plane: no classified-region layer, so a k-NN decision region cannot be filled. l11 draws the 1-NN boundary as 335 marching-squares `segments` computed offline. Wanted: a grid layer (`cells: { x, y, values }`) or a `heat` whose expression can read a precomputed grid.
- plane: a point with `on` can ride only a function, not a polyline of `segments` or a list of points, so the ROC point cannot be dragged along the empirical curve. l12 moves it with a slider over a threshold index and `derive` reading arrays in params. Wanted: `on` a points list (piecewise linear) or a `polyline` function entry.
- controls: no categorical control. l13's model switch is a 0-to-1 slider whose readout shows "0" and "1", with the model's name only in a ternary caption. Wanted: `options: ["logistic", "k-NN, k = 3"]` on a control, binding the index.
- series: lines cannot be shown or hidden by a control, and `reveal` applies to every series at once. l14's P&L explore wanted "toggle costs on" and "add the coin" steps. Wanted: `hide` bindable to a control (one more vote for the stat-learning level 1 request for plane points).
- code widget (Python): `isclose` is fixed at rel 1e-9, so scikit-learn outputs are rounded to 4 places in each call and a value on a rounding boundary could fail across platforms (none did in Pyodide). One more vote for per-case `tol` (raised for C++ in cpp-pricing level 2).

Content note (cpp-pricing): level 2 names its payoff classes Call/Put and level 3 CallPayoff/PutPayoff; the cpp course never teaches inheritance, so cpp-pricing l5 teaches virtual functions from scratch. The topic 1.9 second reader should check the handover and decide whether cpp level 3 needs one screen on inheritance and `virtual`.

Raised later (ensembles level 1):

- tree canvas: `state.collapse: [""]` (collapsing the root, to show depth 0) throws `ReferenceError: Cannot access 'b' before initialization` in `tree.js` `place()`: the fallback `top + b / 2` reads the `const b` declared later in the same function. l1's growth slider starts at depth 1. Fix: rename the inner `b`.
- tree canvas: node labels are params only; no state patch changes a node's pill text. A decision tree cannot show an inner node's question while expanded and its counts while collapsed. l1 and l3 put counts in pills, questions on branch labels (`p`) and predictions in values. Wanted: `state.labels: { "<path>": "text" }`.
- validator: a `tap-regions` answer naming a plane polygon is rejected ("region is not on the plane canvas"), although the engine makes polygons tap targets (pass 5). l1 wanted "tap the leaf rectangle the new loan lands in"; it asks for the training loans in that leaf instead. The validator should collect `state.polygons` (and area) ids.
- controls: no categorical control, again. l3's "Tree (1: depth 1, 2: depth 3, 3: unlimited)" and l4's leaf-size "Setting" sliders show indices, with the meaning only in the caption. One more vote for `options: [...]` on a control.
- plane: no step-function entry. l3 and l4 draw leaf rectangles and regression-tree steps as precomputed `polygons` and `segments` per setting, swapped by `derive` reading arrays in params. Wanted: a `steps` function entry (breakpoints and levels, closed and open ends) that a slider could switch.
- tree canvas in a stacked `pair`: on two depth-2 trees with long branch labels, labels of neighbouring subtrees sit close (l3 bootstrap pair, "leverage > 3.56" just above "leverage ≤ 4.62"). Readable, but a little more label spacing would help.

Raised later (cpp level 3):

- C++ runtime (precompiled headers): once the builder has made a PCH for a unit's leading `#include` lines, an `#include` of any other header later in the unit fails, for example in the `postlude` after the learner's code. The message is `fatal error: cannot open file '/usr/include/c++/v1/cstdint': Out of memory`. The same source compiles without the PCH, so the failure depends on timing: cpp l12 screen 8 passed alone and failed after earlier lessons. Workaround used: every header the postlude needs goes in the prelude's leading includes. Needed: the worker's file system in PCH mode should serve the full header tree, or the worker should retry without the PCH on such an error. Until then, a line in AUTHORING's C++ addendum.
- validator: a C++ case whose call returns `std::string` text that looks like a number (`Fixed<2>::from_units(1234).str()` expecting `"12.34"`) warns "expect is the text ...; a number is written without quotes". The warning cannot know the return type. It should be silenced by a per-case flag such as `"text": true`, or worded as a question.
- C++ runtime: there is no unwinder, so `catch` never runs. Invariant lessons therefore cannot show the caller recovering from a refused operation, or test the strong guarantee (a refused `sell` leaves the position unchanged, checked after the catch). Tests can only check that the program stops (`raises`). Needed: libunwind (or the wasm exception-handling proposal) in the sysroot.
- C++ code widget: there is no "must not compile" case. A `private` member written from outside, `Pnl + Quantity`, a deleted copy and a missing `const` can only be shown in `console` canvases. A deleted copy alone can be tested, through `std::is_copy_constructible_v`. Needed: a case shape such as `{ "compiles": "Connection b = a;", "expect": false }`, compiled as an extra snippet after the learner's code, with the first `error:` line shown.
- boxes canvas: `card` items with code labels (`set_bid(b)`, `spread()`) in a container with several cards per row are drawn narrower than their text, so the label touches or overflows the card edge. cpp l10 used `cols: 2` and shorter labels. Card width should come from the measured label in the code face.
- C++ code widget: learner code is compiled without `-Wall`, so `-Wreorder-ctor`, `-Wuninitialized` and `-Wreturn-stack-address` never appear under the editor. cpp l11 teaches member initialisation order through a `console` of clang `-Wall` output instead. Needed: `-Wall` by default, or a widget field such as `"warnings": ["-Wall"]`.

Raised later (fin-ml level 1):

- series: only markers are tap targets. "Tap the barrier hit first" (fin-ml l2) puts marker dots on the barrier lines, because `hlines` and the `window` cannot be tapped. Wanted: `hlines` ids and the `window` as tap targets.
- series: no vertical reference line. The triple-barrier time limit is drawn as the right edge of `window`. Wanted: `vlines: [{ id, i, label }]`, as plane has, patchable so a slider on h can move it.
- graph: with `shape: "rect"` nodes, directed edges showed no visible arrowheads at 1280 or 390 px, and `bend` on an edge passing behind a middle node drew it straight through that node (fin-ml l3's two-stage picture; the edge was dropped and the layout changed). Worth checking arrowheads on rect nodes and `bend` on straight runs.
- no canvas for overlapping intervals over a day axis. fin-ml l4 draws label windows as plane `segments` in a stacked `pair` with an `array` of days; the array takes only one `highlight.range`. Wanted: `highlight.ranges: [...]` on array, or a timeline kind (rows of intervals over tappable day cells).
- code widget (Python): `isclose` is fixed at rel 1e-9, so scikit-learn probabilities are tested with `bool(np.allclose(..., atol=0.002))` calls whose failure shows only "False". One more vote for a per-case `tol`.

Raised later (cpp level 4):

- C++ code widget: there is no test framework and no `try`/`catch`, so writing tests needs a hand-made stand-in in the prelude. Level 4 hides about 25 lines: `TEST(name)`, `CHECK`, `CHECK_CLOSE`, a registry, and a postlude `main` that prints `N passed, M failed`. Planted bugs are chosen by a number on `stdin`, which prints `planted bug k: caught/missed`. Needed: a shipped header-only test kit (`"testKit": true`) and a way to run the learner's tests against planted variants without routing through `stdin`, as the pytest course's `outcome(planted)` does. `REQUIRE_THROWS`-style tests stay impossible until the sysroot has an unwinder.
- C++ code widget, script mode: stderr is never shown for a run that exits 0, so the stand-in's per-check detail (`line 12: CHECK_CLOSE(...) failed`) is invisible exactly when a learner needs it. The detail cannot go to stdout, because the case compares the whole of stdout. Needed: stderr shown, folded, under a failing stdout row, or a compare mode that checks only the last line.
- C++ code widget: function-mode `call` expressions that end in a member access or index on a returned temporary (`sort_book(...)[0].price`, `price_lattice(...)[3]`) return a dangling reference through the harness's `decltype(auto)`, giving garbage or "memory access out of bounds". Authors must use `setup` (`auto b = ...;`) first. The harness could decay the result (`auto v = expr;`) when the expression is not a plain call, or AUTHORING could say so.
- C++ code widget: there is no way to compile several translation units, so separate compilation (header, `.cpp`, linker) is simulated by putting the header in the `prelude`. A real "two files, one link" exercise, such as a `files: { "rates.h": ..., "main.cpp": ... }` option, would let l17 show a genuine multi-file build.
- Why sheet (explanation copy) with `derive`: an `explain.canvas` that changes a derived parameter (`params.n` 3 to 5 on the l14 array explore) was drawn mid-tween. The caption and cells showed n = 5, but the derived `active` and `done` showed n = 4. l14 now repeats every derived key in the explain patch. The copy should run derive on the final state, or apply the patch instantly.
- array canvas: a cell count that grows through `derive` (cells from 4 to 8) was laid out on two rows in the Why sheet without `wrap`, with the caption on top of the second row. l14 now keeps the length fixed and dims unused slots with `active`. Layout should follow the current number of cells.
- boxes canvas: cards in a `row` container keep the width of a slot, not of their label, so "BARC −200" overflows its slot (l15 hook). Labels were cut to the keys alone. Row slots should take the widest card's width, as other containers do.

Raised later (validation level 2):

- No time-line canvas for folds. validation l5 and l6 use a one-row `grid` (15 day cells, or 6 blocks), whose cells shrink to about 18 px at 390 px wide, too small to tap reliably. `array` has only one `highlight.range` and no per-cell colour, so it cannot show train, test, purge and embargo at once. Wanted: a timeline kind (a row of wide cells or ranges over a day axis, shade sets per role, hatched purge and embargo, tappable cells), which would also serve fin-ml l4.
- Stepping a canvas through authored states needs nested ternaries in `derive` (`"shade.test.ids": "fold < 1.5 ? [...] : fold < 2.5 ? [...] : ..."`, one for train, test and caption each). Wanted: a control that selects one of a list of authored state patches (`"frames": [{...}, ...]`, bound to a slider), so "slide through the folds" is data, not expressions.
- plane `segments` are not tap targets. The l7 overlap picture makes each row a tappable dot beside its label bar; tapping the bar itself (the label window) is the natural action. Wanted: segments (and polygons already are) in tap mode.
- number-line has no zoom or scale break: a 5-row `gap` on a 0 to 1,000 axis draws as a dot between the train and test intervals (l6). Wanted: a zoom inset or `state.view` as plane has.
- Python code widget: `isclose` is fixed at rel 1e-9, so scikit-learn scores are rounded to 4 places in the call and compared exactly; a platform difference in the fifth place would flip a rounding digit. Another vote for a per-case `tol`.

Raised later (fin-ml level 3):

- longform widget: rejected in lesson files ("longform belongs in homework and exams"), so l12's "draft the what-failed paragraph against a rubric" became a `multi` that picks good sentences. Wanted: a self-marked draft widget for lessons (free text, then the rubric and a model paragraph, ungraded for progress).
- series canvas: series values are fixed arrays, so a cost slider cannot redraw cumulative net P&L live (net_t = gross_t − c·traded_t); l10 shows total net P&L against cost on a `plane` instead. Wanted: a series whose values are an expression over data arrays and params (`values: { "expr": "gross - c * traded", "data": {...} }`), or derive writing whole series.
- plane canvas: no `xScale: "log"`, so the DSR against the number of trials could only be drawn for N = 2 to 30; N = 1 to 1,000 went to a `bars` chart. Wanted: log x on plane, as series and number-line already have.
- curve formulas and derive: `Phi` exists but no inverse; l11's DSR and E[max] curves inline a 300-character Abramowitz-Stegun approximation (error about 4.5e-4). Wanted: `PhiInv(p)` (and `NormInv`) in curve formulas and derive.
- table widget: `bind` reaches tree branches and grid cells only, so values a learner types (three Sharpe standard errors) cannot draw on a bars or plane canvas as they type. Wanted: `bind: "values.<category>"` for bars and `bind: "points.<id>.y"` for plane.
- Python code widget: another vote for a per-case `tol`; the scikit-learn study is compared after rounding to 4 places with `isclose` at 1e-9, so a fifth-place platform difference would flip a digit.
- graph canvas: rect nodes with labels of about 18 characters overlap at four per row in the 540 px column (labels are clipped, "dels and trial log"), and the frame does not widen or warn; l12 uses three per row. Wanted: node spacing checked against measured label widths, or automatic wrapping.

Raised later (validation level 3):

- sim outcomes (bars and series `sim`): `max` and `min` are `Math.max`/`Math.min` and take no list, so `max(sample(N, n()))` is not possible. The best of 40 had to be written as 40 copies of the term (2 KB outcome strings in l12) and no slider can change N. Wanted: list-aware `max`/`min` (and `argmax`), so a best-of-N simulation follows a control on N.
- expression widget: Compute Engine parses a capital `N` as its built-in numeric-evaluation function, so an answer such as `\frac{\sqrt{2\ln N}}{\sqrt{T}}` never grades as correct (the playthrough reported "not correct, key null"). l12's derive became an `order` item. Wanted: treat lone single letters as symbols in the grader, or a validator warning for `N` (and other CE built-ins such as `D`, `E` used as variables).
- bars canvas: with `showValues`, a bar of height 0 shows no value label, so "the scaling leak is 0.00" was invisible; l10 adds a caption. Wanted: a value label at the baseline for zero bars.
- graph canvas: directed edges between `rect` nodes spaced about a node-width apart show no arrowheads (the edge stops at the rims with no room), so a pipeline's order is not readable; l10 numbers the steps in their labels. Wanted: a minimum edge length or arrowheads drawn on short edges.
- number-line canvas: plain points with near-equal values (seven training days between 19 and 27 on a 10 to 70 axis) draw on top of each other with no stacking or jitter. Wanted: `stack: true` to lift coincident dots, as labels already stack.

Raised later (ensembles level 2):

- plane canvas: `heat` and `contour` take only an expression, so a fitted model's decision regions cannot be drawn. The brief's picture, a wiggly single-tree boundary against the smoother forest boundary over class points, was dropped from l5 and l6 (dozens of hand-made polygons per tree would be needed). Wanted: a region map from data, either `heat` over a lattice of values (`{ "grid": { "x": [..], "y": [..], "values": [[..]] }, "colours": {...} }`) or a list of axis-parallel rectangles with a class colour each, plus per-point colours for the class dots.
- tree canvas: an internal node shows only its question pill, so l8 screen 1 had to put each split's Gini drop (0.120, 0.040, 0.020) in the caption. Wanted: per-node annotations (`state.notes: { "y": "ΔGini 0.040" }`) and row counts beside internal nodes, which would also serve ensembles l1 to l4 and boosting.
- series canvas: x is an index (or dates), so a curve sampled at B = 5, 10, ..., 150 cannot be drawn at its true positions; l7 had to fit all 150 forests to fill every index. Wanted: numeric x values (`x: [5, 10, ...]` per series), as `bars` has `xAxis: "value"`.
- Python code widget: another vote for a per-case `tol`. Accuracies are rounded to 4 places and compared at rel 1e-9, and matching scikit-learn's `permutation_importance`, which shuffles with its own generator, needed a boolean case (`abs(mine - ref) < 0.01`) instead of a tolerance.

Raised later (fin-ml level 2):

- series: a slider cannot choose which data a line shows. fin-ml l6's "difference at d" slider preloads 11 series (d = 0, 0.1, ..., 1) and derives `hide` from `d` with `ids.slice(0, k).concat(ids.slice(k + 1))`. It works, but it multiplies the JSON and every hidden series still sets the y range and the label margin. Wanted: a series whose `values` are picked by a param (`valuesBy: { "d": { "0": [...], "0.1": [...] } }`), or `state.series` patches by id.
- series: with `x: "dates"` and `dateFormat: "%Y"`, ticks fall at evenly spaced indices and print that date's year. A Dec 2016 tick reads "2016", so a 2021 train/test boundary looked like 2020 (fin-ml l5). Wanted: with a year format, ticks at the first date of each year (or of each month for `%b`).
- plane: no polyline from data arrays. fin-ml l6's correlation and variance-ratio curves against d are 42 separate `points` with `tap: false`, and the curve labels have to be attached to single points. Wanted: `state.lines: [{ id, points: [[x, y], ...], label, colour, markers: true }]`.
- graph: as fin-ml level 1 reported, `shape: "rect"` pipeline nodes show no arrowheads on directed edges (fin-ml l8's feature-pipeline detective screen and review-2), so the flow direction comes only from the top-to-bottom layout.

Raised later (validation level 1):

- series canvas: no error bars or band, and x is an index only, so a validation curve (mean CV MSE ± fold sd against log α) could not be a `series`. l3 draws it on a `plane` from points, connecting segments and one vertical segment per error bar (27 objects, patched by id to widen the grid). Wanted: per-series `lo`/`hi` arrays (bars or a shaded band) and numeric or log x values on `series`.
- boxes canvas: four containers (a source of three job cards plus training, validation and test) overflow the 540 px column on desktop and are cut off at 390 px; `size: "medium"` or `"large"` makes it worse, and at the default size a 6-letter card label ("Report") spills past its border. l1 now starts the cards drawn above three containers at the default size. Wanted: containers that wrap onto a second row or scale to the column, and card text fitted to the card.
- bars canvas: an `hlines` label is drawn over the bars it crosses (l4's "mean only" line across 15 bars of similar height). Wanted: a label placement that keeps clear of bars, or a `labelPos` (left, right, above the plot).
- tree canvas: with `compact: true`, 16 leaves whose labels are 4 characters ("In 1") overlap in the 540 px column, though the README says 16 leaves fit; l4 uses bare digits. Wanted: leaf pills that shrink or stagger when they collide.
- Python code widget: another vote for a per-case `tol`. MSEs from scikit-learn are compared after rounding to 4 places at rel 1e-9; CPython and Pyodide agreed on every case here, but a fifth-place platform difference would flip a digit.

Raised later (arma level 1):

- validator: hint `highlight` ids for `walk` accept only `path{i}` and barriers, and for `scatter` only `p{i}` and `line`, though README-B lists walk `mean` and `spread` and scatter `fit`. arma l1 wanted to point at the ±2σ√t envelope and at the returns fit line; the hints highlight `path0` or nothing instead. Wanted: the validator's id patterns brought in line with README-B.
- series: another case of choosing data with a slider. arma l2's rolling-window explore preloads rolling means and sds for five windows per panel (22 series) and l4's drift explore five walks and five differences, each driven by a nested-ternary `hide` derive; every hidden series still sets the y range, so the drift-0 walk is squashed into a 0 to 300 axis. Wanted: a computed series (`rolling: { of: "x", w: "w", stat: "mean" | "sd" }`, or `valuesBy` a param) with a y range that follows the visible lines.
- series: with `x: "dates"` and `dateFormat: "%Y"` (already raised by fin-ml level 2), ticks at index multiples printed "2017" under Dec 2017 and a Nov 2018 training boundary looked like mid-2017. arma l1 dropped `dateFormat` and uses the default "Dec 17" labels. Wanted: year-aligned ticks.
- pair: only two panels. arma l2's hook needed a trio (a trend, a variance change, a change in memory) and l4 an I(0), I(1), I(2) trio; both offset or rescale three series inside one chart, which leaves a meaningless y axis. Wanted: a `stack` of three or more canvases, or `series` panels (`panel: 2` on a series) with their own y axes.
- walk: no mean-reverting model. arma l3 wanted the learner to reseed a random walk beside an AR(1) with a φ slider (watching φ = 0.9, 0.95, 0.98 drift towards a walk on the same shocks); it uses fixed seeded series instead. Wanted: `model: "ar1"` with `phi` (and `mu`), sharing the normal draws so a φ slider morphs the paths.
- bars: `state.cut` recolours the bars beyond a cut but nothing reports their share, so arma l3 asks "about what share lies left of −1.645" as a four-way choice. Wanted: `values.beyondCut` (count or share of `data` past `cut.at`) readable in a derive caption, so a cut slider can find the 5% point live.
- content size: arma l1 repeats the same 2,000 dates and closes across four canvases (366 KB after slimming one screen to a table). Wanted: a lesson-level `data` block that canvases reference by name (`"values": "@price"`).

Raised later (ensembles level 3):

- validator: a plane `functions` entry with `heat` (README-B, pass 5) in `params.functions` is rejected ("a function needs expr, or x and y ... or implicit"). ensembles l10's depth-1 against depth-2 heat maps put the heat entries in `state.functions` instead, which renders the same. Wanted: the validator to accept `heat` and `contour` entries in params.
- plane heat: one colour whose opacity rises with the value, so a signed surface (a boosted model's forecast over two features) needs two layers, `max(F, 0)` and `max(-(F), 0)`, each repeating a 200-tree ternary expression (about 35 KB per side of the pair). Expressions cannot index arrays (`[` is rejected by the compiler), so a precomputed grid of fitted values cannot be passed instead. Wanted: a diverging heat (`diverging: true`, colours for below and above a midpoint) and a data-grid heat (`grid: { x: [..], y: [..], z: [[..]] }`).
- controls: no list of stops. ensembles l9's "fit after 1, 5, 20 and 100 stumps" is a slider over an index 0 to 3 driving a nested ternary in the curve expression, and the readout shows 0 to 3, not the number of stumps. Wanted: `stops: [1, 5, 20, 100]` (with labels) on a control, readable by name in `expr` and derive.
- bars: category labels that collide are dropped without notice (l12's middle label "High vol, small move (202)" vanished at 540 px); l12 now uses short labels plus a caption. Wanted: labels that wrap onto two lines, or a validator warning on long labels.

Raised later (backtest level 2):

- bars and series: no way to rerun a computation from a slider. backtest l5's explore ("share of each move that happens overnight") needed the next-open Sharpe recomputed as the slider moves; it preloads eleven Sharpes from regenerated `make_bars` variants in `params.params` and reads them with `nx[round(f * 10)]` in a derive. Wanted: a control with `stops` whose value picks a preloaded series or value set by name, so authors need not hand-build the lookup.
- table canvas: a look-ahead "cut" explore wanted the learner to slide the cut date and see which rows of a full run and a cut run disagree. A table cannot recompute, so backtest l8 shows one fixed cut (row 600) as a tap-rows task. Wanted: table cells driven by a derive over preloaded arrays (`values: { "r0c2": "tgt[cut - 5]" }` with a control on `cut`).
- series: another vote for year-aligned date ticks. With `dateFormat: "%Y"`, five years of weekly points printed "2019" twice (Jan and Dec 2019); backtest l5 and l8 use `"%b %y"` instead.
- Python code widget with the pytest postlude: the learner's test file is written alone to `test_mine.py`, so it cannot see prelude names. backtest l8's pytest screen has its prelude write an `engine_lib.py` module (the engine, the check and the planted engines) into the working directory and put it on `sys.path`; the learner's file imports from it. Wanted: a documented way to ship helper modules beside the learner's test file (for example `files: { "engine_lib.py": "..." }` on the widget).

Raised later (backtest level 1):
- code widget: the import scan behind `loadPackagesFromImports` (`importsOf` in `js/engine/widgets/code.js`) reads `import numpy as np, pandas as pd` as numpy only, because the alias stops the comma list. pandas then never loads, and every test fails with key "error". The brief's shared `make_bars` generator starts with exactly that line. Workaround: each backtest prelude puts `import numpy` and `import pandas` on their own lines above the verbatim generator. Wanted: parse `import a as x, b as y` (and `from a import b as c`) correctly.
- playthrough: a code screen that fails reports only `not correct, key "error"`, with no traceback or failing test. Workaround: rebuild the prelude, solution and tests as a standalone file and run it with `pyrun.mjs` to see the error. Wanted: the first failing test's message in the failure line.
- validator: a `generalise` screen in `backtest` must state its rule in KaTeX. The fenced-code-block allowance applies only to courses on the code track, and `backtest`, a guided Python build, is not on it. Workaround: a KaTeX line ($\text{target}_t = f(\text{bar}_0, \ldots, \text{bar}_t)$, the order identity, the cache key) beside the code block. Wanted: `backtest` (and other Stage 2 project courses) treated as code courses for this rule.
- series: no step style, so a target or position path (constant between days, jumping on trade days) is drawn as sloped lines between days (l3 targets and orders ladder). Workaround: accepted the interpolated line, with the table beside it carrying the exact numbers. Wanted: `step: true` on a series.
- graph: edges drew no arrowheads unless `directed: true` was set on each edge and on the params, although README-B says directed is the default (l3 pipeline). Workaround: set it explicitly.

Raised later (backtest level 4):

- distribution: no way to slide N for the expected maximum of N trials. The derive set has `Phi` but no inverse normal, and no family draws the maximum of N normals, so backtest l15 shows N = 10 and N = 100 as two fixed `observed` lines on separate screens. Wanted: `Phiinv(p)` in derive (for the closed form) or a `max-normal` family with an `N` parameter.
- bars sim: an outcome cannot take the maximum of a list (`max(...xs)` is rejected as member access), so l15's "best of 48 noise Sharpes" writes `n()` out 48 times in `max(...)`. Wanted: `max` and `min` that accept a list, as `mean` and `sd` do, so `max(sample(N, n()))` works with N from a control.
- validator: `fit` is rejected as a highlight id on a scatter canvas ("not a plausible id"), although README-B lists `fit` among scatter ids. l15 dropped the highlight from that hint. Wanted: the validator to accept `fit` (and `line`, `r-p{i}`).
- pair: two `console` canvases side by side clip their lines at phone and laptop widths, with no hint that they scroll; l16's tap-sides hook uses `stack: true`. Wanted: consoles in a pair to stack automatically when a line would scroll, as two tables already do.

Raised later (backtest level 3):
- derive: no documented helper for a running maximum or any list reduction over a canvas's own data, which a drawdown explore needs (slide "today" along an equity series, draw the running peak, caption the drawdown). Workaround (backtest l10 explore): `let: { v: "params.series[0].values", pk: "Math.max.apply(null, v.slice(0, now.i + 1))" }`, which works only because derive runs as JavaScript under `with`. Wanted: documented `cummax`, `slice` and list `max`/`min` in the derive function set.
- series: with `x: "dates"` and `dateFormat: "%Y"` over five years the ticks fall at half-years, so each year is printed twice (2019, 2019, 2020, ...). Workaround: `dateFormat: "%b %Y"`. Wanted: year ticks on year boundaries when the format shows only the year.
- series: marker labels are not kept apart, so two markers at almost the same value a few months apart overlap (l10 hook: the November 2019 and April 2020 lows). Workaround: shortened labels ("Nov 19", "Apr 20"), one above and one below. Wanted: the same label stacking that number-line points have.
- explain.canvas: a Why? patch cannot show a different control value. A patch of `params` is flagged by the validator as a whole canvas, and derive re-runs in the Why? copy from the opening control value, so patched `values` or captions are overwritten (l9 leverage explore, l9 years-of-data distribution). Workaround: the Why? state only highlights. Wanted: an `explain.canvas` key that sets a control's value (for example `controls: { "k": 3 }`) before derive runs.
- table widget: cells return no per-cell keys, so `byKey` cannot name a misconception in one cell (l9: dividing the squared deviations by n instead of n − 1). Workaround: the generic feedback and the explanation name it. Wanted: a cell `wrong: [{ id, value, tol }]` as on numeric, surfaced as `byKey` keys.
- plane: no log x axis, so capacity (net Sharpe against book size from £10k to £100m) is a `table` of five book sizes rather than a curve (l11), as the brief anticipated. Wanted: `xScale: "log"` on plane.

Raised later (alpha level 1):

- bars validator: for object categories (`{ "id": "all", "label": "..." }`) the canvas ids are the category's own ids, but the validator also accepts `x`-prefixed ids such as `xall` in `hints[].highlight` and `explain.canvas.highlight`, which then highlight nothing (alpha l3 hook, l4 forward-return bars). Workaround: use the plain category ids. Wanted: an error when a bars highlight id is neither a category id nor `x{label}` of a plain category.
- code widget import scan (already raised by backtest level 1): `import numpy as np, pandas as pd`, the first line of the brief's shared `make_panel` generator, loads numpy only, so every alpha prelude-based test failed with key "error" and "No module named 'pandas'". Workaround: every alpha level 1 prelude puts `import numpy as np` and `import pandas as pd` on their own lines above the verbatim generator. Later alpha levels copying the generator need the same two lines until the scan is fixed.

Raised later (alpha level 3):

- bars with `groups`: the legend entries are spaced at a fixed width, so at 540 px "Momentum" runs into the next swatch (alpha l10 hook, l12 monthly-mix ladder). Workaround: accepted, still legible. Wanted: legend spacing measured from each label's width, or wrapping.
- boxes with `itemKind: "card"`: ten cards labelled like "S01 +0.42" in one container overlap each other at 540 px (alpha l11 quintile sort). Workaround: cards show only the signal value ("+0.42") and the hints name values instead of shares. Wanted: a container that grows rows to fit its cards, or a validator warning when they cannot fit.
- series `revealAt` control on an `x: "dates"` axis: the readout shows the index, not the date reached, so "Months shown 12" is the best label available (alpha l9 explore, l11 explore). Workaround: `indexFrom: 1` so the count reads naturally. Wanted: a readout format that shows the date at the reveal point.
- plane points: no grouping or legend for two sets of measured dots (alpha l12 trap: research-period and held-out ICs by weight). Workaround: two point colours explained in the caption. Wanted: point groups with a label, or a series-style polyline of points on a plane.

Raised later (alpha level 2):

- validator: a point-hint `highlight` of `fit` on a scatter is rejected as "not a plausible id" (README-B lists `fit` as a scatter id), so alpha l8's scatter and pair screens keep point hints with no highlight. Wanted: accept `fit`, `line` and `r-p{i}` on scatter, and `left:`/`right:` ids on a pair of scatters.
- validator: an `explain.canvas` patch that sets a bars canvas's `state.params` (the value a control drives, here `k`, which a derive reads to set every bar) is warned as "looks like a whole canvas". alpha l8's sector-demeaning Why? sheet needs `"params": { "k": 1 }` so the derive does not reset the bars to their raw heights; it renders correctly. Wanted: no warning for a `params` key without `kind`.
- series: a `window` driven by two controls (`window.from`, `window.to`) is the only way to let the learner move a look-back window, and the sliders read in array indices, not "days ago". alpha l5's explore labels them "(index)" and puts the days-ago reading in a derive caption. Wanted: draggable window ends on the series itself, or a control `display` expression for the readout.

Raised later (opt-dash level 1):

- code widget / playthrough: the Pyodide worker keeps the packages it has loaded from one screen to the next, so a screen whose own imports never load pandas still passes the playthrough if an earlier screen loaded it. opt-dash l3's `implied_q` screen imports only numpy and math, and the brief's generator line `import numpy as np, pandas as pd` loads numpy only, so a learner opening that screen cold would have hit "No module named 'pandas'". Workaround: every opt-dash prelude puts `import numpy as np` and `import pandas as pd` on their own lines above the verbatim generator. Wanted: the import scan to read comma-separated imports, and the playthrough to check each code screen in a fresh worker, or at least that its own imports cover the packages it uses.
- scatter: derive cannot count the points above a control-bound `line.b`, so l2's explore (spread over mid against moneyness, with a cut slider) could not caption "n quotes above the cut" live. Workaround: a static observation question ("where do the quotes above the cut sit?"). Wanted: a derive value such as `values.above` (points above the line), or `count(points, "y >= line.b")`.

Raised later (garch level 1):

- series canvas: a control cannot pick which precomputed series is shown, so l4's "slide the window length" explore holds four rolling-vol series (5, 20, 60, 250 days) and derives `hide` from an index control, as fin-ml l6 does. The slider's readout then shows 0 to 3, not the window; the caption carries the real value. Wanted: a control with labelled discrete stops (`values: [5, 20, 60, 250]`) or `valuesBy` on a series.
- bars canvas: `groups[].label` does not render KaTeX (`$r_t^2$` showed as raw text in the legend of l2's three correlograms). l2 uses Unicode labels ("squares r²"). Wanted: group legend labels rendered like other canvas labels.
- bars canvas: there is no reveal-after-grade for a bar, so l2's hook ("predict the lag-1 ACF of r²") draws the r² bar at 0 and the real 0.3596 appears only in the Why sheet. Wanted: `revealAfterGrade` (as grid has) or a state patch applied after a correct answer.
- walk canvas, `model: "markov"`: `P` is a fixed param and cannot be bound to a control, so l1's Calm/Storm regime picture cannot let the learner raise the storm persistence and watch storms lengthen; only the reseed control is offered. Wanted: controls on `P.i.j` (with the row renormalised) or a `stay` parameter per state.
- plane canvas, tap mode: at 390 px the points for windows 15, 20 and 30 days on l4's error-against-window chart sit about 20 px apart, so tapping the minimum is fiddly on a phone. Wanted: a larger hit radius, or tap targets that snap to the nearest point.
- number-line canvas: another vote for stacking coincident points (l3's trap puts "her reading" 3.01 on top of the normal's 3, hiding one dot).
- distribution canvas: shaded tails beyond ±5 sds (l3's transfer, probability 5.7 × 10⁻⁷) are invisible at the canvas's scale. Wanted: a log-density option or a zoom inset for tails.

Raised later (alpha level 4):

- canvas `derive` in the Why? sheet: derive runs after `explain.canvas`, so a patch to derived values (bar heights, point positions, a caption) is overwritten. The only way to show the explore's answer state (l13 H's momentum at +180%, l14 the cost where two lines cross, l15 five years of data) is to patch the free parameter, `"params": { "h": 1.8 }`, which the validator warns looks like a whole canvas (also raised by alpha level 2). Workaround: the explanations only highlight the relevant bars, lines or shading. Wanted: accept a `params` patch without `kind` silently, or let `explain.canvas` set control values (`controls: { "h": 1.8 }`).
- plane: a long `xLabel` drawn below the ticks collides with curves that cross below y = 0 when the x axis sits mid-plot (l14 explore: net Sharpe lines falling to −8). Workaround: accepted, still legible. Wanted: the x-axis title kept clear of curves, or `xLabelPos: "bottom"` at the frame's foot.
- bars as a capacity curve: no log x axis on plane, and no line-of-points chart, so net Sharpe against book size (£10m to £1bn) is a `bars` canvas with categories and an `hlines` threshold (l14 transfer). Workaround: tap the first bar below the line. Wanted: `xScale: "log"` on plane (already raised for fin-ml) or a points-joined series on numeric x.
- code widget `budget`: setting `budget` replaces the automatic 30 s allowance for heavy imports instead of adding to it (code.js: `spec.budget != null ? budget : heavy ? 30000 : 0`). For l16's full-panel `run_research` screen I set `budget: 20000`, and it passed because package loading has its own timer. Wanted: say this in AUTHORING section 3, or add the budget on top of the heavy-import allowance.

Raised later (arma level 2):

- pair canvas: a pair cannot hold a pair (validator error), so there is no 2 by 2 layout. l6's hook needed four correlograms to compare. Workaround: a stacked pair whose two bars panels each hold two series as coloured blocks of object categories (lags 1 to 12 twice). Wanted: a `grid` of up to four canvases, or nested pairs.
- scatter: `fitLabel: "auto"` draws the equation inside a dense cloud of 500 points, where it cannot be read, and derive cannot read the fitted slope. l5's lag explore and l7's fitting screen set `fitLabel: ""` and show the slope in a derive caption from a precomputed lookup (`SL[k]`). Wanted: `values.m` and `values.b` for the live fit, and a placement option for the fit label. Also another vote for accepting `fit` as a hint id.
- walk canvas: its paths come from the engine's own RNG, so they cannot reproduce the NumPy-seeded `make_walk(4, 500)` whose correlogram sits below it in l5's trap. Workaround: the prose says the top panel draws other walks. Wanted: a walk fed with given paths (`params.paths: [[...]]`) that the reseed control steps through.
- series canvas: another vote for a control that shifts a series or picks one of several. l5's lag-shift hook uses 11 precomputed shifted copies, and l7's φ hook 21 precomputed paths, both chosen through a derive `hide` chain (l7 maps φ to an index with `let: { j: "round(f * 20)" }`).
- plane canvas: a long `xLabel` on the lower plot of a stacked pair overlaps the pair's caption (l7 hook, "Days after a shock of 1"). Workaround: a shorter label, "Days $h$". Wanted: the pair caption placed below the full height of the lower plot.

Raised later (opt-dash level 2):

- payoff canvas `value`: the Black-Scholes curve takes `t`, `sigma` and `r` but no dividend yield, so l5 could not show how $q = 1\%$ lowers the call on a payoff chart. Workaround: a `plane` with the closed form written in `Phi` and a control on `q`. Wanted: `value.q`.
- series canvas, a control bound to `markers.<id>.i`: the readout shows the zero-based index, so l6's "steps $n$" slider reads one less than the step count and is labelled "Marker (steps − 1)". Wanted: an `offset` (or the x-axis value) for the readout, or a marker that rides `revealAt` with the axis label.
- tree canvas: draws non-recombining paths only, so a recombining CRR tree (l6 hook, 15 nodes) has to be laid out by hand as a `graph` with node coordinates, as trees l7 did. Wanted: `recombining: true` on `tree` with `n` levels and node labels per (step, ups).
- code widget, `compare: "isclose"` on lists: a `nan` entry never compares equal, so l6's `exercise_boundary` tests convert `nan` to `None` inside the test call (`[None if x != x else round(x, 6) for x in ...]`), which the learner sees in the results table. Wanted: an `isclose` that treats two NaNs as equal (as `math.isclose` does not, but `numpy.allclose(equal_nan=True)` can).

Raised later (opt-dash level 3):

- code widget, package loading: the import scan (`importsOf` in `js/engine/widgets/code.js`) reads `import numpy as np, pandas as pd` as numpy alone, because the `as np` breaks its comma list. The course generator's first line is exactly that, so a code screen whose prelude holds the generator and whose learner code does not import pandas failed with `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'pandas'` (l12's three code screens; earlier screens passed only because pandas was already loaded in the worker). Workaround: every prelude starts with its own `import pandas as pd` line before the verbatim generator. Wanted: parse `import a as x, b as y`, or load from the prelude's modules after a first `ModuleNotFoundError`.
- plane canvas, Why? patches: an explore's answer state lives in `state.params` (a slider's σ0 in l9, the expiry in l10, the fit's b and c in l10), and a `{ "params": ... }` patch is ignored as a whole canvas (also raised by alpha and backtest). Workaround: the explanations only highlight the tangent, curve or fit. Wanted: `explain.canvas.params` accepted as a state patch.
- series canvas: no step chart, so l11's forward vols (constant between expiries) are drawn month by month as a joined line, which slopes between steps. Workaround: monthly values and prose that says "the forward vol for each month". Wanted: `step: true` on a series (as matplotlib's `drawstyle="steps-post"`).
- plane canvas, `heat`: the heat map's single-colour opacity ramp is pale over a 0.17 to 0.42 range, so l12's hook leans on the contours to show where vol is high. Workaround: contours at 0.18 to 0.30 with labels, and a tap question with four labelled points. Wanted: a sequential palette option and a colour key for `heat`.

Raised later (arma level 3):
- playthrough tool: it runs each code widget's `solution` but never its `starter`, so "the starter fails its tests" is unchecked. The arma level 3 triage ran every starter and solution against the hidden tests in CPython with a small harness instead. Wanted: a `--starters` flag (or a default check) that runs the untouched starter and fails the screen when every hidden test passes.

Raised later (opt-dash level 4):

- payoff canvas, `tangent.label`: `{delta}` always prints two decimals. So l13's hook asks for "0.64" (tolerance 0.006), and the closed form's 0.6368 comes only in the explanation. Wanted: a format such as `{delta:.4f}` or `tangent.decimals`, and `{gamma}` for the curvature at the spot.
- plane canvas, function labels: on a curve that leaves the frame at its top-right corner, the label is clipped at the right edge (l14's hedged-P&L parabola, "hedged P&L" cut off). `labelAt` with `labelSide: "below"` put it across the curve. Workaround: the label was dropped (the y-axis title names the curve). Wanted: labels kept inside the frame, and placed off the curve when `labelAt` is given.
- validator, "a point hint usually highlights the canvas": this fires on code screens whose canvas is a static console of example calls, where nothing on the canvas relates to the hint. Workaround: those hints highlight the console's example lines. Wanted: no warning when the screen's widget is `code`.

Raised later (exams topic-0.1 and topic-0.2):

- validator, table widget keys: `validate.mjs` (around line 469) lists only `r{i}c{j}` as keys a table can return, so a cell's own `wrong[]` id in `feedback.byKey` is an error, although `table.js` returns that id and AUTHORING section 3 documents it. Workaround: the exam tables key their feedback on cell ids and carry no cell `wrong[]`. Wanted: the validator adds each cell's `wrong[].id` to the table's keys.
- number-line canvas with tap-regions: on a 390 px phone an 11-dot line (−5 to 5) draws small dots and 9 px labels, so the tap targets are tight (topic-0.2 item 7). Workaround: the shortest range that holds the trap values. Wanted: larger dots and hit areas on phones, or a taller line when its points are tappable.
- plane canvas, function labels: on topic-0.2 item 25, `labelSide: "below"` with `labelAt: 3.4` on $y = x + 2$ left the label above the line, touching the parabola. Workaround: accepted, still legible. Wanted: `labelSide` honoured when `labelAt` is given (also raised by opt-dash level 4).

Raised later (exams topic-0.3, topic-0.4 and topic-0.5):

- exam screen, timing: a topic exam has one clock for the whole paper, with no per-question time or pace readout. So the 0.5 mental arithmetic exam cannot time each sum as Zetamac does. Workaround: 20 in-head numeric items under the 45-minute clock, each prose saying "In your head, no paper." Wanted: an optional per-item `seconds` (or a pace line on the result page: seconds per answer).
- validator, topic exam size: `validate.mjs` requires 20 to 30 items for every topic exam, though topics 0.4 (40 h) and 0.5 (20 h) run only 45 minutes and the brief asked for 16 to 22. Workaround: 20 items each. Wanted: a range scaled by `minutes` (for example 16 to 30 for a 45-minute exam).
- tree canvas: a six-level folder tree scales its 540 px frame down on a 390 px phone, leaving about 8 px labels (topic-0.4 item 6). Workaround: the tree was cut to six levels with short folder names. Wanted: the frame narrowed to a phone column, as the graph canvas does (also raised by topic-1.8).
- exam start card: the lede reads "`{name}` covers every course in the topic", so it shows "Precalculus topic exam covers every course" with no article (`exam.js`, start card). Wanted: "The `{name}` covers ..." or an optional `description` on a topic exam.
- fenced code blocks: a traceback's last line, 63 characters, scrolls out of view in the 540 px column, hiding the key words `'str' and 'float'` (topic-0.4 item 16). Workaround: the line is repeated after the block as inline code, which wraps. Wanted: a `wrap` option on ` ```text ` blocks, as the console canvas has `wrap: true`.
- playthrough `--why` on an exam: the full-page result capture shows only the score header, the rest blank while the result list fades in, so the worked explanations cannot be reviewed from the screenshot. Wanted: wait for the result animation to finish before the capture.

Raised later (garch level 3):
- distribution canvas, `family: "t"`: no location or scale parameter, so the unit-variance Student-t that a GARCH VaR uses ($t_5 \times \sqrt{3/5}$) cannot be drawn. l10's $t_5$ screen draws the raw $t_5$ (variance 5/3) with a same-variance normal overlay (σ = 1.291) and the prose explains the rescaling. Wanted: `scale` (and `loc`) on the t family, or `standardise: true`.
- distribution canvas, graded drag: `drag: true` shows the shade handles, but no widget grades where the learner leaves a bound, so l10's hook ("drag the cut until the tail holds 1%") asks a choice question beside the drag. Wanted: a `drag-point` target on a distribution shade bound (`{ "bound": "to", "target": -2.136, "tol": 0.02 }`, or a target probability).
- plane canvas, x-axis title: a long `xLabel` that is moved below the ticks automatically ("Forecast annual vol (%)", l12) overlaps the derive caption under the plot on explore screens and the question line on problem screens. Workaround: a shorter label, "Forecast vol (%)". Separately, an `hlines` label near y = 0 (3.841 on a 0 to 40 axis, l11's Kupiec curves) collides with the title at the axis end; workaround `xLabelPos: "below"`. Wanted: the below-axis title counted in the drawing's height, and reference-line labels kept clear of the axis title.
- playthrough tool, sets: `--set garch/review-3 --set garch/homework-3` once reported "0 items" and then timed out on its reload retry, while another agent was writing files in the same course; the same sets passed alone, and in every later run. A fresh browser at the same moment loaded all 10 items. Wanted: on a retry, log the set's fetch status and the page's console, so a transient failure can be told from a broken file.

Raised later (garch level 2):
- plane canvas, x-axis title: another vote for the level 3 bullet above. In l6's hook the title "Yesterday's return $r_{t-1}$ (%)" was moved below the ticks and printed over the derive caption, and the short title that replaced it sat on a dashed `hlines` line at y = 0.5 (the variance floor). l8's sensitivity plane ("Persistence $p = \alpha + \beta$") overlapped its caption the same way. Workaround: titles of a few characters ("$r_{t-1}$ (%)", "$p = \alpha + \beta$") and no floor line in the hook; the prose names the floor instead.
- bars canvas, a reference line on a histogram: `state.cut` was the only way to mark the true value (0.98 persistence in l7's trap, 15.87% in l8's 20-path histogram), and it recolours every bin above the cut, which reads as a second category. Workaround: accepted the recolouring, with the cut labelled "true 0.98" and the prose saying what the line is. Wanted: `vlines: [{ id, x, label }]` on bars with a numeric axis, as plane and series have, drawn without recolouring.
- derive, a recursive filter: there is no function for $y_t = a + b\,x_{t-1} + c\,y_{t-1}$, so a slider cannot re-filter share B's real returns through GARCH or EWMA live. l7's explore shows the closed-form expected path after one shock instead, and l5 and l6 switch between precomputed paths. Wanted: `recur(x, a, b, c, y0)` (or `garch(r, omega, alpha, beta, s0)` and `ewma(x, lam, y0)`) returning the list, for `series.<id>.values`.
- series canvas, picking one of several precomputed paths: another vote. l6's explore switches three ARCH paths (α = 0, 0.3, 0.5) with an `options` control, a derive `hide` built from `ids.slice`, and a caption made of nested ternaries holding each path's sd and kurtosis. Wanted: `index: "k"` on series (as plane `exprs` and bars `dataSets` have) and a caption lookup by index.
- playthrough tool, sets: another vote for the level 3 bullet. `--set garch/review-2 --set garch/homework-2` failed with "0 items" and a `waitForFunction` timeout on both tries while another playthrough was running; the same command passed alone straight after, and in every later run.

Raised later (coint level 1):

- plane canvas with a drag-point widget: a target `{ "on": "f", "x": 1.47 }` on a function given as a `points` polyline does not work, because `evaluate` does not read polylines. Show solution put the point off the curve and the playthrough failed with key `P` (l3 screen 4, the ADF statistic of $y - \beta x$ over a grid of $\beta$). Workaround: a free point with a plain `[1.47, -5.13]` target and `tol: [0.05, 0.5]`, so the learner drags the dot to the valley floor rather than along the curve. Wanted: `evaluate` interpolating a `points` polyline, so `on` and `{ on, x }` targets work on data curves.
- series canvas, frames explore across series of different scales: the y axis comes from `params.y` or the union of every params series, and a frame cannot change it. l2's four pairs could not be stepped through in levels, since pair C is a price pair from £37 to £142 and pairs A, B and D are walks from 0. Workaround: the explore steps through each pair's gap ($y$ minus a multiple of $x$) on one fixed axis, beside a bars panel of daily correlations. Wanted: `state.y` (patchable, so a frame can set the range), or an axis that refits to the visible series.
- scatter canvas inside a frames explore: the brief wanted each pair's daily changes on a scatter beside its levels, switched by the same control. Scatter points are documented only as patchable by id, so a frame cannot swap the whole cloud. Workaround: the daily correlation is shown as a bar per pair, and B's cloud has its own screen. Wanted: `dataSets` with `index` on scatter (as bars has), or hide by `group`.
- validator, bars ids: `cut` is not accepted as a highlight id on a bars canvas, though the cut line is drawn as its own object. l4's explore hint wanted to ring the cut line. Workaround: the hint rings the bin `b32` at the answer instead. Wanted: `cut` accepted as a bars id.

Raised later (exam topic-1.9):

- validator, table cell `wrong[]`: `table.js` returns a cell's own wrong id as the key (pass 7), but `validate.mjs` accepts only `r{i}c{j}` as `byKey` keys on a table, so a named slip such as "mean instead of median" is an error. Workaround: items 11 and 14 key on `r{i}c{j}` and name the likely slip in that line.
- exam timing, `examMinutes`: 140 hours gives 60 minutes, but the topic 1.9 paper has 6 compiled code items and a longform among 25, and the second reader asked for 90. The C++ compiler's first download (10 to 40 s) and first compile also come out of the exam clock. Wanted: a `minutes` the exam file may raise, or code items weighted in the formula. Workaround: 60 minutes, as `examMinutes` gives.
- choice and multi options, fenced code: an option's code block clips lines past about 33 characters on a 390 px phone, with no fade or scroll hint, unlike the console canvas. Workaround: every option snippet rewrapped to 33 characters with short names (`Bids`, `v`).
- code widget, C++ source checks: there is no C++ twin of Python's `__learner_source__`, so a test cannot refuse `delete` or demand `std::unique_ptr`. Workaround: item 21's `Grid` has a private destructor with `friend struct std::default_delete<Grid>;`, so `delete g` and `make_shared` do not compile.
- playthrough tool, code variants: the playthrough checks only `solution`; starters and wrong versions needed a separate harness that mounts `ZQ.cppCode` in Chromium (`scratchpad/exam-1.9/codecheck.mjs`). Wanted: a `--variants <file>` mode that grades extra sources against a code widget and expects them to fail.
- playthrough `--why` on an exam: another vote for the topic-0.3 bullet. `topic-1.9-done-why.png` is blank below the score.

Raised later (contests level 1):
- code widget, Python script mode: `sys.stdin` is a `StringIO`, so `sys.stdin.buffer` does not exist and `sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()`, the standard Codeforces fast read, raises `AttributeError`. Needed: a stdin with a `.buffer` (a `TextIOWrapper` over `BytesIO`) so contests l3 can teach the exact idiom. Workaround: l3's program reads `sys.stdin.read()`, its prose says to write `.buffer` on Codeforces, and `read_tokens(data)` takes bytes as an argument.
- code widget, script-mode output: `print` writes to a `StringIO`, so a `print` loop over 10^5 lines costs about 3 times a `join` in the app, against about 17 times in CPython to a pipe; the learner cannot feel the slow-output trap. Workaround: contests l3 quotes the CPython timings and says the app's gap is smaller.
- validator, `table` widget: a text-answer cell's `wrong[]` entry (`{ "id": "n3-at-20", "text": "O(n^3)" }`) is returned as its key by `table.js`, but the validator rejects that id in `byKey` ("the widget cannot return the key"): the text-answer branch returns before collecting `wrong[]` ids. Workaround: contests l2 keys `byKey` on the cell ids (`r0c1`).
- plane, `yScale: "log"`: tick labels with many digits (100,000,000,000) are clipped at the left edge in the Why? modal and at 390 px. Needed: a scientific or power-of-ten tick format (`yFormat: "pow10"` printing 10⁸). Workaround: contests l2 cut the range to 10^11 and accepted the clipping.
- array canvas, `params.steps` stepper: an `explain.canvas` patch `{ "step": 3 }` applies step 3's pointers and highlight in the Why? copy but keeps the opening caption (step 0's). Workaround: contests l3 repeats step 3's caption in the patch.
- code widget, counting reads: a `list` subclass that counts `__getitem__` and `__iter__` cannot see `in`, `index`, `count` or `sort()`, and counts a slice as 1 read. So `xs[i+1:].count(k - x)` and `sum(xs[l-1:r])` pass a counting test and only a size test stops them. Needed: a harness helper (a counted sequence that is not a `list`) or a documented pattern. Workaround: contests homework 1 pairs every counting test with a 200,000-value size test.
- code widget, script-mode size tests: `stdin` and `expect` must be literal, so a $t = 10^4$ case puts about 120 KB per screen into the JSON (contests l3 is 341 KB with the Python and C++ screens). Needed: a generated stdin (a `stdinSetup` Python snippet) and an expected output taken from running `solution`. Workaround: small arrays ($n \le 3$, values from −9 to 9).

Raised later (coint level 2):

- scatter canvas, frames: `params.points` accepts `[x, y]` pairs, but a frame (or any state patch) that sends `points` as `[x, y]` pairs draws nothing and logs NaN errors (`<circle> attribute cx: Expected length, "NaN"`), because `state.points` must be `{ id, x, y }` objects. l5's explore steps through 250, 500 and 1,000 days of a pair. Workaround: every frame lists its points as objects with ids `p0` to `p999`, which roughly doubles the data in the file. Wanted: `[x, y]` pairs accepted in state patches as in params, or `dataSets` with `index` on scatter.
- scatter canvas, residual direction: `residuals: "line"` draws only vertical gaps, so the $x$ on $y$ regression's sideways gaps cannot be shown beside the $y$ on $x$ gaps. l5 needed both to show why the two hedge ratios differ. Workaround: the two fitted lines on one cloud, with the difference explained in prose. Wanted: `residuals: { line: "line", direction: "x" }` (horizontal) and `"perpendicular"` (for total least squares).
- derive, list arithmetic: there is no element-wise division or reciprocal, so a rolling z-score series, `(s - rolling(s, w, "mean")) / rolling(s, w, "sd")`, cannot follow a window control. l6's explore wanted the z itself to redraw. Workaround: the explore draws the spread with its rolling mean and ±2 sd bands, which carry the same crossings. Wanted: `vdiv(a, b)`.
- derive, counting over a list: there is no count or conditional sum over a list (`count(abs(z) > 2)`), so a caption cannot report how many days pass a threshold for the chosen window. Workaround: the three counts (90, 81, 65) are precomputed and picked by a ternary on the window in l6's caption. Wanted: `countIf(list, "abs(v) > 2")` or `vgt(a, k)` returning 0 and 1 with `sum`.

Raised later (coint level 3):

- series canvas, long date axis: a 2,520-day series carries 504 weekly sample points (every fifth business day) and `shade` `from` and `to` snap to those dates, so l12's formation shade ends on 2019-10-25 (day 1,255) rather than day 1,259. Wanted: a `dates` axis that accepts the full daily list, or `shade` by index on a dates axis.
- plane canvas, infinite value: a half-life of `math.inf` (the 250-day window at day 1,500, no reversion) cannot be plotted. Workaround: l11's point sits at 70 with the label "none" and a note in the prose. Wanted: an `off-scale` marker or an arrow at the top edge of the plot.
- number-line, derive caption: the canvas cannot run a backtest, so l9's draggable entry reads a precomputed table of nine Sharpe, change and P&L values by index, `sh[round((e.x - 1) * 4)]`. Wanted: nothing now; recorded because every later "drag a setting, read a result" screen needs the same table.
- tools/playthrough.mjs: no mode runs a starter against the hidden tests, so "the starter fails" is checked by a separate CPython harness. Wanted: a `--starters` flag that runs every starter and reports how many tests each passes.

Raised later (contests level 2):

- code widget, source-check cases: a hidden case such as `{ "call": "'pow(' not in __learner_source__", "expect": "True" }` (l5 `power_mod`) or `'math' not in __learner_source__` (l6 `gcd`) passes on the untouched starter, so the results show one green row before the learner writes anything. Needed: a case flag (for example `"after": "all"`) that runs and reports it only once the value cases pass, or counts it only as a condition on them. Workaround: none; the `how` line says what the row checks.
- table canvas, row count on a phone: l8's nine-row table of reachable positions has `maxHeight: 300`, which shows seven rows at 390 px and scrolls the last two, so the learner cannot compare every row at a glance. Needed: a denser row height (or a `compact` that applies to short tables). Workaround: the hint names the rows to compare.
- code widget, operation counting: the `_M` int subclass in l5 (`power_mod`) sees only the operators it overrides (`*`, `%`), not `**`, `pow` or `divmod`, so the count test guards a hand-written loop and a separate source check bans `pow(`. Needed: nothing now; recorded because any later counting test must list the operators it can see.

Raised later (contests level 3):

- code widget, source-check and `None` cases: a hidden case that only checks `'bin(' not in __learner_source__` (l11 `popcounts`) or expects `None` (l10 `greedy_counterexample`, the "no failing case" tests) passes on the untouched starter. Workaround: `popcounts` joins the source check to a value check, and the `None` cases go through a prelude helper `trials_run` that also counts the calls to `gen`, so a stub returns `(None, 0)`. Wanted: a case option such as `"not_starter": true`, or `--starters` in the playthrough (see coint level 3).
- code widget, read counting: the `_Counted` list subclass sees iteration (`len` reads at once), indexing (1 each) and a slice (1 read, then the copy is read free), so `updates[:]` hides the whole list. l12 `apply_ranges` and homework `spans` therefore test `0 < reads <= bound`, so a stub scores 0 and fails. A range-loop solution reads each update once and passes the counting case, so the size case (5 times $10^9$ additions) is the real guard. Wanted: a documented counting helper in `AUTHORING.md` with the reads each operation costs.
- code widget, no import ban: a numpy slice loop (`a[l:r + 1] += v`) runs l12's size case in about 0.8 s in CPython, because imports of numpy get 30 s extra and the pure-Python size cases assume no vectorised brute force. Wanted: an `imports: false` or `forbid: ["numpy"]` widget field, or a hidden source check that is not bypassed by `import numpy as n`.

Raised later (contests level 4):
- table widget, five input columns on a phone: l13's time log (Problem, Read, Code, Debug, Wrong, with an editable total row) scrolls sideways at 390 px and the Wrong column is off screen, with no scroll hint. Shortening the first header did not help. Needed: a narrower input cell, or a fade or arrow on a sideways-scrolling table.
- lesson player, no clock: a virtual contest cannot be timed in the app, so l13 asks for a 60-minute timer outside it and grades a worked log. Wanted: an optional count-up or countdown on a lesson screen, or a `table` of ungraded editable cells that keeps the learner's own minutes, mistakes-log lines and plan between sessions (l13 time log, l16 mistakes log).
- code widget, counting cases: a case that checks only a read count or a call count (`reads_for(...) <= 8000`) passes a do-nothing starter, because the starter reads nothing. Workaround: every counting case also asserts the answer (`f(x) == k and reads_for(f, x) <= bound`). Wanted: a `compare: "reads"` case that checks the value and the count together.
- Pyodide recursion: the brief's note that deep recursion crashes before `sys.setrecursionlimit` helps is wrong for this app: a 100,000-deep DFS and a million-frame recursion both pass with the limit raised (Node and a Chromium worker, Pyodide 0.27.7). The default limit of 1,000 raises `RecursionError`, so tests catch an unmodified recursive DFS only. Worth a line in `docs/briefs/` for later levels.

Raised later (topic 2.1 exam):

- Prose in a `longform` prompt: a Markdown blockquote (`> text`) prints the `>` literally. The prompt quotes a colleague's note, so I used italics in quotation style instead. A blockquote style in `Z.md` would suit every "review this note" item.
- `grid` with 12 columns in one row (a monthly time line for a `tap-regions` item) draws cells of about 25 px on a 390 px phone, and the column heads shrink to a few pixels. A `rows: 1` grid could wrap to two rows of six, or take a larger minimum cell.
- Cross-platform drift in scikit-learn: `GradientBoostingRegressor(max_depth=2)` gave a validation MSE of 14.9285 in CPython 3.13 (NumPy 2.5.3, scikit-learn 1.6.1) and 14.9527 in Pyodide 0.27.7 on one seeded case, while four other cases matched to 4 places. Authors should take every scikit-learn expected value from Pyodide, or compare several seeds across both before keeping a test. A line in `AUTHORING.md` section 3 would save the next author the surprise.
- Table widget: a cell's own `wrong[]` ids still cannot be `byKey` keys (another vote, as in the topic 1.9 exam). Items 7, 9, 21 and 29 use `r{i}c{j}` keys instead.
- The exam's `--why` result screenshot is a single 30,000 px tall page, so it cannot be reviewed as an image. A per-item crop or a paged result view would help authors check 30 explanations.

Raised later (topic 2.2 exam):

- `examMinutes` gives 45 minutes for topic 2.2 (60 hours). This 22-item paper has three statsmodels or SciPy code items (first load 20 to 40 s each time Pyodide starts) and a longform, so 45 minutes is tight. The file follows `examMinutes`; 60 would suit.
- Table widget: five columns with two or more input cells overflow a 390 px phone and scroll sideways with no hint (the BIC column of an AIC and BIC grid was off screen). Workaround: three columns, with the log-likelihood folded into the model label as KaTeX.
- `bars` with `groups` and no `yLabel` draws a default "Count" axis title, wrong for a correlogram. Setting `yLabel` fixes it, but a correlogram default of "ACF" would save every author the step.
- Code editor shows about 54 characters a line before it scrolls sideways, with no wrap. Starters and docstrings had to be rewrapped by hand; a soft-wrap option for docstrings would help.
- The exam `--why` result screenshot is one page about 24,000 px tall (22 items), unreadable as an image (another vote for the earlier bullet).
- `series` tap-regions on markers: markers that sit within a day or two of each other on a 60-day chart overlap on a phone, so I spaced the eight dots at least 2 days apart by choosing the window.

Raised later (exam 2.4):

- code widget, script mode and large stdin: a hidden `stdin` must be written inline, so e-code-pairs carries 32,000 values (about 95 KB of JSON) to make a quadratic count stop at the 5 s limit. Wanted: a `stdinGen` (a seeded generator line run before the case) or a `prelude` that can set the case's stdin.
- code widget, no import ban: the numpy slice loop passes e-code-quiet's size case in about 1.8 s, so test 8 also checks `'numpy' not in __learner_source__` inside the same case as the value. Wanted: a widget field `forbid: ["numpy"]` that refuses the import with a plain message.
- exam timing: topic 2.4 has 140 hours, so `examMinutes` gives 60 minutes for 26 items with 6 code items and a longform. A learner who writes each program from scratch will not finish; the file follows `examMinutes`. Same request as the 1.9 bullet: a `minutes` the exam file may raise, or weights for code items.
