- grid canvas, small cells on a phone: the 6 by 10 sieve grid (e-sieve-tap) draws cells about 22 px wide at 390 px. They stay tappable but the numbers are small. Wanted: a `cols` layout that wraps to two rows of five on a narrow screen, or a larger minimum cell.

Raised later (exam 2.3):
- Code tests that use exact float equality on a pandas frame (`DataFrame.equals` on a slice of a result) passed in CPython (pandas 3.0.5) and failed in Pyodide (pandas 2.2.3) for the same correct code, because row sums differ in the last bit. Authors should compare with `np.allclose`; a line in `AUTHORING.md` section 3 would help, and the validator could warn on `.equals(` in a test `call`.
- `order` grading returns only the first line out of place, so `byKey` cannot say which pair was swapped. With `same` groups the key is the first line whose group differs. A per-pair `byKey` form (`"s5>s3"`) would let an item name the exact misordering.

Raised later (limits level 5):

- `sketch` widget across vertical asymptotes: it grades one continuous curve over one `xRange`, so the full sketch of $x^2/(x^2-1)$ (three branches, two asymptotes) could not be set. Workaround: the learner sketches the middle branch on $[-0.8, 0.8]$ with the asymptotes drawn as `vlines`. Wanted: a `sketch` with `branches: [[a, b], [c, d]]` or a gap list, so each branch has its own control points.
- `plane` tap mode has no x-intervals or curve pieces: "tap where the graph bends up" could not be asked on the graph itself. Workaround: a `number-line` sign chart with intervals and point ids (`tap-regions` on the inflection point). Wanted: `tapIntervals` on `plane`, with named x-intervals that highlight their stretch of curve.
- `number-line` interval rows are 16 px apart and each label sits above its bar, so two stacked rows of labelled intervals overlap (the $f'$ and $f''$ sign chart). Workaround: `row: 1` and `row: 3`. Wanted: row spacing that grows to fit the labels, or `rowGap`.
- `plane` with `equal: true` and `maxHeight` draws a square figure about 215 to 300 px wide on desktop, so tick labels and point labels read small (the sliding ladder and the mirror-image picture). Workaround: `maxHeight` 300 to 380. Wanted: an `equal` plane that may be wider than tall, with the longer range centred, as a `height` plus `width` pair.
- `drag-point` with an `expr` target is graded on the expression's value, not on x, so a tolerance of 0.12 on `cos(2*pi*x/3)` means about 3 minutes of clock time on the stock path, and the author must translate the tolerance by hand. Wanted: `tolX` on an `expr` target, or a `values: [x1, x2]` list of accepted x positions for a point on a curve.

Raised later (multivar level 5):

- No 3D view: l18 and l19 are about solids and planes in space, and a 3D canvas would have shown the corner solid with its slice at height z, the cylinder and spherical cells, the plane x + 2y + 3z = 14 with its closest point, and the line where two planes meet. Workaround: the shadow on the xy-plane plus a slice slider (a `plane` with a control bound to z), a contour map of the function restricted to the plane (two free variables), and `table` canvases listing the equations.
- plane `equal: true` with `height`: when the ranges are close to square the frame narrows to `height` and is then scaled up to the column width, so the drawing grows to about 500 px tall and pushes the question below the fold. `canvas.maxHeight` fixes it, but every equal-axis plane in a lesson needs it. Wanted: `height` that caps the displayed size, or a line in `README-B.md` saying so.
- plane `heat` entry: the heat map of u^2 on a 1.4 range draws as visible small cells with banding rather than a smooth wash. A smoother (or coarser, honest) rendering would suit the Jacobian lesson, where the colour carries the integrand.
- matrix canvas and table widget: a "fill the linear system" task needed a separate `table` widget under a static `matrix` of the system, because a `table` cell cannot `bind` to a matrix cell. Wanted: `bind: "S-0-1"` on a table cell so the learner's entries appear in the matrix as they type.
- plane polygons from a map: the image of a draggable cell under x = (u + v)/2, y = (u - v)/2 needed eight derive lines for four hidden vertices. A polygon (or a pair side) property such as `map: { matrix, offset }` would draw a polygon's image directly and serve every change-of-variables, rotation and shear lesson.
- scatter `groups` legend sits in the top right corner and covers data points on a unit-square cloud (the l18 Monte Carlo screen). Wanted: a legend position, or placing it outside the plot area.

Raised later (eigen level 4):

- `matrix` canvas, `rowOp` label: with two matrices side by side (the elimination screen of l15), the curved arrow and its label ($R_2 \to R_2 - \tfrac13R_1$) are drawn on top of the right-hand matrix. Wanted: room for the label between matrices or above the arrow. Workaround: no `rowOp`; the KaTeX operator between the matrices and a caption carry the step.
- `matrix` canvas on a phone: three or four matrices on one row scale down until the entries are 6 to 8 px (the 390 px screenshots of l13, l14 and l17), and symbolic cells such as `$a$` and `$?$` are smaller still. Wanted: a minimum entry size with the matrices wrapping to a second row, as the `pair` canvas does with `minScale`. Workaround: at most two matrices on any canvas where the entries must be read, with the third in the caption.
- `choice` and `multi` keep the authored order unless `shuffle: true`, and most existing lessons list the correct option first, so its position gives the answer away. Wanted: shuffle by default, with `shuffle: false` for ordered options, or a validator warning when the correct option comes first in more than half of a lesson's choice widgets. Workaround: `shuffle: true` on every unordered option list in this level.
- `code` widget, forbidding a library call: the pseudoinverse and least-squares screens must not call `np.linalg.pinv` or `lstsq`. A source-check case alone passes the untouched starter, so the check is folded into a value case (`... == expected and 'lstsq' not in __learner_source__`). Wanted: the `forbid: [...]` field already asked for under exam 2.4, with a plain message naming the banned call.
- `scatter` `sim`: README-B documents `sim` and `points` separately, and I found no documented way to mark two chosen, tappable days (A and B) inside a seeded cloud. Workaround for the l16 hook: a list of explicit seeded points generated offline, with the two named points added at the end. The cloud is then fixed and has no `rho` slider. Wanted: named points allowed beside `sim`.
- Pyodide NumPy: `np.linalg.inv` and `np.linalg.solve` return `nan` on an exactly singular matrix (already noted in section 3). The pseudoinverse trap in l17 therefore says "an error or a matrix of nan" instead of naming one outcome.

Raised later (integrals level 4):

- No 3D view for solids of revolution (l17). A solid is drawn as its generating region with the mirrored curve, midpoint `riemann` rectangles read as half-discs, and a `pair` whose right side shows the slice face-on as an annulus (a `polygons` entry with `sector`). A 3D or oblique canvas with a spin-angle slider, one highlighted disc, washer or shell, and a shell that unrolls to a slab would have taught the disc and shell methods and the horn better.
- `plane` `area` on an integrand that blows up at an endpoint: the area value (`label: "auto"`, `values.<id>`) uses Simpson with 400 strips, so it is wrong near the blow-up (1/√x from 0.0001 to 1 reads 2.0176, exact 1.9800), and `from: 0` cannot be used. Workaround: shade from a small offset and print the closed form in a `derive` caption (l16). Wanted: an adaptive or substitution-aware integral, or an `improper: true` flag on the area.
- `plane` polygon `sector` with `theta1: 0` and `theta2: 360` (a full ring, used for washers and shells in l17) draws a radial seam line at 3 o'clock. Wanted: a full-annulus form with no seam, such as `ring: { cx, cy, r1, r2 }`.
- `expression` widget: the plain-text forms in `accept` (`ln(x)-0.5ln(x^2+1)`) are parsed as LaTeX by the Compute Engine (`ln` becomes l times n), so they never match there and serve only the text fallback; LaTeX forms in `accept` do match. Authors need a line in `AUTHORING.md` section 3 saying to list LaTeX forms for the maths engine and plain text for the fallback.

Raised later (random-vars level 5):

- `table` widget cell `wrong[]`: `table.js` returns a cell's `wrong[].id` as the feedback key (pass 7), but `validate.mjs` rejects those ids in `feedback.byKey` ("the widget cannot return the key"). Workaround: no cell-level `wrong`, and one `byKey` line per cell position (`r1c3`) naming that cell's likely slip. Wanted: the validator accepts cell `wrong[].id` keys.
- `bars` `sim`: no readout of the sample mean or variance of the simulated outcomes (`showMean` draws the weighted mean of the bar heights only). Workaround: a `series` canvas with `sim.running: "varp"` and a dashed `hlines` target (l17 and l18). Wanted: `values()` fields and a derive name for the sample mean, variance and sd of the run.
- `series` `sim` running variance: the first outcomes (n = 1 to a few dozen) lie far outside a tight `y` range, so the line draws a vertical spike at the left edge (l18, `y: [16000, 22000]`). Wanted: `sim.skip: n` to start the plotted line at the nth outcome.
- `plane` point labels: with `labelPos` left to its default, label avoidance moved "E[X²] = 34" beside the curve point instead of the chord point it names. Workaround: an explicit `labelPos` per point (`nw`, `se`). Wanted: a `labelFixed: true` that keeps a label at its stated side and lets other labels move.
- Validator ladder warning: l17's ladder moves from a `bars` chart to a `grid`, a `table` and another `bars` chart of the same numbers, which draws two "usually reuses the previous canvas kind" warnings; only table and chart pairs are exempt. Wanted: allow `grid`, `table` and `bars` of the same numbers.
- `bars` has no stacked mode, so the two pieces of the law of total variance (inside, between) and their total are three separate bars. Wanted: `stack: true` on `groups`, with a total label.
- `distribution` has no `negbin` or `multinomial` family, so the negative binomial pmf is a `discrete` family with probabilities computed offline, truncated at 40 trades to keep the validator's "probabilities do not add to 1" warning quiet. Wanted: `family: "negbin"` with `r` and `p` (in trials, with a `failures: true` option for the other convention).
- `order` widget on a 390 px phone: the line cards run about 20 px past the right edge of the screen, so the last word of a wrapped line and the end of a wide inline formula are clipped (l17 derive, l20 derive). Workaround: long formulas split into two `$...$` spans so they can wrap. Wanted: cards that fit the column, with the number and drag handle inside it.
- Wide `table` canvases and widgets on a phone: a four-column table (the l17 conditional-variance answer table, the die table) puts the editable column off screen behind a sideways scroll. Workaround: two or three columns, shorter headings and `compact: true`. Wanted: a stacked layout or a warning from the validator when a table with an editable cell has more than three columns.

Raised later (limits-thm level 4):

- `distribution` canvas has no Gamma or chi-square family (Beta and t exist). The wait for the third trade, the chi-square and the Gamma tail needed them. Workaround: a seeded `bars` histogram with a `curves` overlay for the Gamma (hook of l18), a `plane` expression with `lgamma` and a slider on the degrees of freedom for the chi-square, and a `poisson` distribution for the tail. Wanted: `family: "gamma"` (shape, rate) and `"chisquare"` (nu) with mean, sd, shading and the probability label.
- `distribution` canvas with `family: "beta"`, `a = b = 0.5`: the shaded probability label is wrong because the density is unbounded at both ends. A shade from 0 to 0.25 reads `P(0 <= X <= 0.25) = 1` against the true 1/3. Workaround: `showProb: false`. Wanted: integrate with the endpoint singularities handled, or use the regularised incomplete beta for the family.
- `plane` joint-density pictures: a polygon's wash covers the `heat` map beneath it, so a "tap the region" task on a joint density hides the density inside the region, and the heat map is faint at low density (range `[0, 1.4]` on a density with peak 2 was used to strengthen it). Wanted: an outline-only polygon (`fill: "none"` or an `opacity`), and a heat-map `opacity` or palette parameter.
- `bars` `showMean` uses the bin midpoints, so a simulation of the first of five default times reads `mean = 10.173` (true mean 10) and the best of 20 normals reads 1.861 (true 1.8675). Wanted: a `showMean: "exact"` that uses the simulated values themselves, since lessons quote the exact figure beside the label.
- No 3D surface or volume view: joint densities are volumes under a surface, and l14 had to use a top-down `heat` map, a rectangle of probability that a slider resizes, and vertical slice lines for marginals. Wanted: a `surface` canvas (or a `plane` mode) with a shaded column over a region, as already asked for under multivar level 5.

Raised later (markov level 4):

- No Poisson-process model on `walk` (or a counting-path canvas). l16's hook needed a seeded arrival path the learner can redraw and count per minute: a `model: "poisson"` with `rate`, `horizon`, a New path control, arrival ticks along the axis, the counting staircase and a count for each whole minute. Workaround: a `series` with `step: true` and `xValues` on a 0.05-minute grid from one fixed seed, with dashed `vlines` at whole minutes and a `reveal` slider. The steps then sit on grid points rather than at the exact arrival times, and the learner cannot redraw the path.
- `walk` with `model: "markov"` owns its seed: the engine draws the path, so an author cannot quote the visit shares or the number of flat steps of the path the learner sees (l15's hook could ask only a qualitative question). Wanted: `state.showShares: true` (a bar of visit shares for each state beside or under the path) and a way to give the seed whose stream is the author's own Python `random.Random(seed)`.
- Table widget feedback keys: AUTHORING section 3 says a cell's `wrong[]` entry names its own key for `byKey`, but `validate.mjs` accepts only the cell ids (`r2c1`), and rejects the wrong-answer id as "the widget cannot return the key". Workaround: one `byKey` line per cell, naming the likely slip for that cell. Wanted: the validator should accept `wrong[].id` (or the doc should drop the sentence).
- `distribution` always prints its probability pill (`P(X = 0) = 0.1353`), so a screen that asks for that number leaks it. `showProb: false` hides it, but nothing in README-B says this is the way to hold an answer back, and the Why? copy needs `showProb: true` patched back in `explain.canvas`. Wanted: a README-B line, or a `hideAnswer` flag that shows the label only after grading.
- `matrix` canvas on a 390 px screen: a 2 by 2 matrix of `\tfrac` entries with row and column labels is drawn at about 7 px (l13's flow matrix), unreadable without zooming. Wanted: a minimum entry size, or `scale: "fit"` that grows a small matrix to the column width. Workaround: none; the entries are repeated in the question text.
- `graph` on a phone: four states in a row with a self-loop on each and a pair of opposite edges between neighbours (l13's inventory chain) draws edge labels on top of each other at 390 px. Wanted: a `layout: "line"` that spaces nodes over the full frame width, or labels that move onto separate sides of each pair. Workaround: `height` 210 and `maxHeight` 170 so the chain stays visible; the labels still crowd.

Raised later (exam topic-1.3 gap extension):

- validator, topic exam size: `validate.mjs` caps a topic exam at 30 items, and topic-1.3 already had 27, so the twelve items the gaps plan asks for (limits L5, integrals L4, multivar L5) could not be added one by one. Workaround: three four-part `table` items, one per gap level. Wanted: a cap scaled by the topic's hours or courses (topic 1.3 spans four courses and 18 skills), or a multi-part item kind that the exam counts as one item but marks per part.
- exam scoring of a multi-part `table` item: `exam.js` scores each item as right or wrong, so one slip in four parts loses the whole item. Wanted: per-cell marks for a `table` item in exams (each editable cell a fraction of a mark), shown as "3 of 4 parts" on the result page.
- `tap-regions` beside a `table` widget: an item has one widget, so "tap the inflection points" cannot sit inside a multi-part item. Workaround: the curve sketch part asks for the inflection's x value in a table cell, with the curve drawn above. Wanted: a compound item whose parts carry their own widgets.

Raised later (exam topic-1.5 gap extension):

- validator, topic exam size: the 30-item cap left room for six items on topic-1.5 (24 already), against thirteen asks in the gaps plan for random-vars L5, limits-thm L4 and markov L4. Workaround: five multi-part `table` items (two to four cells each) plus one `choice`. Wanted: a cap scaled by the topic's courses or hours, or per-part marks so a packed item is not all or nothing (another vote with the topic-1.3 bullets).
- `table` widget cell `wrong[]` keys: still rejected by `validate.mjs` in `feedback.byKey` although `table.js` returns them. Workaround: one `byKey` line per cell (`r0c1`, `r1c1`) naming that cell's likely slips. Wanted: the validator accepts cell `wrong[].id` keys.

Raised later (exam topic-1.4 gap extension):

- validator, topic exam size: topic-1.4 had 26 items and the 30-item cap left four slots for five asks from the gaps plan (eigen L4). Workaround: the Cholesky factor and the conditional mean share one five-cell `table` item (`a-chol-conditional`), linked through $Z_1 = (x - \mu_X)/L_{11}$. Wanted: a cap scaled by the topic's courses or hours (another vote with topic-1.3 and topic-1.5).
- `table` cell `wrong[]` keys: still rejected in `feedback.byKey` by `validate.mjs` although `table.js` returns them. Workaround: `a-chol-conditional` keys on `r0c1` to `r4c1` and names each cell's likely slips in one line. Wanted: the validator accepts cell `wrong[].id` keys.
- `code` widget, banned calls: the `lstsq` and `pinv` ban is a substring test on `__learner_source__` inside a value case, so a comment that mentions `lstsq` fails a correct answer, and `getattr(np.linalg, 'lst' + 'sq')` slips past. Workaround: the `how` and the prose say the source must not name them. Wanted: a `forbid: ["lstsq", "pinv"]` widget field that checks names in the parsed AST and reports its own row (another vote with eigen level 4).
- `code` editor at 390 px: about 38 characters of a starter line are visible before it scrolls sideways, against the 54 that AUTHORING gives for the 540 px column. Workaround: the two new starters are wrapped to 36 characters. Wanted: soft wrap in the editor on a phone, or the phone width stated in AUTHORING.
- exam timing: `minutes` stays 60 while the exam now holds 30 items, three of them code and one longform. Workaround: none; the header was left alone because the task was append-only. Wanted: `examMinutes` (or the validator) to weigh code and longform items when it sets the allowance.

Raised later (momentum-rep level 1):

- Canvas labels, auto fractions: any `tex: 'auto'` label that is exactly `a/b` (`texOf` in `_base.js`) renders as a stacked fraction, so a number-line point labelled with Jegadeesh and Titman's J/K notation ("12/12", "3/12") draws as 12 over 12. Workaround: labels "12/12 cell" and "3/12 cell". Wanted: a `fractions: false` param (or auto fractions only when `format: "fraction"`), documented in README.md.
- `graph` on a phone: eight `rect` nodes in two columns at x 0.25 and 0.75 overlap at 390 px, because the frame narrows to 300 px and `padX` (node radius + 26) takes about 60 px a side, leaving the columns 90 px apart for 120 px labels. Workaround (l5): nodes at x 0 and 1 with short labels. Wanted: column positions that account for rect label widths, or a validator warning when two rect nodes at the same y would overlap at 300 px.
- Explore rule and `place`: a `place` widget in order or assign mode does not count as a canvas-bound widget, so the brief's "reading order with a place in order mode" cannot be an explore screen. Workaround: an ordered `tap-regions` on table rows (l1) and on graph nodes (l5). Wanted: either accept `place` order mode on explore screens or say in AUTHORING that ordered taps are the explore form of an ordering task.
- Why? copy of an ordered tap: patching `selected` into `explain.canvas` shows the rows in selection blue without the 1, 2, 3 order badges, so the explanation cannot show the order the learner was meant to tap. Workaround: a `highlight` on the first row only (l1) and a node rearrangement with arrows (l5). Wanted: `state.order: [ids]` (or `selected` plus `ordered: true`) drawing the badges in static copies.
- Playthrough and starters: `playthrough.mjs` checks only that each code solution passes; nothing checks that a starter fails its tests, which AUTHORING and CLAUDE.md require. Workaround: a CPython harness in the scratchpad that runs solution and starter against every hidden test (pure Python and NumPy cases only). Wanted: a `--starters` flag that applies the starter, runs Check and fails the screen if every test passes.

Raised later (brief algebra):

- `expression` (and `numeric`) modulo n: answers in $\mathbb{Z}_n$ or polynomials over $\mathbb{Z}_p$ are compared as real values, so `8x + 9` is marked wrong against `3x + 4` over $\mathbb{Z}_5$ with the generic line, and a residue of 16 against 4 modulo 12 needs its own `wrong` entry. Wanted: a `"modulus": n` option that compares values (or polynomial coefficients) modulo n, with a key such as `unreduced` for a congruent answer outside $\{0, \dots, n - 1\}$. Workaround in the algebra brief: coefficient `table` cells in $\{0, \dots, p - 1\}$ and `numeric` residues with `wrong` entries for the usual unreduced values.
- No permutation answer type: a permutation typed in cycle notation cannot be graded up to rotating a cycle, reordering disjoint cycles or writing fixed points, because `table` text cells compare exact strings (`(1 5 4)(2 3 8)` against `(2 3 8)(5 4 1)`). Wanted: `compare: "cycles"` on a table cell, or an `expression`-like `kind: "permutation"`, that parses cycle or two-line notation (with a stated composition order) and compares the maps. Workaround in the algebra brief: two-line notation with numeric bottom-row cells, a `choice` among cycle forms, or `place` in order mode with number chips.

Raised later (brief topology):

- `derive` and `plane`, iterated maps: `derive` has no loop, so a cobweb diagram (topology l13, `cos x` and `2/x`) or an orbit of a contraction (l12, $\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x} + \mathbf{b}$) is a `let` chain of fixed length (`x1 = cos(x0)`, `x2 = cos(x1)`, ...) writing `hidden: true` points joined by `segments`, about 16 points and 16 segments for eight steps. Wanted: an `iterate(f, x0, n)` derive function returning the list of iterates, or a plane layer `cobweb: { f, from, n }` that draws the staircase from a point or control, so a slider on $n$ or $x_0$ redraws any number of steps.

Raised later (brief pdes):

- `derive` matrices: there is no matrix builder or matrix arithmetic (a `tridiag(n, a, b, c)`, `eye(n)`, `madd`, `mscale`), and `vadd` works on flat lists only. A live explicit or Crank-Nicolson heat scheme with a slider on the step ratio must spell out its step matrix as an array literal with `lam` in every entry (`"let": { "A": "[[1-2*lam, lam, 0, ...], ...]" }`, then `vecmat(u0, matpow(A, n))` into `state.series`), which is workable for 9 interior points (pdes l8) but not for 30 or more. Wanted: `tridiag` and element-wise matrix `madd` / `mscale`, so `inv(madd(eye(n), mscale(T, -lam/2)))` gives a Crank-Nicolson step on any small grid.
- `walk` overlays cannot accumulate along a path: an overlay `expr` sees the current position `x` and step `n` only, so a Feynman-Kac estimate with a random discount, such as the Vasicek bond $E[e^{-\int r\,dt}]$ on an `model: "ou"` walk, cannot be shown as a running mean on the canvas (pdes l11 puts it on a `code` screen and a `series`). Wanted: a path-integral variable in overlay formulas (for example `I`, the running sum of `x * dt`, or a `cumulative: true` overlay), so `exp(-I)` can be an overlay and `showStopped` its running mean.

Raised later (brief measure-prob):
- `walk`: stopping rules beyond barriers and `stopAt`. `measure-prob` l13 needs a path to stop at a rule that is a stopping time but not a barrier (an `expr` in `n`, `S` and the running maximum or minimum, ending the path the first time it is true, such as "the first time S >= 1 after step 3") and, for the trap, at a time that is not a stopping time (the step of each path's maximum over the first N steps), with `showStopped` and `values.stopped` working as for barriers, so the running average of S at the look-ahead time visibly drifts off the start line (to E[max of 10 steps] = 1067/512 for a fair walk). Workaround in the brief: the exact distribution of the maximum on a `bars` canvas and an enumeration `code` screen.

Raised later (brief convex):

- `vectors`: no shaded cone. A normal cone or a cone of feasible directions is the set of nonnegative combinations of two arrows, but `span` shades only a line or the whole plane, `lines` draws bands through the origin, and `angles` draws only an arc. Wanted: `state.cone: ["a1", "a2"]` (or `show: ["cone"]`) shading the wedge between two arrows at a shared tail, highlightable and tappable by id, so a KKT screen can show $-\nabla f$ inside or outside the cone (`convex` l10). Workaround in the brief: an `angles` arc on `vectors`, or a `plane` with a polygon wedge and plane `vectors` when the wedge itself matters.

Raised later (brief numerics):
- `plane` `trajectory`: fixed-step RK4 is the only integrator and no step dots are drawn. numerics l11 and l12 (and odes l16) want `method: "euler" | "implicit-euler" | "trapezoidal" | "rk4"`, `showSteps: true` (a dot at each step) and an adaptive mode (`tol`, drawing accepted and rejected steps), so a slider on h works on a nonlinear equation and the l11 controller on y' = y^2 is drawn live. The briefs use exact curve formulas for linear equations and the content agent's own controller output as plane `points` with a `frames` control meanwhile.
- `derive`: no way to build a matrix from lists. A `tridiag(a, b, c)` (and `diag(v)`) function, used with the existing `vscale`, `vadd`, `matpow`, `solve` and `vecmat`, would let a derive march an explicit, implicit or Crank-Nicolson finite-difference scheme from a slider on N or dt and write the grid's V(S) to a series or plane (numerics l13, l14; pdes level 3). The briefs draw the schemes' output from the agent's NumPy run as plane `points` polylines switched by a `frames` control, and the live picture is the amplification-factor curves.

Raised later (brief stat-models):
- Data arrays in formulas: curve formulas (`plane` `expr`, `contour`, `heat`, `bars` `curves`) refuse `[`, and derive `sum(a, b, "term")` compiles its term the same way, so a formula cannot read `data[k]` from `params`. A kernel density over a histogram with a bandwidth slider (stat-models l12), and a log-likelihood surface over data (l2), must be written out one term per observation (fine to about 60 terms, generated by script). Wanted: list indexing inside `sum(k, 0, n, term)` in curve formulas and derive, or a `bars` `kde: { h, kernel }` overlay over `data` with `h` bindable.
- Metropolis walk: `walk` has no Metropolis model, so a live MCMC chain whose proposal step follows a slider cannot be drawn (stat-models l16). Wanted: `model: "metropolis"` with a target log density formula in `x` and params, `step` slider-bindable, per-path `starts` (dispersed chains), and `values.acceptRate` and `values.rhat`. Workaround: the learner's code prints chains to a `console`; l17 draws four exact Gibbs chains in a `series` from derive `ar1` and computes R-hat in derive.
- Posterior families and quantiles: `distribution` has `beta` but no `gamma`, `chi2` or `f` family, and no quantile functions for Beta or Gamma, so a credible interval's shaded ends cannot follow a slider (stat-models l14, l15). Wanted: those families, and `qbeta(p, a, b)`, `qgamma(p, shape, rate)` in curve formulas and derive. Workaround: Gamma, chi-square and F densities as `plane` formulas with `lgamma`, intervals shaded at fixed ends per screen.
- Sim order statistics: the `sim` outcome language has `mean`, `sd`, `sum` but no `median`, `quantile` or `sort`, so a bootstrap of a median or a trimmed mean cannot run in a `bars` `sim` (stat-models l13). Wanted: `median(list)`, `quantile(list, q)`, `sort(list)`. Workaround: the bootstrap of the mean runs live; the median's runs in a code screen.
- Sim draws: the `sim` outcome language has no `beta(a, b)`, `gamma(shape, rate)` or `poisson(lambda)` draws, so a posterior predictive or a predictive check cannot be simulated in a `bars` `sim` (stat-models l15; Poisson counts in l1 and l5). Workaround: exact pmfs on `bars` and `distribution`, and simulation in code screens.
- Robust fits: `scatter` `fit: true` is OLS only, so a robust line cannot follow a dragged outlier (stat-models l13). Wanted: `fit: "huber" | "tukey" | "theil-sen" | "lad"` drawn live beside OLS. Workaround: the Tukey bisquare line as a fixed `state.lines` entry, which is exact while the dragged point keeps weight 0 (residual beyond 4.685 scale units).

Raised later (proof level 1):
- Table widget `wrong[]` keys: a cell's `wrong: [{ "id": "exclusive", "text": "F" }]` is returned by the widget as the key `exclusive` (AUTHORING section 3, table), but the validator rejects `feedback.byKey.exclusive` as a key the widget cannot return (proof l2 truth tables, l2 transfer). Wanted: the validator adds each cell's `wrong[].id` to the table widget's keys. Workaround: `byKey` keyed by the zero-based cell id (`r0c3`), with no `wrong[]` entries.
- Venn labels: `params.labels` are drawn as plain text, so `"$P$: price above £100"` shows the dollar signs, and a label longer than about 15 characters ("Unrated counterparty", "Q: volume above 1m") runs past the universe frame on the right (proof l2, l3). Wanted: KaTeX in venn labels and labels kept inside the frame (wrap or shrink). Workaround: short plain labels ("P: price") with the full meaning in the `universe` caption.
- Venn subset picture: `venn` always draws overlapping circles, so "P ⇒ Q holds when circle P sits inside circle Q" cannot be drawn (proof l3). Wanted: `subset: ["A", "B"]` (A drawn inside B, region `A` empty or absent) or a toggle that animates A into B. Workaround: overlapping circles with region `A` labelled "empty" or shaded as the only breaking region.
- Boxes card backs: a `card` has no hidden back label, so the four-card task cannot turn the chosen cards over to show what is behind them after grading (proof l3, review-1). Wanted: an item `back: "1 signature"` shown when `face` patches it to down, or a `flip` patch that swaps label and back. Workaround: the explanation highlights the two cards and says what each could hide.
- No-canvas warning before the trap: the "problem screen without a canvas before the ladder ends" warning is keyed to the trap's position and fires for `manipulate` and `place` screens, whose widgets show their own maths (proof l5). Wanted: exempt `manipulate`, `place`, `order` and `code` widgets from the warning. Workaround: the trap screen moved ahead of the step-by-step negation screens.

Raised later (proof level 2):
- `place` assign, interchangeable chips: a token listed in the `accepts` of two slots gets only the last slot as its home (`home[id]` in `widgets/place.js`), so with `accepts: ["t-fx1", "t-fx2"]` on both the Ana and Cal slots a learner who drops one FX chip on each is marked wrong, and the test hook's answer moves both chips into Cal's slot and still passes (proof l7 reasons, l8 arrows). Wanted: a validator error when a token id appears in more than one slot's `accepts`, or per-slot grading by group counts. Workaround: list each chip in one slot only (`t-fx1` for Ana, `t-fx2` for Cal) with `byText: true`.
- `graph` arrow diagrams on a phone: `lanes` are horizontal only, and four pill nodes in one lane ("Rates", "FX", "Equities", "Credit") overlap at 390 px (proof l8, l9). Wanted: vertical lanes (`{ "x": 0.2, "label": "domain" }`) or pill nodes that keep a gap within a lane as the frame narrows. Workaround: domain in a left column and codomain in a right column by node `x`, no lanes, and a caption naming the sides.
- `venn` region values are plain text: `"∅"` draws as a small "ø", and KaTeX such as `$\varnothing$` is not typeset (proof l7, homework-2). Wanted: `$...$` in `values`. Workaround: the word "empty" in the two empty regions.
- `number-line` interval ends under points: a point at an interval's end hides the filled or hollow end dot, so $[0, 1)$ with tappable points at 0 and 1 showed two filled dots (proof l6). Workaround: the interval on `row: 2` above the axis and point labels `below`. Wanted: interval end dots drawn above points, or a documented offset.

Raised later (markov level 5):
- `walk` overlays `sq` and `ratio` measure $S$ from the start (position less `start`), so with `start: 3` the `sq` panel is $(S_n - 3)^2 - n$ and its stopped average starts at 0, not at $S_0^2 = 9$; `ratio` starts at 1, not $r^{S_0}$ (markov l19 duration and unfair-ruin screens, where optional stopping needs $E[S_T^2] - E[T] = i^2$ and $E[r^{S_T}] = r^i$). Wanted: an `absolute: true` option (or a documented `x`-based default for both). Workaround: `expr` overlays `x^2 - n` and `(q / p) ^ x`, which read the position itself.
- `walk` `state.showStopped: false` is a validator error ("not walk or an overlay id"), though `false` is the natural way to switch the panel off in a shared canvas helper (markov l20). Wanted: accept `false` as off. Workaround: leave the key out.
- Lesson summary ideas on a phone: an idea holding a long inline formula (such as $p\,f(s + 1, n + 1) + q\,f(s - 1, n + 1) = f(s, n)$) widens the whole ideas list past the 390 px column, so every idea is cut at the right edge; a long summary `formula.tex` is cut likewise (markov l18, l22). Wanted: the ideas list keeps the column width and long inline maths scrolls in its own box, as display maths does in prose. Workaround: shorter inline maths and formulas in the summary.
- `tree` `state.values` on inner nodes and the root: a value written "1/2" is typeset as a stacked fraction that overlaps the node's pill (markov l17 forecaster and Pólya urn). Wanted: values drawn clear of the pill, or plain text for values. Workaround: Unicode fractions (½, ¾, ⅓).
- `graph` with `markov: true`: an edge weight given as a string ("1/2") draws no label, and on a 390 px column the labels of a five-node chain with self-loops overlap the nodes (markov l17, l21). Wanted: string weights shown (as fractions) and edge labels kept off the nodes on narrow frames. Workaround: numeric weights, `show: "none"` with the chances in the caption, `loopAngle: 90`.

Raised later (proof level 3):
- `order` lines on a phone: a line holding one long inline formula (`$n^2 + n = (2k + 1)(2k + 2) = 2(2k + 1)(k + 1)$`) does not wrap at 390 px, so the card runs past the column and pushes the Left out cross off screen (proof l10 cases proof). Wanted: KaTeX line breaks at top-level `=` inside order lines, or the card scrolling its maths sideways as display maths does. Workaround: split the formula into two maths spans (`$... = (2k + 1)(2k + 2)$ $= 2(2k + 1)(k + 1)$`) so the line breaks between them.
- `number-line` tick labels at fine steps: with `step: 0.0001` on `[1.414, 1.4145]` the automatic labels round to three places and print "1.414" five times (proof l12 zoom on $\sqrt{2}$). Wanted: tick labels with as many decimals as the step needs. Workaround: `labels` as an explicit list of `{ x, text }` to four places.
- `plane` polyline labels: a `functions` entry given by `points` (with `label`) drew no label in the screenshots, so two series ($2^n$ and $n^2$) had no names (proof l13). Wanted: polyline entries labelled like `expr` curves. Workaround: a `label` on the last state point of each series.
- `venn` region values on a phone: a five-number list in region `AB` ("3, 5, 7, 11, 13") runs out of the lens at 390 px (proof l14 trap). Wanted: region values that wrap or shrink to fit their region. Workaround: a smaller universe (1 to 10), so each region holds at most four short numbers.

Raised later (analysis level 1):
- `number-line` tick labels at fine steps (seen again after proof level 3): `step: 0.0005` on `[1.4135, 1.415]` printed "1.413, 1.414, 1.415, 1.415" (analysis l1 zoom on $\sqrt2$, l3 ten halvings). Wanted: tick labels with the decimals the step needs. Workaround: explicit `labels: [{ x, text }]`.
- `number-line` tap targets that sit close together: candidates 0.99 and 1 on a line that must also show 2 are about 2 px apart, so `tap-regions` on points cannot tell them apart and their labels collide (analysis l2 upper-bound hook). Wanted: a zoom inset or callout rows for points closer than a finger width, or a minimum tap size that offsets labels. Workaround: the candidates as `tap: "rows"` of a `table`, stacked under the number line in a `pair` (`active: "right"`, `stack: true`).
- `pair` with a `number-line` beside a narrow `table`: side by side, the number line is drawn at about 40% of its size, with 6 px labels, even at 1280 px (analysis l2). Wanted: a pair that stacks when one side would fall below a readable scale, as matrices and bars already do. Workaround: `stack: true`.
- `number-line` point labels against interval labels and end dots: a labelled point at the left end of an interval (`s = 1.4` at the start of `(1.4, 1.425)`) draws its label over the interval's label and end circle (analysis l3 case one and two, l4 density gap and ticks). Wanted: point labels kept clear of interval rows. Workaround: `labelPos: "left"` or `"right"` on the point.
- `table` widget width on a phone: 5 columns with two input columns (or 6 digit columns) scroll sideways at 390 px, hiding the last inputs (analysis l3 bisection, l5 diagonal). Wanted: input cells that shrink to their placeholder, or a stacked layout for narrow screens. Workaround: 4 columns with short headers, and the diagonal table transposed to 5 rows of 3 columns.
- `derive` caption arithmetic at exact boundaries: `floor(1 / (1 - s.x))` at `s.x = 0.99` gives 99, not 100, because $1 - 0.99$ is $0.010000000000000009$ in floating point, so the caption named a term that is not above $s$ (analysis l2 explore). Wanted: a `frac`-exact or tolerance-aware `floor` in derive. Workaround: work in integer hundredths, `floor(100 / (100 - round(s.x * 100))) + 1`.

Raised later (linear-maps level 1):
- `vectors` `segments` are read from `state` only: `params.segments` is ignored without a warning, though README-B lists `segments` among the vector options beside `vectors` and `points` (linear-maps l1, the dashed line $x + y = 1$ did not draw). Wanted: read `params.segments` too, or a validator warning for it. Workaround: `state.segments`.
- `space` planes edge-on at the opening camera: a plane whose normal is near perpendicular to the view direction draws as a thin sliver, and the default camera (az -60, el 25) does this to $x_1 + 2x_2 + 3x_3 = 0$ and $x_1 + x_2 + x_3 = 0$; a slip the other way shows a span face-on, so a vector off it looks inside it (linear-maps l2 plane and transfer, l3 span(A, B)). Wanted: a validator warning when a plane's normal is within about 15 degrees of perpendicular to the camera direction, or `camera: { "oblique": "<plane id>" }`. Workaround: az and el chosen by hand from the direction $(\cos az \cos el, \sin az \cos el, \sin el)$, then checked in screenshots.
- `matrix` lists that change length: there is no way to take a column matrix out of a row, so the exchange (linear-maps l3) and the extension (l4) show removed vectors as `hidden` dashed boxes in their places. Wanted: `state.removed: [ids]` that collapses or strikes through a matrix, animated in `manipulate` patches. Workaround: `hidden` plus the prose "dashed boxes are not in the list".
- Several vector answers at once: no widget grades three vector answers on one screen (the three pieces of $\mathbf{0}$ in linear-maps l2's trap); a `table` cell takes a number or text, not a vector. Wanted: a table cell graded like a vector `expression` (with the `length` key), or an `expression` with several labelled fields. Workaround: two pieces given on the canvas and one `expression` for the third.
- Playthrough `--why` in set mode takes no screenshot of a `longform` item's model answer and rubric, so the rendering of a long model answer (display maths, bold headings) cannot be checked from the shots (linear-maps homework-1). Wanted: a `-why` shot after Mark my answer. Workaround: the model answer kept to short paragraphs and display maths that fits 540 px.

Raised later (analysis level 2):
- `table` canvas cells clip a long inline formula at 390 px instead of wrapping or scrolling: `$|a_nb_n - ab| \le |a_n|\,|b_n - b| + |b|\,|a_n - a|$` in a two-column proof table lost its right end, and so did "Choose $N_1$ with ... for $n \ge N_1$" (analysis l7 trap and multi screen). Wanted: inline KaTeX in table cells that wraps at operators, or a per-cell sideways scroll with a fade. Workaround: shorter cells with words between short formulas, and the left-hand side moved into the column header.
- `table` widget cell `wrong[]` ids are refused as `byKey` keys by the validator ("the widget cannot return the key"), although AUTHORING section 3 (`table`) says a matching cell `wrong` id becomes the key (analysis l10 trap: entering one step $\tfrac13$ instead of $H_4 - H_2$). Wanted: the validator to accept cell `wrong` ids, or the doc corrected. Workaround: `byKey` lines keyed by cell id (`r1c1`) that name the likely slip.
- `choice` and `multi` keep the authored option order unless `shuffle: true`, and authors habitually write the correct option first (analysis level 1 does throughout), so the answer is usually the top card. Wanted: shuffle by default for unordered options (with `shuffle: false` for ranges and steps), or a validator warning when a file's correct options are nearly all first. Workaround: `shuffle: true` on every unordered `choice` and `multi` in analysis level 2.
- `plane` sequence plots need one point object per term: the l6 explore lists 100 points, the l7 explore 170 (two sequences), each generated offline. Wanted: a points layer from a formula over whole numbers, such as `{ "id": "a", "seq": "(2*n + 1)/(n + 3)", "n": [1, 100] }`, with ids `a1`, `a2`, ... for hints and taps. Workaround: generated `state.points` lists.
- `plane` with `xScale: "log"` over nine decades prints every power of ten in full ("100,000 1,000,000 10,000,000") and the labels run together at 390 px (analysis l10 transfer, iterations up to $10^9$). Wanted: log tick labels thinned by width, or written as $10^k$. Workaround: the curve plotted against $\log_{10} n$ on a linear axis.
- `number-line` intervals in one `row` take default colours by list index, and two adjacent halves came out in the same colour, so the halves $[1, 1.25]$ and $[1.25, 1.5]$ read as one bar (analysis l9 bisection). Wanted: distinct default colours (or a visible gap) for intervals that share a row and an endpoint. Workaround: explicit `colour: 5` and `colour: 6` on the two halves of each row, and the explanation patch removes the rejected halves with `null`.
- `derive` `floor` at exact boundaries (seen again after level 1): the first good index $\lfloor 5/\varepsilon - 3 \rfloor + 1$ under an $\varepsilon$ slider can land one short when $5/\varepsilon$ is a whole number in floating point (analysis l6 explore, l7 explore). Workaround: a nudge, `floor(5 / eps - 3 + 0.000001) + 1`; for a frames slider (l10) the values are precomputed exactly.

Raised later (linear-maps level 2):
- `vectors` with `state.matrix`: `state.hide: ["unitsq", "b1", "b2"]` hides neither the shaded image of the unit square nor the basis arrows, so a change-of-basis picture about two eigenvectors (linear-maps l8, $\mathbf{A} = \begin{pmatrix}4&1\\2&3\end{pmatrix}$ with $\mathbf{x}_1, \mathbf{x}_2$) also carries $\mathbf{A}\mathbf{e}_1$, $\mathbf{A}\mathbf{e}_2$ and a parallelogram the screen is not about. Wanted: hide ids for the matrix overlay (`unitsq`, `b1`, `b2`, the image grid) or `showBasis: false`. Workaround: `basisLabels` naming the arrows $\mathbf{A}\mathbf{e}_1$, $\mathbf{A}\mathbf{e}_2$ so they at least read correctly.
- `expression` with a matrix answer: an `accept` entry written as the `pmatrix` LaTeX shows on the "Accepted: ... for example" line as raw source (`\begin{pmatrix}5&0\\0&2\end{pmatrix}`), and leaving `accept` out draws the validator warning "list the accepted plain-text forms" (linear-maps l8, homework-2). Wanted: accept examples typeset when they are LaTeX, or no warning for matrix answers. Workaround: plain-text accept forms `[[5, 0], [0, 2]]`; whether the fallback grader reads them as a matrix is untested.
- `space` planes drawn as lines (`span` with one vector, with or without `through`): `labelAt` is ignored, so the kernel line and its parallel coset $\mathbf{u} + \ker T$ both put their labels at the same end of the box, close together (linear-maps l6). Wanted: `labelAt` honoured for line spans, as for planes. Workaround: a camera (az 50, el 35) that separates the two lines so the labels do not overlap.

Raised later (analysis level 3):
- `tools/validate.mjs`, `table` widget: a cell's own `wrong: [{ id, value, tol }]` (engine pass 7, documented in AUTHORING section 3) is rejected as a `byKey` key ("the widget cannot return the key"), although `widgets/table.js` returns that id. Needed: "you typed the term $a_n$, not the total $S_n$" on the l11 hook. Workaround: keyed by the cell id (`r1c1`) with conditional wording ("If you typed the term ...").
- `place` widget (assign mode), 390 px: a token whose text is long inline KaTeX does not wrap. The chip runs past the right edge and widens its slot past the column (l13 "Four facts, four reasons", a chain $S_{2m} \le S_{2k} \le S_{2k+1} \le S_{2n+1}$ with words). Needed: chips that wrap or scale their maths to the column. Workaround: shortened the token and moved the full chain into the explanation.
- `plane` `riemann`: no `colour` per sum, so when a list shows left-end and right-end rectangles together, the sets look alike (l12 integral-test picture: rectangles under and over $y = \tfrac1{x^2}$). Needed: `colour` (or a hatch) per Riemann entry. Workaround: a two-frame control shows one set at a time; the explanation shows both and names them in the caption.
- `plane` reference lines: a `vlines` label at the top of the plot collides with an `hlines` label near the top edge (l12 hook: "n = 1" against the line y = 1, under the y-axis title). Needed: labels on vertical and horizontal reference lines kept clear of each other and of the axis title. Workaround: empty vline labels, with the value in the caption.
- `tools/playthrough.mjs --mobile`: screenshots cover only the 390 x 844 viewport, so a `place` token bank, the lower rows of a table widget or the end of a long option list never appear in a screenshot. Needed: `--full` (scroll the lesson column and capture each part, or the whole column). Workaround: a scratch Playwright script that answers, scrolls and captures the screen at 390 px.

Raised later (linear-maps level 3):
- `vectors` labels on axis-aligned arrows: an end or mid label of a vector along an axis lands on that axis's tick labels ("σ = 3" over the tick 3.0, $\mathbf{u}$ over 0.4, $\mathbf{v}$ over 0.5 in linear-maps l11 parallelogram and transfer, l12 transfer). Wanted: vector labels that keep clear of tick labels, as plane labels do, or a `ticks: false` option. Workaround: `labelPos: "mid"` with `labelSide` chosen to put the label on the side away from the ticks.
- `space` vector labels at a shared tip: two arrows that end at the same point (the chain $c\mathbf{v}$ then $\mathbf{w}$ ending at the head of $\mathbf{u}$, l12) put their end labels on top of each other. Wanted: end labels that move apart when they collide. Workaround: `labelPos: "mid"` on the chained arrow.
- `vectors` `angles` and `lines`, like `segments` (level 1 bullet), are read from `state` only; in `params` they are dropped without a warning (l11 transfer's right-angle mark, l14 the two spans). Wanted: read them from `params` too, or a validator warning for each. Workaround: `state.angles`, `state.lines`.

Raised later (analysis level 4):
- `expression` widget, two free symbols: the six seeded points (each symbol in 0.3 to 3) never fell where a*epsilon < 1, so for l15's edge case the answer `\min(\frac{a}{2}, \frac{a^2\varepsilon}{2})` graded equal to the cap alone, `\frac{a}{2}`, and to the slip `\min(\frac{a}{2}, a^2\varepsilon)` (checked in Chromium with ZQ.grade.exprEqual and the local Compute Engine). A switch "inside the tested range" is not enough when it depends on a product of symbols. Needed: author-set sample points or per-symbol ranges on an expression widget (for example `"vars": { "varepsilon": [0.01, 0.5] }`), or a validator warning when an answer's `\min`/`\max` takes one branch at every seeded point. Workaround: the screen asks only for the second bound `\frac{a^2\varepsilon}{2}`, with the cap given in the prose and the combined `\min` checked by a numeric quiz item (0.0005).
- `plane` hook with four pictures (l17 "Four functions with no top"): a `pair` holds only two canvases, so the four counterexamples to the extreme value theorem could not be seen side by side while the learner matches them. Needed: small multiples (a 2 by 2 grid of plane panels, or `pair` with up to four sides). Workaround: one plane with a four-frame "Picture" control (A to D) that hides all but one function, and a `place` widget with one slot per picture.
- `table` widget `bind` onto a `number-line` (l16 bisection hook): a bound cell adds its point as a plain unlabelled dot, so the five typed midpoints cannot read m1 to m5 on the line. Needed: a label for a point created by a bound cell (for example `"bindLabel": "$m_1$"`), or bound points that keep the label of a pre-declared hidden point. Workaround: the caption says each typed midpoint appears as a dot, and the explanation draws the nested intervals by row.

Raised later (interview plan):

- [Must] E3. Exam item banks: a paper's `items` are fixed, so a resat mock repeats itself. Wanted: `draw: [ { "from": "bank/<family>", "tags": [...], "difficulty": [3, 4], "n": 5 } ]` resolved per attempt with a stored seed, items seen recorded, fresh items preferred. Workaround: three fixed versions per mock.
- [Must] E8. Drill library: drills are Python only, untimed and unfiltered. Wanted: `language: "cpp"` drills through the C++ runner the `code` widget already uses, filters by pattern and difficulty on `#drill`, a stopwatch on each drill, and a "session" of two drills in 60 minutes with one countdown. Workaround: C++ problems as lesson `code` screens.
- [Should] E11. Follow-up parts: an item asks one question. Wanted: `parts: [...]` on an item, each revealed after the previous answer is checked, graded per part, so an interviewer's follow-ups can be modelled outside lessons (lessons have `ladder`). Workaround: a `table` widget with several cells, all visible at once.
- [Should] E13. Concurrency canvas: the C++ runtime has no threads. Wanted: an `interleave` canvas showing two or three threads' operations as steppable lanes over shared variables, with tap-to-mark the racing step and a `derive` of the final value. Workaround: `order` and `table` screens on listings.
- [Should] E14. Data files for code items: every Python run starts in an empty directory, so datasets must be inline in `prelude`. Wanted: `data: { "houses.csv": "content/data/houses.csv" }` on a `code` widget, copied into the working directory before each run (size cap stated). Workaround: seeded generators in `prelude`.
- [Could] E15. Calculator panel: `calculator: true` on a paper opens a basic calculator (the reported SIG test allows one). Workaround: the intro says to use one.
- [Could] E16. Application tracker on You: firms, roles, deadlines, stage and notes in the state document, exported with it. Workaround: a `recruiting` homework table.
- [Could] E17. Role filter: optional `roles` in `course.json` (trader, researcher, developer, strats) and a Study plan setting like `settings.quantOnly`, so Learn and Today can follow one track.
- [Could] E18. Readiness view: per role, bank coverage by family, the latest mock scores and pace against targets, on You.
- [Could] E19. Freshness: `asOf` and `sources` fields on lessons that state firm facts, shown under the lesson title, with a validator warning after 12 months.
- (E10 spoken-answer recording and E20 attention games dropped by user decision, 29 Oct.)

Raised later (interview course maps, 29 Oct):
- `app/js/screens/learn.js`: the hard-coded `MONTHS` still shows stage 3 as 6 months (curriculum now 550 h, 8 months) and stage 0 needs updating for 515 h; topic 0.7 has no `CLOSE` entry. Needed: derive months from `curriculum.json` hours, and a `CLOSE` line for 0.7.
- Topics with no exam (0.6 proof, 0.7 recruiting): the stage's "n of m topics passed" count can never reach m. Needed: count only topics that have an exam, or mark 0.7 as exam-free in `curriculum.json` (for example `exam: false`).

Raised later (ito level 1):
- `walk` has no time axis: the x axis is always the step index 0 to `steps`, so a scaled walk of $n$ steps of $\pm 1/\sqrt{n}$ over one unit of time cannot be drawn against $t \in [0, 1]$, and with a control on `steps` the ticks change meaning (at $n = 4$ they read 0.5, 1.0, ..., 4.0). Wanted: a `dt` or `T` param that maps steps to time (ticks, `stopAt` and `showSpread` in time units). Workaround: ito l1 and l2 x labels "Step (all the steps fill one unit of time)" and "Step (100 steps per unit of time)", with `stopAt` and `stopLabel: "t = 0.25"` to mark a time.
- `scatter` with `sim`: with axis labels only, the auto range (from `sx`, `sy`) stopped at ±2 for sd 1, and the points beyond it were drawn outside the plot frame, over the margins (ito l2 explore, 300 pairs). Wanted: an auto range that covers the drawn points, or points clipped to the frame. Workaround: explicit `xRange`/`yRange` (±4, ±6) with `xLabel`/`yLabel`.
- `distribution` `showMean` labels its marker "$\mu = 108.329$" with no way to rename it; in a GBM lesson $\mu$ is the drift (8%), so the label says the drift is £108 (ito l4). Wanted: `meanLabel` and `medianLabel` (and a `decimals` for the marker). Workaround: a `plane` with the lognormal density as an `expr` and `state.vlines` labelled "mean" and "median", the median line moved by `derive` from the σ control.
- `plane`: a long `xLabel` ("Price in a year $S_1$ (£)") is drawn below the canvas box and overlaps the caption under it and, in the Why? sheet, the caption text (ito l4, 540 px and 390 px). Wanted: the canvas height to make room for a long x label, or the label kept inside the box. Workaround: the short label "$S_1$ (£)".
- `bars` with `bins` offset to centre a lattice (min −3.125, width 0.25, so each bin holds one value of a scaled coin walk) labels the ticks at the bin edges, −3.125, −2.375, ... (ito l1 central limit screen). Wanted: tick control on bins (`xStep`, or ticks at bin centres). Workaround: none; the ticks were left as they are.
- KaTeX inside `$...$` reads a thousands comma as a separator with a space after it, so "$m = 4,096$" renders "4, 096" (ito l3 captions, generated with Python's `{:,}`). Wanted: a validator warning for `\d,\d{3}` inside maths. Workaround: `4{,}096`.
- `table` widget: a cell with the text answer "0" is refused ("editable cell needs a numeric answer, or a text one such as True") although "dt" in the same table is accepted (ito l3, the Itô multiplication table with cells dt and 0). Wanted: text answers that look numeric accepted as text, or the message to say a numeric-looking answer must be a number. Workaround: `"answer": 0` with `"tol": { "abs": 0 }` for the zero cells.


Raised later (puzzles level 1):
- `title` screen ignores `canvas`: AUTHORING section 2 says a title takes an "optional canvas in a static state", but `player.js` `titleStep` draws only the course art and the prose (puzzles l1, a table of the five interview books and the families each is rich in). Wanted: render the canvas on a title screen, or correct the doc and warn in the validator. Workaround: a Markdown list in the title prose, trimmed to 58 words.
- `graph` with `markov: true`, a chain of seven stage nodes in a row (coupon stages 0 to 6) at 390 px: nodes touch, and self-loop and edge labels overlap the nodes (puzzles l2). Wanted: a `layout: "chain"` that wraps or shrinks node radius to the column, with loop labels kept clear. Workaround: a hand zigzag (y alternating 0.28 and 0.72) with loops pointing up on the top row and down on the bottom row.
- `graph` back edges in pattern-waiting chains (HHH: H to start and HH to start) cross the forward edges, and their labels sit on the crossing; `bend` on the long edge has little visible effect (puzzles l3). Wanted: route a back edge as an arc below the chain (`side: "below"`) with its label on the arc. Workaround: hand positions in a trapezium and the caption naming the chance of each flip.
- `tree` with one level of six branches (a die's faces in the l1 reroll game): the $\tfrac16$ branch labels pile up near the root. Wanted: label staggering on a single wide fan, as already done for crowded trees of 7 or more leaves on later levels. Workaround: `state.show: "none"` and "Each face has chance 1/6" in the caption.
- `code` `compare: "fraction"`: `expect` is evaluated in the learner's namespace, so `"Fraction(4, 1)"` raises NameError when the learner wrote `import fractions` rather than `from fractions import Fraction` (puzzles l2, l3, l6, homework-1). Wanted: the harness to bind `Fraction` for `expect`. Workaround: `prelude: "from fractions import Fraction\n"` on every such widget.
- Neither `tools/validate.mjs` nor `tools/playthrough.mjs` runs the starter against the hidden tests, so a test the starter passes (l2's `coupon_sim(6, 500, 3) == coupon_sim(6, 500, 3)` held for `None == None`) goes unnoticed. Wanted: the playthrough (or the validator, through Pyodide in node) to fail a code widget whose untouched starter passes any test. Workaround: a scratch CPython harness that runs solution and starter on every case; the test became `... == ... > 0`.

Raised later (mental-pressure level 1):
- `table` widget drills (item 129, "a timed table set"): no countdown, live score or lock, so every drill (15 products at 8 s, 12 divisions at 8 s, 15 decimals at 6 s, homework's 20 at 6 s) says "time yourself with your own watch". Wanted: a `timed: { seconds, lock: true }` option on a table widget (or a set item) that shows the timer pill, records the elapsed time with the attempt, and lets the explanation name it. Workaround: target times in prose only.
- Warm-up data: `ZQ.state.warmup.record(score, seconds)` keeps only the score of a round, not each question's operation, seconds and wrong tries, and a lesson cannot read the warm-up's scores. l1's error analysis needs exactly that log. Wanted: the warm-up to save a per-question log (op, seconds, first try right) and a read-only `ZQ.state.warmup.log()` a lesson or set could show in a `table` or `bars` canvas, plus a per-operation summary on the warm-up's end screen. Workaround: a seeded made-up candidate's log (`random.Random(50)`, and seeds 51, 52, 54 in homework).
- Warm-up settings: the drill rule ("drill the slowest operation alone, then mix") has no clocked tool, because the warm-up runs only Zetamac's four-way mix and a lesson cannot launch it with other settings. Wanted: a link from a lesson to `#warmup?ops=div` (a one-operation round, saved apart from the main trend) and an 80-in-8 mode (480 s, four-option items, +1 and −1). Workaround: prose sends the learner to the Today warm-up; the drills are self-timed tables.
- `tools/validate.mjs`: a `byKey` line for a `table` cell's own `wrong[]` id is refused ("the widget cannot return the key"), although AUTHORING section 3 (table) says the matching wrong id is the key (l1 log screen, a "flipped" mean 10 ÷ 56.2). Wanted: the validator to add each editable cell's `wrong[].id` to the widget's keys. Workaround: the slip folded into the cell's own `r3c2` line.
- `derive` in the Why? copy re-runs from the canvas's control value, so an `explain.canvas` that moves derived points (`points.a.x`) is overwritten and the explanation shows the opening state (l4 explore, a log number-line driven by a `stops` control `k`). Wanted: AUTHORING to say a derived screen's explanation patches `params.<control>`, or derive to skip targets the patch sets. Workaround: `explain.canvas: { "params": { "k": 1000 } }`.

Raised later (interview briefs, 2 Nov):
- `tools/validate.mjs`: `longform` is refused in reviews (and lessons), but AUTHORING section 5 says reviews may hold it. Either allow it in reviews or correct AUTHORING; spoken-answer practice now sits in homework only.

Raised later (mm-games level 1):
- `tools/validate.mjs` refuses a `byKey` line for a `table` cell's own `wrong[]` id (as mental-pressure level 1 also found), although `widgets/table.js` returns it (mm-games l1 digit table: 200 pages for 100 to 300, a fencepost slip; l1 chessboard 9 for one 3 by 3 square). Wanted: the validator to add each editable cell's `wrong[].id` to the keys. Workaround: each slip named in the cell's positional `r{i}c{j}` line.
- `sliders` widget, `check` form: the result must be a finite number (`Number.isFinite`), so a band check written with `&&` (a boolean) never passes, while `wrong[].check` is read for truthiness. Wanted: AUTHORING and the sliders README to say so, or the grader to accept a boolean `true` as 1 (mm-games l3 quote: width at most 2 and centred within 0.25). Workaround: multiply comparisons, `(hi - lo <= 2) * (hi - lo > 0.25) * (abs((lo + hi) / 2 - 10.5) <= 0.25)` with `value: 1`.
- `number-line` intervals cannot be dragged by their ends; a "drag your 90% range" hook needs two extra drag points and a `derive` (`"i1.from": "min(lo.x, hi.x)"`) (mm-games l1 explore, l3 quote). Wanted: `drag: true` on an interval (handles on both ends, ids `<id>.from`, `<id>.to`) and a `drag-point`/`sliders` bind to them. Workaround: points plus derive, as described.
- `number-line` with `scale: "log"` prints tick labels as 10000, 100000 without thousands separators, unlike the rest of the app's numbers (mm-games l4, trades on a log axis). Wanted: grouped digits (10,000) or a `format` option on log ticks. Workaround: commas in the caption and point labels only.

Raised later (recruiting level 1):
- `tools/validate.mjs`, `table` widget: a cell's own `wrong: [{ "id", "value" }]` (documented in AUTHORING section 3) is not collected as a feedback key, so `byKey` on that id is an error ("the widget cannot return the key"). Needed: add each cell's `wrong[].id` to the keys. Workaround: `byKey` on the cell ids (`r3c2`) with a general line per cell, no named slip.
- `table` canvas, `table` widget and Markdown tables in longform model answers: four columns of ordinary text (a programme name, a deadline, a status) already scroll sideways at 390 px and hide the last column, not only five or more. Needed: tighter padding or wrapping so four short columns fit a phone. Workaround: three columns, with the firm folded into the programme name.
- Homework start screen grammar: with two longform items it reads "Item 1 and Item 2 is written. You mark it yourself". Needed: "are written" and "mark them" for more than one item.
- `number-line`: several draggable points parked at one x draw as one handle (only the top one can be grabbed until it moves), and intervals without `row` stack by index so a row's label overlaps the bar above it. Needed: fan out coincident handles; keep interval labels clear of other rows. Workaround: park points 0.5 apart; put every interval on `row: 1` and show only windows that do not overlap in time.
- Dated facts (E19): every day count is hand-written in prose from an as-of date, so moving the date from 29 October to 2 November 2026 meant rewriting each count by hand. Needed: `asOf` on a lesson plus a caption or prose function such as `{{days("2026-12-06")}}` counted from it, and a validator warning when `asOf` is over 12 months old. Workaround: counts written out for 2 November 2026 and checked twice.

Raised later (mental-pressure level 2):
- Generated items (`generator: "arith"`, item 452) make only whole-number add, subtract, multiply and divide items, and only in mock and stage exams. Level 2's drills are fraction comparisons (L or R), chained percentages, products within 5% and roots to one place, so none could be generated or reseeded per attempt. Wanted: generator kinds such as `fraction-compare`, `percent-chain`, `estimate` (graded by a relative tolerance) and `sqrt`, and generators allowed in a timed level review. Workaround: fixed, hand-checked table drills in each lesson and homework.
- `timed` (item 454) works only on a whole level review, so the four lesson drill tables (12 comparisons at 4 s, 12 percentages at 6 s, 12 estimates at 5 s, 10 roots at 8 s) and homework's 20 at 6 s are still self-timed. Wanted: a countdown on a single `table` widget or set item, with the elapsed time saved and named in the explanation (item 129 for lesson drills). Workaround: target pace in the prose; review-2 carries a 300 s guide clock (`lockAtZero: false`).
- `number-line` with a draggable-by-derive point and an interval on `row: 1` (l7 explore, the 5% band round 2,496): the point's label is drawn above the interval bar, away from its dot, and two points at nearly the same x (exact 2,496, estimate 2,500) draw as one dot. Wanted: point labels kept beside their own dot when intervals stack, and coincident points offset or ringed. Workaround: the band's own label dropped and named in the prose.
- `code` widget: no way to forbid an operation in the learner's code. Homework's `quick_product(a, b)` must not multiply the two full numbers. Wanted: a documented check (for example `forbid: { "binop": "*", "between": ["a", "b"] }`, read from the AST). Workaround: a prelude class `Full(int)` whose `__mul__` refuses another `Full`, with the harness passing the 20 seeded pairs as `Full` values.
- Follow-up questions (interview plan E11) and a per-item pace (E9) do not exist, so each lesson's interviewer follow-up is a separate `ladder` screen titled "The follow-up", and review and homework items cannot carry one. Wanted: `parts` on an item, revealed after the first part is checked, and an optional `pace` (seconds) shown on the item. Workaround: follow-ups as ladder screens; pace stated in prose.

Raised later (recruiting level 2):
- `longform` widget: the textarea placeholder is hard-coded to "Write each step and the reason for it." (`widgets/longform.js`), which misleads on a CV, a "why us" paragraph or notes on spoken answers (recruiting homework 2, all six longform items). Wanted: an optional `placeholder` on the widget. Workaround: none; the prompt says what to write.
- `boxes` canvas, `card` items: in a two-column container a label of about 14 or more characters overflows its card outline and overlaps the next card, at 1280 px and at 390 px (recruiting l9 hook, "Practise on real link", "Ask about scrap paper"). Wanted: cards sized to their label inside multi-column containers, or wrapping onto two lines. Workaround: labels of 15 characters or fewer ("Real link trial"), glossed in the prose.
- `table` and `console` canvases with `canvas.maxHeight`: a capped box cuts the last visible row with no cue that more rows follow (recruiting l6 hook, a 14-row CV in a 360 px box; l7 and l10 12-line transcripts). Wanted: a fade or shadow at the bottom edge while rows remain, as a console already shows at the right edge. Workaround: "Scroll the table to the end" in the prose.
- Homework start card: "About 48 minutes" for homework 2, whose items are a one-page CV, three "why us" paragraphs, a spoken project account and three spoken answers (hours of work). Wanted: an optional `minutes` on a homework file or on a `longform` item that the estimate uses. Workaround: none.
- Lesson timers (E6): the 30 s preparation and 2 minute answer of recorded video practice, and the 45 s "why us" and two-minute project account, are stated in prose with "use a phone timer" (recruiting l7, l8, l10, homework 2). Wanted: E6 countdowns on a lesson screen (prepare, then answer), never recording.

Raised later (puzzles level 2):
- `grid` with `population`: a group's `label` is only an accessible name, so the dots have no visible legend and a learner cannot tell "blue called blue" from "green called blue" (puzzles l8 audit, l9 witness and crash alert). Workaround: each caption names every colour and count. Wanted: an optional legend row under the dots (swatch, label, count per group).
- `grid` with `population`: `highlight` (in state or an `explain.canvas` patch) washes the chosen groups pale and hides their own colours, so a caption legend no longer matches the picture (l9 "Is it blue?"). Workaround: caption patches instead of highlights. Wanted: a ring or outline round highlighted groups that keeps their colour.
- `grid` with `population`: one dot per person, so the 10,000 people of the standard medical-test example (l9 worked 1) cannot be drawn in a phone column. Workaround: a `tree` with counts on the leaves. Wanted: `perDot: 10` (each dot stands for 10, the count label says so).
- `tools/validate.mjs`, `table` widget: a cell's own `wrong: [{ id, value }]` id is refused as a `byKey` key (seconding the recruiting level 1 bullet). In every puzzles level 2 drill table this made the per-row misconception lines generic ("Check the second answer ...", keyed `r1c1`) instead of naming the slip. Workaround: `byKey` on cell ids only.
- Drills: a lesson may hold 12 screens, so each lesson's three to six drill puzzles share one `table` screen with one Check, and a drill puzzle graded by `choice` (l12's labelled ant) cannot sit in it and moved to the quiz. Wanted: a `drill` screen (or E11 `parts`) holding several items, each graded with its own widget and `byKey`, with one pace line for the set.
- `boxes` item kind `bag`: a label such as "25 red" or "26 black" overflows the sack at default size and two sacks overlap each other's text, at 1280 px and 390 px (l7 "A full deck"). Workaround: `card` items, which widen to their label. Wanted: bags that widen to fit their label like cards.
- `walk` with `showHits` and both barriers: the hits label ("12 reached £10, 28 went broke (of 40)") is drawn on top of the upper barrier line and its "£10" label at 390 px (l10 hook). Wanted: the count placed clear of the barrier lines, for example above the plot or in the caption strip.

Raised later (game-theory brief, 2 Nov):
- `tree` canvas: no information sets. A simultaneous move, or a poker decision made without seeing the other card (game-theory l6 table-versus-tree screen, l13 three-card game), needs a dashed link or a shared outline round the nodes a player cannot tell apart. Wanted: `state.infoSets: [[paths]]` drawn as a dashed loop, with an optional label. Workaround in the brief: simultaneous moves stay in payoff tables, and l13 draws the deal as `boxes` cards with a caption saying who sees what.
- No widget for playing rounds against a scripted opponent (game-theory l4 matching pennies, l5 unequal pennies, l13 bluffing): the learner should pick a move each round against a seeded mixed or exploitable strategy and watch a running payoff, then be told the exploit. Wanted: a `play` widget (moves, a seeded opponent rule or mix, rounds, a running total in a ledger), related to the trading-game widget E12. Workaround: seeded `bars` simulations and `plane` expected-payoff lines with a control on the mix.
- Payoff tables (game-theory level 1, every lesson): a `grid` cell holds plain text only, so the two payoffs in a cell cannot be told apart by colour or type (Ana's in one colour, Ben's in another, or the best reply underlined), and best replies for two players show only as two shade sets with an inner ring. Wanted: a bimatrix option on `grid` or `table` (`cells` as pairs, each player's number in its own series colour, `state.best: { ana: [ids], ben: [ids] }` underlining that number). Workaround: `"3, 1"` text, the Ana-rows and Ben-columns convention stated on every screen, and two `shade` sets.
- `expression` widget: a closed form with a negative base raised to a symbolic power, such as Ana's share $\frac{1 - (-\delta)^n}{1 + \delta}$ after n rounds of alternating offers (game-theory l10), is undefined at most of the six seeded points from 0.3 to 3, and the free symbol n is not held to whole numbers. Wanted: a per-symbol domain on the widget (`symbols: { n: "integer 1..12", delta: [0.1, 0.95] }`). Workaround: `numeric` answers for stated n and δ, and `expression` only for $\frac{1}{1+\delta}$.

Raised later (recruiting level 3):
- `table` widget: an editable cell's input has a fixed minimum width, so a 4-column widget with inputs in three columns (Story, then three Yes or No columns) runs about 20 px past the column at 390 px even with one-word headers and a "?" placeholder (recruiting l11 coverage screens). Wanted: inputs sized to their expected answer (for example `size: "short"` on a cell, or a width taken from the placeholder). Workaround: headers cut to one word and one row label shortened; the table still scrolls sideways a little.
- `table` widget: every cell is centred, so a column of full sentences (l11 hook, Tom's eight sentences to label S, T, A or R; review-3 STAR labels) reads as ragged centred text. Wanted: `align` on the widget, as the `table` canvas has. Workaround: none.
- `longform` widget: no live word count, so the rubric line "under two minutes aloud: about 260 words at most" on six spoken stories (homework 3) must be counted by hand. Wanted: a word count under the textarea, optionally with the spoken time at a stated pace (`pace: 130` words a minute shows "174 words, about 1 min 20 s aloud"); never recording. Workaround: each model states its own word count and time.
- `number-line`: a point label on the axis is drawn over interval bars stacked on rows 1 and 2 (l13 day line: "Bad round" under the R1 and R2 bars; l15 months line: "2028 postings open" running into the interval's own label). Wanted: point labels placed clear of interval bars and their labels. Workaround: `labelPos: "below"` on the point, or the point label dropped and the interval left to name it.

Raised later (exam 0.7):
- `tools/playthrough.mjs --why --shots`: the results page screenshot of a 30-item exam stops painting after about item 21 (topic-0.7-done-why.png is 28,463 px tall, blank below 17,400 px), so the last items, here both longform model answers and a Markdown table in one, cannot be checked from the shots. Wanted: the results page captured in several viewport-height screenshots, or one per item. Workaround: none; the report says the models were not seen rendered.
- `table` canvas: no way to keep a short column from wrapping, so dates such as "8 Nov 2026" break over two lines at 390 px when a neighbouring column holds a phrase (topic-0.7 target-list and planning tables). Wanted: a per-column `nowrap` or width hint on `params.columns`. Workaround: shorter text in the other columns.

Raised later (topic 0.7 fix pass, 2 Nov):
- `boxes` canvas: at 390 px a card deck of 8 or 9 cards scales card text to about 7 to 8 px (recruiting l1 hook, l9 hook), and `cols: 1` on the deck makes it worse when the containers then fit on one row (l1). Wanted: a minimum card font, with the canvas growing taller or wrapping containers instead of shrinking text. Workaround: two-word labels; l9's deck is one column.
- Lesson player at 390 px: the left-edge rail handle overlaps the first letters of the question line (recruiting l14 screen 4, topic-0.7 exam items 22, 26 and 29). Wanted: inset the handle or a 16 px left gutter at narrow widths.
- `pair` canvas with `stack: true`: each side's table takes its own width, so a short-row table draws narrower and centred above a full-width one (recruiting l7 hook, Entry A against Entry B). Wanted: both sides at the same width. Workaround: Entry A's rows padded.

Raised later (fix pass 0.5):
- `numeric` widget: `parseNum` strips a leading £ but not a trailing unit, so "27p" in a field with `unit: "p"` is unparsed and Check stays disabled; a learner who types the unit gets no response (mental l3 screen 9 and quiz 3, review-1 item 5, homework-1 item 5). Wanted: drop a trailing unit that matches the widget's `unit` before parsing. Workaround: none; the label already says pence.
- `grid` canvas at 390 px: the 12 × 12 table and the 1 × 12 rows draw cells about 22 px wide with digits near 8 px, small for a tap task (mental l5 screens 0 to 5, l9 screens 1 to 3). Same need as the two grid bullets above (wrap a 12-cell row into two rows of six, or a minimum cell size with sideways scroll). Workaround: none.

Raised later (fix pass 1.8):
- `orderbook` and `table` canvases at 390 px: the order-book sizes (ds l18 screens 0 to 4) and the knapsack table cells (graphs-dp l14 screens 1 to 5) draw at about 8 px; `canvas.maxHeight` does not help. Wanted: a minimum label size on both canvases at narrow widths, wrapping or scrolling the table instead of shrinking it.

Raised later (puzzles level 3):
- `bars` and `series` `sim` outcomes: no `sin`, `cos`, `atan2` or `pi`, so Buffon's crossing rule, centre `u(0, 0.5)` below `(l/2) sin(θ)` with `θ = u(0, pi/2)`, cannot be written (puzzles l17 "Estimate π"). Workaround: the angle comes from a uniform point in the quarter disc (`let` a and b, `filter` on `a*a + b*b <= 1`, outcome `u(0, 0.5) <= 0.5 * b / sqrt(a*a + b*b)`). Wanted: the trigonometric functions and `pi` in outcome strings, as derive has them.
- `bars` with `sim.filter` used as a rejection sampler: the count reads "1,000 trials (n discarded)", which suggests lost data to a learner (l17). Workaround: the caption explains the discards. Wanted: `showDiscarded: false` on the sim.
- `series` `sim` outcomes: no loop, so "draw uniforms until the sum passes 1" (l15) needs nine chained `let` partial sums (`s2: "s1 + u()"`, ...) and an outcome adding nine comparisons, truncated at N = 10 (chance 1/9!). Wanted: a first-passage helper such as `untilSum(t, e)` (the number of draws of e until their sum passes t) or a bounded `repeatUntil`.
- `circle` canvas: one point only (`angle`), so several points on a circle and the semicircle that starts at the point after the largest gap (l14, five screens) were built on a `plane` with `equal: true`, a parametric circle, points moved by derive from three angle controls, and a `sector` polygon that a derive hides (`"hide": "gm >= 180 ? [] : [\"S\"]"`) when no semicircle holds the points. Wanted: `circle` with `state.points: [{ id, angle, drag }]` along the rim and `state.arcs: [{ from, to }]` shading an arc between angles.
