- Lesson `title` screen: a `canvas` on it is ignored (the player draws the course art), although AUTHORING section 2 says a title takes an optional canvas in a static state (l13 drew a stick broken in three; removed). Wanted: draw the canvas when given, or drop the line from AUTHORING.
- `pair` of two `plane` canvases side by side: each side scales down, so at 1280 px the right-hand plane's tick labels and axis titles draw at about 7 px (l17 needle floor beside its angle-distance rectangle). Workaround: `stack: true`, with the prose changed from "on the right" to "the lower chart". Wanted: a pair of planes that stacks automatically when a side would scale below about 80%, as matrix and bars pairs do.
- `plane` `contour` entries draw over the whole plot range, so on a unit square with a margin (`x: [0, 1.1]`, kept so axis titles clear the polygons) the `|x - y|` contours run outside the square (l18). Workaround: ranges cut back to `[0, 1]`, with the axis titles over the shading. Wanted: a `domain` or clip box on contour and heat entries.

Raised later (mental-pressure level 3):
- `timed` (item 454) works only on a whole level review, so the l9 block of 20 at 6 s, the four l12 mock blocks at 2 minutes each, the l10 drill (12 at 8 s), the l11 drill (10 at 10 s) and homework's two drill tables (2 and 3 minutes) are self-timed with the learner's own watch. Wanted (item 129): a countdown on one lesson screen or set item, with the elapsed time saved per block and readable by a later screen, so a mock's block times can be reviewed. Workaround: pace in the prose, "note your time on paper", review-3 with a 300 s guide clock.
- Net scoring (E1, `scoring` on exam papers) does not reach lessons: a `table` of 20 four-option letters is graded all-or-nothing and must be fixed before moving on, and no later screen can read which rows were wrong on the first Check or how long they took. l12 therefore reviews a seeded sample candidate (seed 81) instead of the learner's own mock. Wanted: a table option `scoring: { right, wrong, blank }` that records first-attempt marks per row, and a way for a later screen's canvas to show the learner's own marked rows. Workaround: "count the amber rows on your first Check"; the sample candidate's log in a table and a bars chart.
- Generated items (E4, `generator: "arith"`) exist only in exam papers and make only whole-number add, subtract, multiply and divide items, so the 80-in-8 mix (decimals, fractions, percentages, next term of a sequence) cannot be generated or reseeded per attempt. Wanted: generator kinds `dec`, `frac`, `pct` and `seq` with near-miss options, usable in exam papers and in a lesson table. Workaround: a fixed seed-80 paper from `mockgen.py`, whose script is in `docs/reports/mental-pressure-3.md` for `mock-oa-arith`.
- `table` widget: no four-option cell, so each four-option question is a text cell listing "A 189 · B 1,071 ..." beside a text cell for the letter; at 390 px the options wrap onto four lines and the row grows tall (l9 block, l12 blocks, homework drill). Wanted: a choice cell (four small chips, one per option, graded by option id) or a "paper" screen of many single-choice items with one Check. Workaround: no-break spaces between each letter and its value, three columns.
- `bars` with a `frames` control: frames patch state only, so `decimals` (a param) cannot change between frames, and the l12 "Wrong answers" frame shows counts as 5.0 and 1.0 beside a seconds frame that needs one place. Wanted: `decimals` (and `yLabel`) readable from state or settable per frame. Workaround: accepted the trailing ".0".
- `derive` functions: no zero-padding, so the l11 clock caption needs `(arr.x % 60 < 10 ? "0" : "") + fixed(arr.x % 60, 0)` to print 17:05 rather than 17:5. Wanted: `pad(v, width)` or `clock(minutes)` ("17:25") in the derive function set. Workaround: the ternary, as written.

Raised later (fix pass 2.1):
- `graph` canvas: on a 540 px desktop column a graph of wide rect labels draws its usable width as frame minus twice the widest half-label, so a three-column layout collides (fin-ml l12 s1, validation l10 s3 before this pass). Workaround: one column, or two columns with two-line labels. Wanted: fill the column width (the `fitWidth` path) and shrink or wrap rect labels when neighbours would overlap.
- `grid` canvas with `cols: 15` and one row (validation l5 s0, s1, s7, s8): at 390 px each day cell is about 20 px wide, under a comfortable tap target. Re-laying out as 2 rows of 8 would re-key every cell id, answer, patch and hint. Wanted: a `wrap` option that draws a long row over two lines at narrow widths while keeping the `r1c{j}` ids.
- `pair` with `stack: true` holding a wide, short `plane` (fin-ml l4 s0, s1, `height: 180`): at 390 px the plane scales down and its tick labels and segment labels draw at about 7 px. Wanted: planes in a stacked pair keep a 12 px minimum text size, as the graph frame does.

Raised later (puzzles level 4):
- `table` widget, a cell's own `wrong` entries (AUTHORING section 3: "the key is that id"): the validator refuses a `byKey` line for such an id ("the widget cannot return the key"), so the named slip cannot be spoken to (puzzles l24, the coupon 25 for 137/12, the double-six 36 for 42, the Bayes 0.95 for 0.1624). Workaround: the slip is named in the cell's `r{i}c1` line. Wanted: the validator collects cell-level `wrong` ids as producible keys, or AUTHORING drops the sentence.
- `graph` canvas with `markov: true` at 390 px: a pattern automaton of four node columns (start, H, HH, the finished pattern) overlapped while the end nodes carried labels such as "HHT wins", because one long label sets every node's radius (l20). Workaround: the end nodes are labelled with the bare pattern and the caption says the right-hand nodes end the game. Wanted: a per-node radius (or `shape: "rect"` honoured in markov graphs) and wrapping on narrow columns, so one long label does not enlarge every node.
- `walk` overlay `bankroll` with `bet: "double"`: the stake is capped at the current bankroll and doubling never resets after a lost cycle, so the panel cannot show the fixed-length cycles the puzzle prices (a £63 doubler allows exactly six bets, loses £63, then starts again from £1) (l23). Workaround: the explore screen asks only for the shape (small steady gains, a rare sharp drop) and every number comes from exact work in a `table` canvas. Wanted: `maxDoublings: n` on the double rule (lose the cycle after n losses, then restart from `stake`).
- Interview practice deck reads `tags` only on quiz, review and homework items, one family per item: lesson drill screens are `table`s with several puzzles, and l24's two short-calculation tables mix seven and six families, so they carry `["mixed-set", "short"]` and their rows never reach the deck. Wanted: per-row `tags` on table widget rows (and the deck dealing a row as a one-cell numeric item), or a deck that reads lesson drill screens. Workaround: quiz items carry each lesson's family so they join the deck.
- Timed practice inside a lesson: l24 is "a mixed set against a clock" but `timed` exists only on level reviews (item 454), so the set's pace (1 minute short, 4 minutes multi-stage, 3 minutes strategic) is stated in prose and in `pace` per screen, kept by the learner. review-4 carries `timed: { "seconds": 1260 }` as a guide clock. Wanted: `timed` on a lesson screen or a lesson-level block (item 129).

Raised later (fix pass 1.9):
- C++ runtime: a write one past a `new[]` block is not trapped, so a reading loop with `i <= n` passes every test of cpp l8 screen 6 (the second reader's mutation check). There is no AddressSanitizer in the browser; the `how` now says so. Wanted: guard bytes around each `new[]` block in `leakCheck` mode, checked at `delete[]` and at exit, reported like the leak message ("heap write past the end of a new[] block").
- `docs/reviews/voice-scan.py`: it scans `canvas.derive.*` strings as prose, so a derive expression with a long list (cpp-pricing l1 screen 1, `derive.cells`) counted as a 40-word sentence. Workaround: the list's `, ` separators were closed up. Wanted: add `derive` to the script's skipped keys, or skip paths under `.derive.`.
- playthrough tool, code variants: another vote for the topic-1.9 exam bullet (`--variants`). Fix pass 1.9 checked starters and planted bugs by copying `app/` to the scratchpad, swapping each variant into `solution`, and playing the copy (`fix-1.9/mut.py`).
- Content note (cpp-pricing class names), closed: every payoff class in cpp-pricing is `Call`, `Put`, `DigitalCall`, `CappedCall`, `Straddle` or `Basket` deriving from `Payoff`; `CallPayoff` and `PutPayoff` appear nowhere, so no rename is needed. The inheritance question stays open as a new cpp level 3 lesson (review-1.9, Fix pass, notes for new levels), which needs `course.json`.

Raised later (mm-games level 2):
- `table` widget, a cell's own `wrong[]` id: the validator still refuses a `byKey` line for it (raised before by mm-games 1, puzzles 4, mental-pressure 1). It bent l8 screens 3 and 5, where a one-sided count and an unhalved informed loss could not be named. Workaround: both slips are folded into `feedback.generic`. Wanted: the validator adds editable cells' `wrong[].id` to the producible keys.
- `table` widget: no computed cell that follows an input, such as a "new ask = new bid + 2" column. So the l7 four-trade table asks for the position and the bid only, and states the ask rule in prose. Wanted: a cell `derive: "r1c2 + 2"` drawn live from another input, or a `bind` that writes to a sibling cell.
- `tree` canvas with six first-level branches (the faces of a die): at 390 px the probability labels collide along the fanned branches (l8 hook). Workaround: `state.show: "none"`, with "each face 1/6" in the caption. Wanted: on a fan of six or more, one shared probability label, or labels placed at the branch ends.
- `pair` with `stack: true` holding a `boxes` canvas beside a `table`: the cards draw at about 8 px text even with `size: 2`, because the boxes side scales to its own short frame (l9 "Then a 2", cards beside the ledger). Workaround: the prose names the revealed cards. Wanted: a stacked pair that keeps a boxes side at its own size, as matrices keep their width (`fit: true`).

Raised later (brainteasers level 1):
- `table` widget, a cell's own `wrong[]` id: one more vote. The validator refuses a `byKey` line for it, which bent brainteasers l1 s4 (the sum 21 for $a + b + ab$) and the l2 trap (the "yes" that trusts the area). Workaround: `byKey` on the zero-based cell id (`r4c1` for the yes/no cell). Wanted: the validator adds editable cells' `wrong[].id` to the producible keys.
- `manipulate` widget: `target` is one state. Two lizard states have no blue (4, 2, 0 and 1, 5, 0), and two jug states hold 2 litres in the small jug (2, 8 and 2, 0), so "reach any state with no blue" cannot be graded. Workaround: l1 asks for 4, 2, 0 exactly; l5 merges both jug states into one `goal` node, which loses that node's own canvas tick. Wanted: `target` as a list of state ids (or a predicate on state tags).
- `manipulate` canvas patches are per state and merge along the path, so a route cannot be drawn edge by edge on a `grid` lattice (a `paths` segment needs the previous state). Workaround: each state ticks its own lattice cell (`mark`) and highlights itself; the full route appears only in `explain.canvas`. Wanted: an op-level `canvas` patch, or an option that draws a `paths` route through the cells named by the visited states.
- `pace` on items and screens: an engine pass is adding it, and the validator currently ignores unknown fields, so brainteasers level 1 carries `"pace": 180` on every tagged drill screen, quiz, review and homework item (0 errors). Wanted: when `pace` is validated, check these files; the drill prose also states the pace.
- Lesson schema, 8 to 12 screens: the brainteasers shape (hook, three worked teasers, derive, trap, edge, generalise, three to five drills and a two-part follow-up) needs 13 to 15 screens. Workaround: l1 moved its lamp-count multi, one drill and the transfer's invariant choice into the quiz; l5 moved one drill into the quiz. Wanted: a drill block (several tagged teasers on one screen, each graded and dealt to the bank separately), or a cap of 14 for courses on the speed track.
- `docs/reviews/voice-scan.py`: with a relative `VOICE_BASE` and no trailing slash (`app/content/courses/brainteasers`, the form in the orchestrator prompt) it scans 0 sentences and reports a clean pass, because it globs `BASE + '*.json'`. Workaround: an absolute path with a trailing slash. Wanted: join with `os.path.join` and resolve relative paths, and fail when no file matches.
- `number-line` intervals on rows 1 and 2: the default labels (above each bar) of neighbouring rows overlap, 16 px apart (l5 rope timelines). Workaround: `labelPos: "below"` on row 1 and `"above"` on row 2, with rope B's two stages on one row. Wanted: interval labels that stack clear of the next row, as point labels do.
- `derive` function set: no closest-pair helper, so the l3 caption "closest pair of ten points" is a generated `min(...)` over 45 `hypot` terms. Wanted: `mindist(points)` (or `pairs(ids)`) for point sets on a plane.

Raised later (mental-pressure level 4):
- Lessons cannot net-score the learner's own answers or pass them to a later screen (another vote after mental-pressure 3): the l17 battery's three section tables are graded all-or-nothing, so its net-score sheet and S/M/T error sort use a seeded sample candidate (`random.Random(18)`) instead of the learner's sitting. Wanted: a lesson screen that reads the first-Check marks (and seconds) of named earlier table rows, with `scoring` per screen.
- Timed sets exist only on level reviews (item 454), so the l17 sections (2 minutes, 13 min 20 s, 12 minutes) and every drill in l13 to l16 and homework-4 are self-timed with the learner's watch; each carries `pace` and states it in prose. Wanted: `timed` (with `lockAtZero`) on a lesson screen or a run of screens, so a battery section ends on its own clock.
- `table` widget, a cell's own `wrong[]` id: the validator still refuses a `byKey` line for it (another vote). It bent l15 screen 4, where "10% of all 1,000" (100) and the reversed conditional (0.9) could not be keyed; both are named in the cell's `r{i}c{j}` line instead.
- Generated items (`generator: "arith"`) make only whole-number add, subtract, multiply and divide, so a `mock-oa-trader` sequences or probability section cannot be fresh per sitting. `<scratchpad>/mental-pressure-4/gen17.py` (recorded in `docs/reports/mental-pressure-4.md`) has eight sequence templates and six probability templates with labelled slips that could become `generator: "seq"` and `"prob"` kinds.
- `grid` with `population: 1000` and `state.groups`: the group labels are not drawn (no legend), so l15's natural-frequency screen explains the four colours only in the caption ("116 positives: 18 have it, 98 are clear"). Wanted: a small legend from each group's `label` and `count`.

Raised later (game-theory level 1):
- `table` widget, a cell's own `wrong[]` id: the validator still refuses a `byKey` line for it (another vote). It bent the l1 drill row on beliefs (the even average 2.5 instead of 2.6). Workaround: the slip is named in the cell's `r2c1` line.
- `grid` with a `frames` control: a frame can patch `cells` and `caption` but not `rowHeads`, `colHeads`, `rows` or `cols`, so drill games with different move names or sizes cannot share one payoff grid. Workaround: l3's drill keeps three 3 × 3 games on one grid (all T, M, B against L, C, R); l4's and l5's drills list their 2 × 2 games as rows of a `table` canvas. Wanted: `state.rowHeads` and `state.colHeads` (and a resize) patchable by a frame.
- `grid` shade sets have no legend: a payoff table with Ana's best replies (slot 3, green) and Ben's (slot 4, amber) has to say which colour is whose in prose (l3 hook). Wanted: a set `label` drawn as a small legend under the grid.
- `tap-regions` on a `grid`: the Why? copy draws the learner's selection in solid blue over the `explain.canvas` shade sets, hiding a cell in two sets (l1 screen 2 first showed (D, C) as plain blue). Workaround: `"selected": []` in every such `explain.canvas`. Wanted: the Why? copy drops the selection when the patch shades, or AUTHORING says to clear it.
- `plane` with a y range that spans 0: the x axis sits mid-height, and at 390 px a long `xLabel` collides with function labels at the right edge (l4 pennies lines). Also three crossing lines got a label placed beside the wrong line (l5 lower envelope). Workaround: `xAxis: "bottom"`, `yAxis: "left"` and `labelAt` on each function. Wanted: function labels kept clear of the axis label and anchored to their own line.
- Lesson screens hold one widget, so the brief's "choice, then numeric" follow-ups (l2 three desks, l3 spread table, l4 rock-paper-scissors) take two screens or move a part to the quiz. Workaround: l2 uses two follow-up screens (11 in all); l3 and l4 moved the choice to the quiz. Wanted: a two-part screen (one canvas, two graded widgets in sequence).

Raised later (exam mock-oa-arith):
- Generated items (`generator: "arith"`, item 452) make only whole-number add, subtract, multiply and divide, so 16 of the 80 questions in `mock-oa-arith` (6 decimal, 5 fraction, 5 percentage) are fixed authored items that repeat on every sitting, with only their options shuffled. Wanted: an E4 extension with `dec`, `frac` and `pct` operations and labelled near misses. Decimals: a misplaced point, the reverse operation, a slipped digit. Fractions: tops and bottoms added, the wrong operation, one fraction scaled by the wrong factor. Percentages: the complement, a dropped part, a misplaced point. `<scratchpad>/mock-oa-arith/fixed_arith.py` (embedded in `docs/reports/exam-mock-oa-arith.md`) has the range rules per block and the asserts, ready to port.
- Exam start card, parts list (`.exm-parts`) at 390 px: on a mock with a paper and a check, each part's text column shrinks to about 60 px, so "80 questions · 8 minutes · pass 55 net · forward only ..." wraps a word or two per line and the card grows to about 280 px tall. The 1280 px layout is fine. Workaround: none in content. Wanted: the part text takes the full card width at narrow widths, with the number badge beside the name.
- Pace line on mock results: generated arithmetic items count under their first skill ("Speed arithmetic 1 s") when the generator entry has `skills`, not under `mental-arithmetic` as AUTHORING section 5 says. Wanted: generated items always count under `mental-arithmetic`, or AUTHORING is changed to match.
- `choice` options holding `\tfrac{a}{b}` draw the fraction at about 70% of the option text (numerators near 9 px at 390 px), too small to read at a 6-second pace. Workaround: `mock-oa-arith` uses `\dfrac` in questions and options. Wanted: option and question KaTeX in display style by default, or an AUTHORING note that fraction options use `\dfrac`.

Raised later (puzzles level 5):
- `bars` canvas with a drag-point on `state.pivot`: the bars README says the widget target is "a number", but the validator refuses a bare number on any canvas except number-line and circle ("must be [x, y]"). l26 screen 3 (balance the distribution of the larger serial number at 14) uses `target: [14, 0]`, which grades correctly. Wanted: the validator accepts a bare number for a bars pivot, or the README says `[x, 0]`.
- `circle` canvas: one point and one angle only, so it cannot draw a chord, a second point on the rim or a point inside the disc. Bertrand's three procedures (l29 screens 1 to 3) use a `plane` with a parametric unit circle, a polygon for the triangle, hidden points moved by `derive` and a segment for the chord. Wanted: circle `chords` (two rim angles, or a midpoint) with a live length in `derive`.
- `plane` points: a point with `drag: true` and no `on` cannot be kept inside a region. In l29 screen 3 the chord's midpoint can be dragged outside the unit disc; the derived chord endpoints are clamped with `max(0, ...)`, so the chord collapses instead of vanishing. Wanted: a point constraint such as `within: { "r": 1 }` or `within: "<closed curve id>"`.
- `tree` canvas: no arrow from a leaf back to the root, so a "start again" round (von Neumann's paired flips in l25, a duel round in l28) shows a leaf labelled "start again" and says so in the caption. Wanted: `state.loops: { "<leaf path>": "root" }`, a curved arrow back to the root, as markov graphs draw for a return edge.
- Lesson schema, 8 to 12 screens (another vote after brainteasers 1): the puzzles interview form (three worked puzzles, trap, generalise, derive, code, drill, follow-up, transfer) fills 12 screens, so the l25 drill choice "why are HT and TH equally likely" moved to the quiz, and l28's choice of A's best first move is the trap screen, with the drill choice asking who survives. Wanted: a drill block of several tagged puzzles on one screen, each graded and dealt to the bank separately.

Raised later (brainteasers level 3):
- `plane` points with `hidden: true` draw no label, so a clock face cannot carry its numerals as hidden labelled points (l12 clock, every screen). Workaround: the four main tick `segments` carry the labels 12, 3, 6 and 9 with hand-tuned `labelOffset`. Wanted: `hidden` hides the dot only, or a plain `texts: [{ x, y, text }]` layer.
- `plane` with `equal: true` ignores `params.height` at desktop width: a 240 px clock drew about 410 px tall and pushed its control and question below the fold (l12 screens 1 and 2). Workaround: `canvas.maxHeight: 300`, which works on a plane although the README names only table and console. Wanted: `height` honoured with `equal`, or `maxHeight` documented for every kind.
- Copy rule: a factorial in prose, a title or a question (`60!`, `125!`) is refused as an exclamation mark (l13, l16 quiz, review-3). Workaround: the generator wraps every factorial in KaTeX (`$60!$`) and titles avoid them. Wanted: the validator ignores `!` straight after a digit or a closing bracket.
- `order` widget at 390 px: a line holding display-style KaTeX (`\dfrac`, a long bracketed sum) does not wrap and pushes the leave-out cross off the card (l14 derive and homework-3 hw2, first drafts). Workaround: `\tfrac` and shorter lines with "up to ...". Wanted: order lines that scroll their maths inside the card, as display maths does in prose.
- Validator warning "a problem screen without a canvas before the ladder ends" fires on every screen of a lesson with no ladder, such as the l16 unlabelled mixed set, where a telling canvas would name the puzzle's family. Workaround: neutral setup canvases (coins, a sock drawer, the pirate crew, a line of hats, crossing times) and an unshaded 7 × 7 board. Wanted: the warning only before an actual ladder, or an opt-out flag for a mixed set.
- `derive` expressions: `=` is allowed only inside quoted strings, so `==`, `<=` and `>=` cannot be used; the l12 clock caption shows 12 for hour 0 with `floor(m / 60) < 1 ? 12 : floor(m / 60)`. Wanted: comparison operators in derive expressions.

Raised later (brainteasers level 2):
- `manipulate` at 390 px: op chips keep their full width and two share a row, so labels of about 20 characters ("1 and 6 cross (6 min)") run off the right edge, and a long state `tex` line overflows its box (l11 hook). Workaround: chips cut to "1, 6 cross (6 min)" and the state line to "Over: 1, 3   3 min". Wanted: chips that wrap to one a row, and a state line that wraps or scrolls.
- `manipulate` state graphs are written out in full: the bridge hook with every one- or two-person trip both ways needs 158 states and over 600 ops. Workaround: pairs over and singles back only (46 states, about 37 KB of JSON), stated in the prose. Wanted: a rule-driven state space (a small move generator over a state record) instead of listed states.
- `tree` chain of 7 single children (l6 lions, worked back from one lion): top to bottom it draws about 700 px tall and pushes the question below the fold; left to right the pills shrink to about 9 px text at 390 px. Workaround: left to right. Wanted: a chain wraps onto two rows, or `compact` keeps 12 px text.
- `grid` with `canvas.maxHeight`: a 9 × 3 grid capped at 300 px scales as a picture, so its cell text falls to about 5 px (l9 $f(d, e)$ table). Workaround: transposed to 3 × 9 at full size. Wanted: a grid under `maxHeight` shrinks its cells, not its text, or the README warns against `maxHeight` on a tall grid.
- `graph` self-loops: a loop points away from the centre and draws over the node's `state.values` text below it (l8 ring, box 3 holds 3). Workaround: `state.valueSide: { "b3": "above" }` for loop nodes. Wanted: the value moves to the side away from a loop by default.
- `docs/reviews/voice-scan.py` does not end a sentence at `.'` or `?'` (single quotes), so "She asks, 'Step forward if you know.' Everyone..." counts as one long sentence. Workaround: quotes rewritten with the full stop outside ("...know'. Everyone") or as reported speech. Wanted: the scanner splits after `.'` and `?'` as it does after `."`.

Raised later (exam mock-oa-sig):
- Mock versions (E3 not built): `mock-oa-sig` ships as three fixed files (`mock-oa-sig`, `-2`, `-3`), so Review lists three separate mocks, each with its own score history and trend, and a learner who resits version 1 sees the same 17 items. Wanted: one mock with `versions: [...]` (or a bank draw per position) that deals the next unseen version and keeps one trend. `gen_sig.py` (embedded in `docs/reports/exam-mock-oa-sig.md`) already builds parallel forms from parameter pools with the option rules asserted, ready to port as a generator.
- Exam results page at 390 px: the PASS stamp sits over the score and covers the "7" of "of 17" (`mock-oa-sig` and both versions, 17 of 17). The 1280 px layout is fine. Workaround: none in content. Wanted: the stamp below or beside the score at narrow widths.
- Exam start card at 1280 px: with a description of about 110 words (the brief's text) the engine's rules list ("60 minutes ... 17 questions ... Pass mark 11 net") starts below the card's sticky footer and is cut mid-line until the learner scrolls inside the card. Workaround: none; the description is the brief's. Wanted: the rules list above the description, or a scroll hint.
- Validator, `numeric` `wrong` entries: nothing flags two `wrong` ranges that overlap, or a `wrong` range that touches the key's `tol` range. In `mock-oa-sig` Q9 the brief's "true positives over everyone" (0.0095) and "base rate" (0.01) overlap at a 0.005 tolerance, so the learner's 0.010 would match whichever comes first. Workaround: `gen_sig.py` asserts both rules and lists only the base rate. Wanted: a validator warning for overlapping `wrong` ranges and an error for a `wrong` range that overlaps the key's.

Raised later (game-theory level 2):
- Validator, `table` widget: a cell's own `wrong: [{ id, value | text }]` (documented in AUTHORING section 3, `table`) is not added to the keys the widget can return, so a `byKey` line on that id is an error ("the widget cannot return the key"). Needed: name a slip in a drill table ("you passed at the last node"). Workaround: `byKey` on the cell id (`r3c3`) with a line that covers the likely slip. Wanted: the validator collects cell `wrong` ids as keys.
- `table` widget with text answers that contain commas (payoff pairs such as `3, 5`): after grading, the "Accepted:" line joins every cell's answer with commas, so four pairs read as one list, "3, 5, 4, 2, 1, 3, 2, 0" (l6 centipede). Workaround: none; the cells themselves grade correctly. Wanted: answers in that line separated by semicolons or shown per cell.

Raised later (exam mock-oa-trader):
- Exam results page, "Part by part": a paper with `passNet` and the default 1/0/0 scoring is not `M.scored`, so the line falls into the share branch and prints "15 of 15 correct, 100%. Pass mark NaN%." (`js/screens/exam.js`, the `exm-pres` list reads `s.p.pass`). Seen on all three `mock-oa-trader` papers. Workaround: none in content; the brief requires `passNet`. Wanted: the line uses `needLine(s)` (or "10 of 15 right") whenever `passNet` is set.
- Interval scoring by width is not built: paper C grades intervals as a four-option choice with one tightest interval holding the value, and the wider holder scores 0, where the reported tests score it less. Wanted: per-option marks (`marks: 0.5` on an option) or an interval widget that scores a typed range by whether it holds the value and by its width.
- Bank draw for exam papers (E3) is not built, so `mock-oa-trader` ships as three fixed files (`-2`, `-3`) with three separate trends. `seqs.py`, `probs.py` and `rank.py` (in `docs/reports/exam-mock-oa-trader.md`) hold one template per position with its placement, gap and uniqueness checks, ready to port as generators that would retire the fixed versions.
- `tools/playthrough.mjs --shots` names exam shots by `screenIndex` only, so on a multi-paper exam each paper overwrites the one before (only paper 3's items survive) and every paper's start card and the check card share `start--1`. Workaround: a scratch `examshot.mjs` that names by paper and step. Wanted: the paper index and a step counter in the file name.
- `order` widget in a mock: a three-line ranking scores all or nothing, so a learner who swaps the bottom two scores the same as one who reverses all three. Workaround: none; kept the reported all-or-nothing marking. Wanted (optional): per-pair partial marks for ranking items.

Raised later (mm-games level 3):
- `trade` widget, one size for both sides (engine pass D follow-up 5): at a position equal to the limit every quote is a `limit` break, so l10's one-sided quote at the limit cannot be played. Workaround: the game stops one fill short (at +3 the bid drops to 9, out of the noise trader's `reach`), and the one-sided quote is a static `orderbook` with a `choice` (l10 screen 2). Wanted: separate bid and ask sizes, or a size of 0 on one side.
- `trade` widget, `limit` must be above 0: l10's edge case of a limit of zero (reduce only, then quote nothing) cannot be a game. Workaround: a quiz `choice`. Wanted: `limit: 0` with an opening position, so only the reducing side may trade.
- `trade` widget, one instrument and always flat at the start: l11's hedge leg (buy the first die against a short in the sum) cannot be traded, and a carried value cannot open a second game with the position. Workaround: a `numeric` graded on `{{carry.sum.position}}` beside a `table` whose `derive` shows the residual sd, then a fixed-number drill. Wanted: a second instrument in one game (a hedge leg the learner can take), or `start: { position, cash }` that may read a carried value.
- `trade` results table names counterparties by raw type (`noise`, `scripted`): l13's interviewer and the homework mock's interviewer show as "scripted". Workaround: the prose says so. Wanted: a `label` per counterparty ("Interviewer", "Other traders").
- `trade` spread bounds hold for the whole game: l13 and the homework mock cannot tighten the width limit once two cards are known. Workaround: width 1 to 4 throughout; the narration and rubric ask for the width reason. Wanted: a card that sets new `spread` bounds.
- Carried values in prose: a carried P&L of 3.5 prints "3.5", so "£{{carry.lim.pnl|0}}" reads "£3.5" and a loss "£−5.5". Workaround: "a P&L in pounds of {{carry.lim.pnl|0}}". Wanted: a money format for placeholders (`{{carry.lim.pnl|0|money}}` giving "+£3.50" and "−£5.50").
- Carried values in a canvas state: a whole-string placeholder with a fallback (`"x": "{{carry.lim.position|0}}"`) becomes the text "0", not the number 0, when the store is empty (`carryResolve` in `player.js` returns the fallback string). Workaround: the bare placeholder in canvas state, the fallback only in prose. Wanted: a numeric fallback stays a number.
- Homework items cannot read carry (follow-up 3) and homework may not be `timed`: the narrated game and the mock round use fixed model-run numbers, and the prose states the ten minutes for the learner to keep with a watch. Wanted: a per-item countdown for spoken answers (E6).

Raised later (game-theory level 3):
- Canvas `derive` captions with money: `£{{fixed(x, 2)}}` renders a negative value as "£−70.00" (l11 and l13 hooks, where the call's value runs negative). Workaround: "earns {{...}} pounds". Wanted: a `money(x, 2)` helper (or `£` support in `fixed`) that writes "−£70.00".
- `boxes` cards cannot be turned over by a tap: `face` is state only, so "turn one card, then choose" (l14 hook, the three-card look) cannot be played on the canvas. Workaround: card A is shown face up already and the learner taps the card to take (count mode among the other two). Wanted: a `tap: "flip"` mode on boxes that flips a tapped card and reports it, for explore screens.

Raised later (mm-games level 4):
- `trade` widget, one instrument and no way for the learner to take liquidity: l15 (buy B and C, sell A; walk two dealers' books), l16 (buy A, sell E and F) and l17 (sell the call, buy the put and the sum) cannot be played as games on the taking side. Workaround: the taking side stays on `table` ledgers, `table` widgets and `numeric`, and each game puts the learner on the quoting side against a `scripted` arbitrageur. Wanted: a taking action (lift or hit a background level) and a second instrument in one game.
- `trade` widget, spread bounds fixed for the whole game: l14's range game cannot narrow the allowed width after the hint on turn 2, and the width part of the interval score is only bounded, never graded. Workaround: width 2 to 10 throughout, and the score is taught on `numeric` screens. Wanted: a card that sets new `spread` bounds, or a grading mode that adds the width to the P&L (`score: "interval"`).
- `trade` results table shows `informed` and `scripted` for the interviewer and the arbitrageur. Workaround: the prose names them. Wanted: a `label` per counterparty (repeats the level 3 bullet).
- No `play` widget for a board of bets (engine pass D follow-up 4): l18 has no game. Workaround: rounds on `table` widgets, `sliders` on a `bars` canvas graded by `answer`, and a `numeric` with `carry: "bank"` read in the next screen's prose. Wanted: a betting-board widget with a bankroll ledger across rounds.
- Prose: `\$` does not escape a dollar sign. "£1 buys \$1.25, \$1 buys €0.92" rendered a backslash and set the text between the two dollars as KaTeX (l15 first draft; `opt-dash/l1-reading-a-chain.json` has "\\$437" today). Workaround: "1.25 dollars". Wanted: `\$` as a literal dollar in prose, questions and options.
- Validator: an explore screen whose only interaction is a `distribution` with `params.drag: true` (README-B: "shows the shade handles on an explore screen without a drag-point widget") is refused, "an explore screen needs an interactive canvas". Workaround: a control on a free name `k` with `derive` writing `shade.from` and `shade.to` (l14 screen 1). Wanted: the validator counts `drag: true` on a distribution as interactive.
- Validator warning "a problem screen without a canvas before the ladder ends" fires on a `table` widget screen, which is its own canvas (l16 screen 2, l18 screens 2 and 3). Workaround: none; left as warnings. Wanted: the warning skips `table` widgets.

Raised later (exam topic-3.3):
- Topic exam start card ignores `description`: `js/screens/exam.js` shows a fixed sentence for topic exams and reads `description` only for mocks and stage exams. A topic paper of interview questions could not say once that it is sat without a calculator. Workaround: "No calculator." in the prose of the mental-maths items. Wanted: `description` shown on topic exams too.
- `trade` over `orderbook`: when the learner's quote sits at the price of a book level, the book draws two rows at one price (one marked "You"), which reads as a duplicate level at 390 px. Workaround: book levels set away from the opening quote (bids from £12, asks from £16 around a £12.50 at £15.50 quote). Wanted: the learner's size drawn on the book's row at that price, or a merged row.

Raised later (mock-superday-trader):
- Exam papers, bank draw (E3, item 456 "not built"): `mock-superday-trader` ships one fixed file, so a resat superday repeats its 24 items word for word. Workaround: none; the brief keeps one version because a superday is a rehearsal sat a few times. Wanted: a paper entry that draws a family-balanced set of fresh items from `bank/*.json` per attempt, so the probability and Fermi papers differ on each sitting.
- `trade` in an exam scores right or wrong only (target met and breaks within `maxBreaks`), at the paper's `right` mark. A game that earns £19.50 scores the same as one at the £12 benchmark, and a near miss scores 0. Workaround: one benchmark per game, set about £1 to £7 under the model run. Wanted: a P&L-scaled mark on a trade item (for example `marks: { at: benchmark, full: 20 }`, linear between), shown on the results page.
- `longform` in an exam is all or nothing: 7 or more ticked points of 10 counts as right and scores the full mark, 6 scores 0. Five of the 24 items here are longform, so one rubric point can swing a paper's pass. Workaround: none in content. Wanted: a longform mark of ticked points over 10 (fractional marks are already allowed by `scoring`), or a per-paper `longform: "points"` switch.
- Mock start card at 390 px: each part card in the list squeezes its name and its line ("6 questions · 45 minutes · pass 65%") into a narrow left column, about a third of the card, with the part number alone on the right ("Probability and expected value" wraps to four lines; the check names to nine). Seen on `mock-superday-trader` and `mock-oa-trader`; at 1280 px the cards are fine. Workaround: none. Wanted: the 390 px card puts the number left and lets the text take the full width.
- `orderbook` under `trade`: `depth: 3` counts the learner's "You" row, so with the quote at the top only two resting levels a side show (the third, for example £103.50 above a £102 ask, is hidden). Workaround: none needed for the game. Wanted: `depth` counts resting levels only, or the README says it includes the learner's quote.

Raised later (bank part 1 session 1):
- Playing bank items: `tools/playthrough.mjs` has no flag to play one bank file, and in a set page `ZQ.test.current()` does not name the item shown, so a test cannot choose a wrong answer for the item in front of it. Workaround: a scratch script wraps `ZQ.select.bankDeck` to record the dealt uids, imports a save with the `requires` lessons finished, schedules every other item away with `ZQ.state.bankResult`, and loops decks of 8 four times (right, then each `wrong` id and distractor in turn) until all 50 items are played. Wanted: `--bank <file>` in the playthrough (every item right, then each `wrong` id routed to its `byKey`), and `uid` in `ZQ.test.current()` on set pages.
- Bank family names drift from course tags: the brief listed `coupon-collector` and `derangements` as bank-only, but `puzzles` l2 and l5 now tag their items `every-coupon` and `hats`, and puzzles l1 uses `dice-games` where mm-games and the bank use `dice-ev`. The validator cannot see this because any kebab word is a valid family. Workaround: session 1 uses `every-coupon` and `hats`; `sample.json` keeps the old names. Wanted: a family registry (engine pass B follow-up 6) that warns when a bank item's first tag is used by no course item and is not declared bank-only.
- `choice` options with KaTeX fractions: `\tfrac` inside an option button renders at inline size, too small to read at 390 px (`1m04`, `1m10`). Workaround: plain 7/9 and 3/8 text in options and in their prose. Wanted: option text set at display size for fractions (or `\tfrac` promoted to `\frac` in options), so ranking and fraction-compare items can show real fractions.

Raised later (game-theory fix pass):
- boxes canvas, card labels: `css/canvases.css` line 47 (`.cv-svg text { font-size: 13px }`) overrides the `font-size` attribute that `boxes.js` line 114 sets on card text, so `size: "large"` cards keep 13-unit labels. In a 540-unit frame at 390 px that is about 8.6 px, and the suits in game-theory l11 screens[4], [6], [8] and l12 screens[2], [4], [5], [8], [9] are hard to read. `params.rows: 2` does not help: the cards were never shrunk, only their text. Workaround: the prose names every card. Wanted: set the card label size with `style.fontSize` (or a CSS variable) so it scales with the card.

Raised later (topic 3.3 fix passes, mm-games and mental-pressure):
- Table widget: a cell's own `wrong[]` id still cannot be a `byKey` key (two more votes). mm-games l8 s2 and s4 and mental-pressure l2, l3 and l4 key on `r{i}c{j}` and name the slip in that line.

Bank session 4 (3 Nov):
- Playing bank items (second vote for the session 1 bullet): no `--bank <file>` in `tools/playthrough.mjs`, and `ZQ.test.current()` on a set page has no `uid`. A further snag: an item missed in one scratch pass is due again two days on, so it reappears in the next pass's deck unless every other item is rescheduled first. Workaround: `scratchpad/s4/bank-1-4/play-bank.mjs` wraps `ZQ.select.bankDeck` to record each deal, imports a save with the 19 `requires` lessons finished, and before each pass walks every item not under test to the 30-day box with `ZQ.state.bankResult`. Wanted: `--bank <file>` (every item right, then each `wrong` id and distractor routed to its line) and `uid` in `ZQ.test.current()`.
- Test hook has no way to give a wrong answer: `ZQ.test` offers `answerCorrect()` only, so a script must type a `wrong` value into the input itself, and for `unit: "%"` it must know to type the value times 100 (the first run typed 0.26 for `4m03` and got key `null`). Workaround: the scratch script scales `%` values and clicks distractors by their text. Wanted: `ZQ.test.answerWrong(id)` that enters a numeric `wrong` value in the widget's own units or picks a choice distractor, in lesson and set mode.
- Duplicates rule (DECISIONS "Duplicates (6 Nov)") has no tool: grepping a number such as 40, 90 or 170 hits hundreds of unrelated course and exam units, so the check is done by structure words and reading. Workaround: targeted greps per structure plus a scratch scan (`dups.py`) that lists units sharing an item's parameters and key, read by hand. Wanted: `node tools/validate.mjs --dups <file>` that, for each item, lists course, exam and bank items with the same first tag (or the same family words) that share its parameters or its key.

Bank session 2 (3 Nov):
- Test hook cannot give a wrong answer (second vote for the session 4 bullet), and an `expression` makes it harder: the scratch script must set `math-field.value` to the `wrong` tex and dispatch an `input` event, or fill `.wd-expr-txt` when MathLive is absent, besides scaling `%` values and clicking `.opt[data-id]`. Workaround: `scratchpad/s2/bank-1-2/play-bank.mjs` does all three inside `.lsn-set-body`. Wanted: `ZQ.test.answerWrong(id)` for numeric, choice and expression widgets, in lesson and set mode, plus `uid` in `ZQ.test.current()`.
- Duplicates rule across parallel sessions (third vote for the duplicates tool): sessions 2, 3 and 4 were written at once, so a grep at the start cannot see the other authors' numbers. `2p04` (d12, one paid reroll) and session 4's `dice-ev-twelve-sided-three-rolls` (d12, three free rolls) landed side by side. Workaround: a final grep after the other files landed, and the overlap is listed for the second reader. Wanted: the `--dups` check, run by the orchestrator after a parallel batch.
- `numeric` tolerance has `abs` and `rel` only: a Fermi estimate is right within a factor, not within a fixed share either side, so `2k03` (black cabs in London) had to be a choice of powers of ten. Workaround: a four-option choice of 1,000 to 1 million. Wanted: `tol: { "factor": 3 }`, accepting answers between key/3 and key × 3 (a log-scale tolerance), so Fermi items can take a typed number.
- No widget for a two-way price outside a `trade` game: "fair value and a two-way price" (`2k01`, `2k06`) can grade only one number. Workaround: `2k01` grades the fair value after a reveal and states the quote in the prose; `2k06` grades the offer of a quote of stated width. Wanted: a `quote` widget (bid and offer) graded on its centre within a tolerance of fair value and its width within bounds, usable in sets and the bank.
- `choice` options with `\tfrac` (second vote for the session 1 bullet): `2p11`'s key option $e^{1.5} - \tfrac12 e^{0.5}$ rendered its half too small at 390 px. Workaround: $0.5e^{0.5}$. Wanted: as in the session 1 bullet.

Bank session 3 (3 Nov):
- `choice` with `shuffle: true` in a set page: `runSet` seeds the shuffle from the slot key (`'set' + i`, `player.js` line 1114) and the widget's JSON length (line 156), not from the item. So a bank item dealt into the same deck slot shows the same option order every time it returns, and a learner on Leitner repeats can learn the position of the key. Workaround: none in content; the scratch loop saw all 13 session-3 choice items in more than one order only because they landed in different slots. Wanted: the shuffle seeded by the item's uid and its seen count (or the day), in the bank deck, practice and redo.
- Duplicates across parallel sessions (fourth vote for the `--dups` tool): `3k01` first revealed one heart from four cards, the same structure as session 2's `2k01` (one heart from three), and `3k04` opened like session 4's `4k08`; neither file existed when this session started. Workaround: a last grep and dump of the other sessions' files, then `3k01` shows a heart and a club and `3k04` is reworded. Wanted: the `--dups` check, also flagging items that share a first tag and a structure across files.
- Family names (second vote for the session 1 registry bullet): the brief filed a balance puzzle (`3b02`, one light coin in eight) under `weighing-scale`, which the courses use for the digital-scale bag puzzle, and `brainteasers/homework-2` item 4 (the modulo hat strategy) carries `hats-simultaneous`, not `hats-mod-k`. The validator cannot see either. Workaround: `3b02` uses `weighing-heavy`; the retag is proposed in `docs/reports/bank-1-3.md`. Wanted: a family registry with a one-line definition per family, so a second reader can check each item's first tag against it.

Bank second read (4 Nov):
- Results list for `longform` bank items: every behavioural item has the same `question`, "Answer aloud, then write your answer here.", and the "Problem by problem" list on the results page shows only `question`. A deck of five star stories therefore lists five identical rows, and the learner cannot tell which to revisit. The deck screen itself shows the prompt. Workaround: none in content, since `AUTHORING.md` and the bank brief fix the `question` text. Wanted: for a `longform` item the results row shows `widget.prompt` (or its first sentence) in place of `question`.
- `tools/playthrough.mjs --bank all --mobile`: `--mobile` changes nothing for `--bank`. It only adds 390 px copies of screenshots, and `--bank` takes one shot per file (of the results page, and only with `--shots`), so the "wider than the window" check runs at 1280 px only. Workaround: the second reader ran a scratch copy with the viewport set to 390 px (`scratchpad/review/pt390/pt390.mjs`). Wanted: with `--mobile`, `bankPlay` sets the viewport to 390 px before its loop, so every bank item gets the overflow check at phone width.

Raised later (brief research-int, 4 Nov):
- E11 `parts` leaves four rules open that `research-int` review and homework items depend on (R1.2, H1.2, H2.4, H2.6, R3.7, H3.4): (1) how an item with parts is credited in a set, an exam and the redo queue (all parts right, or a fraction), (2) whether a later part still opens after a wrong earlier one, (3) which item kinds may carry `parts` (review, homework, bank, exam), and (4) the name clash with a stage exam's own `parts` array, which `js/engine/select.js` reads as `E.parts` when it collects items. Wanted: state the credit rule in `docs/AUTHORING.md`, let every part open once the previous one is checked right or wrong, and make the validator refuse `parts` on a quiz item.
- E14 `data` files: the spec says a size cap is stated but gives none. The six `research-int` files total about 88 KB and the largest is 27 KB. Wanted: the cap in `docs/AUTHORING.md`; `data` allowed on drill files, bank items and exam items as well as lesson `code` widgets; hidden `tests` and `setup` lines reading the files by bare name; the validator checking that each path exists and is under the cap. C++ has no file system, so `data` is Python only, and the C++ drills pass data as arguments. Wanted as well: a read-only panel under the editor showing the first rows of each attached file, so the learner sees the columns without printing them.
- `code` `isclose` and `equal` compare NumPy arrays, Series and nested lists, but not a pandas DataFrame (it has no `tolist()`), and `math.isclose(nan, nan)` is false, so a test cannot expect a NaN. `research-int` l17 and l19 return frames and expect a NaN for a group with too few rows. Workaround written into the brief: tests call a scalar or `.round(6).to_numpy().tolist()` and check a missing value with `bool(np.isnan(...))`. Wanted: compare a DataFrame column by column with its index, and treat NaN as equal to NaN under `isclose` when the case says `"nan": "equal"`.
- The `code` widget shows no figure: a matplotlib `plt.show()` or `savefig` produces nothing the learner can see, and `research-int` l15 and l20 teach reports that include a plot. Workaround: the take-home lessons ask for a plot in words and show the finished plot on a `scatter` or `bars` canvas. Wanted: a figure output under the results (the Agg backend rendered to an image), with a size cap.
- No clock on a lesson screen: `research-int` l19 ("A timed data task", 25 minutes) says "start your own clock", because E6 timers cover reviews, practice and bank decks and E8 gives drills a stopwatch, but a lesson `code` screen has neither. Wanted: `pace` on a lesson screen showing a visible countdown that never locks the editor, and a "time taken" line in the results.
- `scatter` `sim` draws n seeded pairs with a fixed n, and only `rho` has a control, so `research-int` l10 cannot show how the t statistic of a correlation of 0.05 grows with n. Workaround: a `derive` caption with the sample r and t at n = 250, and a `table` of t against n. Wanted: a control on `n` for `sim` (the same seeded stream, first n pairs).

Raised later (brief systems, 4 Nov):
- [Should] `<mutex>`, `<atomic>` and `<thread>` under YoWASP libc++ (no pthreads) are unverified. The systems brief teaches mutexes and atomics from listings and the E13 canvas, and no code problem needs them, but `l8-locks-and-deadlock` and `l9-atomics` would like a `compiles` case that checks a learner's `std::scoped_lock lk(a, b);` or a `std::atomic<int>` counter. Wanted: confirm which of the three headers compile (a single-threaded no-op mutex is enough) and say so in AUTHORING section 3; if `std::thread` cannot compile, document that listings using it stay unrunnable. Workaround: `order` tasks over listings.
- [Should] Allocation counting for C++ items. `l15-no-allocation`, `P-format-price`, `P-handler` and homework-4 count calls to `new` by replacing global `operator new` in a hidden `prelude`. That may clash with the `leakCheck` wrapper and depends on the optimiser keeping the allocation (checked with g++ 13.3 and clang++ 18.1 at `-O1`, not with the app's clang 21). Wanted: a harness flag `allocCheck: true` giving `alloc_count()` and `reset_alloc_count()` (calls that survive optimisation), as `leakCheck` does for leaks. Workaround: the prelude counter.
- [Should] Hardware canvases. Caches (`l2-caches`, homework-1) and branch predictors (`l5-branches`) have no canvas: the lessons hand-author a state patch per step on an `array` or `grid` (a 4-line cache over 16 accesses) or fall back to a `table`. Wanted: a `cache` canvas (sets by ways of line tags, an address list, step and back, LRU, hit and miss tally, `derive` access to `values.misses`) and a small `predictor` canvas (history cells, a counter, the guess, a tally), both with the same stepping model as E13.
- [Should] E13 `interleave` details beyond the spec in `docs/briefs/interview-plan.md` (f). (a) Blocked lanes: a lane waiting on a held lock cannot step, and a condition-variable wait sleeps until a later notify, ending in "deadlock" or "asleep forever" states a `derive` can read (U5 to U7 and U11 in `docs/briefs/systems.md`). (b) Weak memory: an optional per-lane store buffer, so the (0, 0) outcome of the store-buffering test (`l9-atomics`) is steppable; without it the lesson teaches that outcome from a `choice`. (c) Expose the enumerated outcome counts to captions and `derive` (`values.outcomes`), so "18 of 20 end at 1" is read, not typed.
- [Could] Bank and drills do not meet. Bank items refuse `code` (AUTHORING section 5) and drills (E8) live apart, so code families such as the ring buffer, an LRU cache and a percentile never join the Interview practice deck's spacing. Wanted: a bank item of the form `{ "drill": "sy-lru-cache" }` that schedules a drill id with the same Leitner boxes. Workaround: drills are practised from `#drill` only.
- [Could] `numeric` accepts decimal only, but wire formats, addresses and byte maps are naturally hex (`0x2823`, `ff ff ff e7`). Wanted: accept a `0x` prefix, or a `radix: 16` option that also shows the answer in hex in feedback, for `l3-virtual-memory`, `l14-messages-on-the-wire` and homework-3. Workaround: bytes asked as decimal.

Raised later (brief coding-int, 4 Nov):

- [Must] E11 parts must accept a `code` widget as a part. Coding-int level 4 homework items 1 and 2 are live rounds whose follow-ups are code (a leaderboard `reset`, a calendar `cancel`). Wanted: a part may be any widget kind, a code part's hidden tests are the cumulative tests of the item up to that part, and the starter of a later code part may show the reference solution of the earlier part, so every part stands alone. Workaround: none that keeps the reveal order.
- [Should] Draw code problems into a mock. E3 `draw` reads `bank/<family>`, and bank items refuse `code`, so `mock-oa-code` cannot draw fresh coding problems per attempt (coding-int l15 and l17 send learners to it). Wanted: `draw: [{ "from": "drills/<pack>", "tags": [...], "difficulty": [3, 4], "n": 2 }]`, or code items with hidden tests allowed in bank files. Related to the earlier bullet "Bank and drills do not meet".
- [Should] Group labels on code tests for partial credit. A slow but correct solution passes the small cases and is stopped by the 5 s limit on the first large one, which is the partial credit that coding-int l15 and l17 teach, but the results show only "Stopped after 5 s on this test". Wanted: a `group` on each case (`small`, `large`), results lines "small group 9 of 9, large group 0 of 2", and the later cases of a stopped group reported as "not run" rather than silently dropped.
- [Could] Carry the learner's code between screens. Staged class problems (coding-int l12 file system, l13 ledger, l19 calendar) use one `code` screen per stage, and each later starter is the earlier reference solution plus stubs, because `carry` holds values only. Wanted: `carry` on a `code` widget that stores the accepted source, and `{{carry.name}}` in a later `starter`, falling back to the reference when the earlier screen was not completed.
- [Could] Return a drill session result to a lesson. Coding-int l17 and l19 send the learner to a two-drill session and a single-drill stopwatch (E8) and cannot read the outcome back. Wanted: the session's minutes per drill and tests passed available to the lesson as `{{carry.session.minutes1}}`, so a later screen can ask the learner to compare their split with the 5, 25, 25, 5 plan.
- [Could] E8 filter vocabulary. Briefs name drill filters by pattern, and `docs/AUTHORING.md` lists no pattern slugs. Wanted: a stated list in AUTHORING section 5 (suggested: `hashing`, `prefix-sums`, `windows`, `stacks-queues`, `heaps-tries`, `intervals`, `bits`, `simulation`), validated against the drill files.
- [Could] Document nested integer vectors in C++ code cases. AUTHORING lists `std::vector<std::vector<double>>` only; coding-int uses `std::vector<std::vector<int>>` with rows of different lengths as arguments and results (events, intervals, grids), `long long` returns and `std::vector<std::string>` returns. Wanted: the supported list confirmed in section 3, and a playthrough case for each.
- [Could] A playthrough assertion for "the starter fails by time". Coding-int l17 and homework-4 use correct but quadratic starters that fail only on the 5 s limit (native `g++ -O1` timings measured: 10.96 s for `count_subarrays_sum_k` at 200,000 zeros, 6.6 s for the `std::find` starter on 200,000 even numbers; the wasm speed is not measured). Wanted: `playthrough.mjs` runs each starter and fails the build if a starter marked `slowStarter: true` does not stop on time in the real runner.

Raised later (fix pass research-int brief, 4 Nov):
- E3 `draw` reads bank files, and bank items refuse `code`, so the `research-int` Level 4 families (interpolation, group regression, data cleaning, rank-one update) cannot be drawn into `mock-oa-quant-data` as code tasks. Wanted: a draw source for drill items or `code` items (a similar bullet exists for coding-int). Workaround: the L4 example draw is numeric and choice only (R4.1 to R4.4, H4.8).
- E11 `parts` inside a `timed` review (`research-int` R1.2): the clock rule for an item whose later parts are still unopened at zero is not stated. Wanted: unopened parts count as missed, and the item's credit is the sum of its parts' credits. Workaround: the brief says so; R1.2 could move to homework if the engine disagrees.
- E8 drill pattern filter: the `research-int` drills carry pattern names (`interpolation`, `group-regression`, `rank-one-update`, `data-cleaning`) that AUTHORING does not list. Wanted: the pattern vocabulary fixed in section 5 (seconding the E8 filter vocabulary bullet from another brief). Workaround: the names are provisional.

Raised later (fix pass systems brief, 4 Nov):
- [Should] E13 `interleave` requirement for the engine pass (acceptance criteria; the `systems` level 2 author must not start until they pass). (a) The step set is load, add, store, fetch-add, lock, unlock, wait and notify, plus conditional steps (U4 "if ok: store, cash out"; U11 `c2` waits only if `c1` saw an empty queue). (b) A lane whose next step is a lock held by another lane is disabled, and a schedule in which no lane can step ends "deadlock". (c) A wait step sleeps until a notify that begins after it, otherwise the lane ends "asleep forever". (d) Steps can be marked by tapping, and `values.outcomes` gives each reachable final, including the end states "deadlock" and "asleep", with its schedule count, counting only the schedules the rules allow (U6 is 4 complete and 2 deadlock of 6, U7 is 162 complete and 6 deadlock of 168, U11 is 5 awake and 1 asleep of 6; a plain interleaving count of the U7 ring would be 34,650). (e) Up to 3 lanes of 4 steps (the U7 ring is 12 steps) with the enumeration cached. A level author states a count such as "of 168" only after the canvas reproduces it. Supersedes the open detail in the E13 bullet raised with the systems brief; the store-buffer option there stays optional.
- [Should] E11 `parts`: each part of an item may be any widget kind (`numeric`, `choice`, `multi`, `table`), graded and revealed in turn. The `systems` homework items that mix kinds are hw-1 item 2, hw-2 items 2, 3 and 6, and hw-4 items 2 and 4; section (f) of `docs/briefs/interview-plan.md` does not say parts may differ in kind.

## Raised later (fix pass coding-int brief, 4 Nov)

- [Should] Orderbook per-order labels. The `orderbook` canvas takes one total and one label per level, so coding-int l10 cannot draw the first-in-first-out queue inside a level with it. The brief now pairs an `orderbook` with an `array` in queue mode. Wanted: a level may carry a list of `[id, size]` orders drawn as stacked segments in time order, or a documented `pair` recipe. Workaround: the `pair` in the brief.
- [Should] Confirm two bindings that the coding-int brief relies on and AUTHORING does not state: a `slider` in `canvas.controls` bound to the nested key `order.size` of an `orderbook` (l10 screen 2), and a `derive` rule `"i5.from": "p.x"` that moves a `number-line` interval from a draggable point (l1 screen 2). If either fails, the level agent raises it and uses the stated fallback.
- [Must] Pass C is still open in `PLAN.md`. The coding-int brief now says that l8 screen 8 (E14), level 3 homework item 5 (E11) and all of level 4 (E3, E8, E11) wait for pass C to be ticked and documented in AUTHORING; `PLAN.md` should split the level 2 to 4 tasks the same way. The `code` part of E11 (level 4 homework item 1) and `#exam/mock-oa-code` are the two open requirements.
- [Could] Wasm timing record. The new l17 slow starters (C++ `count_subarrays_divisible_by_k` at `n = 400,000`, over 60 s natively; `longest_zero_sum_subarray`, 25.96 s natively) are timed on native `-O1` only. The level agent records the real clang and wasm times in its report; if either is under 15 s in wasm, raise `n`.

Raised later (topic 3.5 briefs, engine requirements, 4 Nov):
- [Must] C-parts-code: E11 `parts` accepts a `code` widget as a part. Coding-int level 4 homework items 1 and 2 are live rounds with code follow-ups. A code part's hidden tests are the cumulative tests of the item up to that part, and a later code part's starter may show the earlier reference solution. First needed: coding-int level 4 homework item 1.
- [Must] C-parts-mixed: E11 `parts` of one item may differ in widget kind (`numeric`, `choice`, `multi`, `table`), each graded and revealed in turn. First needed: systems level 1 homework item 2 (also hw-2 items 2, 3, 6 and hw-4 items 2, 4).
- [Must] C-parts-credit: the E11 rules stated in `docs/AUTHORING.md`: item credit is the sum of equal part credits; every part opens once the previous one is checked, right or wrong; unopened parts count as missed when a `timed` review locks; the validator refuses `parts` on a quiz item; the stage-exam `parts` name clash in `js/engine/select.js` is resolved. First needed: research-int l1 (review-1 item R1.2).
- [Must] C-data-cap: the E14 `data` size cap stated in `docs/AUTHORING.md` (suggest 256 KB a file); `data` allowed on drill files and exam items as well as lesson `code` widgets; hidden `tests` and `setup` read the files by bare name; the validator checks each path exists and is under the cap. Python only. First needed: research-int l10 (transfer screen).
- [Must] C-draw-code: E3 `draw` takes a `drills/<pack>` source (or code items with hidden tests are allowed in bank files) so `mock-oa-code` and `mock-oa-quant-data` draw fresh coding tasks per attempt; `exams/mock-oa-code.json` is authored before coding-int l15. First needed: coding-int l15.
- [Must] C-drill-patterns: the E8 pattern vocabulary fixed in `docs/AUTHORING.md` section 5 and validated against drill files: `hashing`, `prefix-sums`, `windows`, `stacks-queues`, `heaps-tries`, `intervals`, `bits`, `simulation`, `interpolation`, `group-regression`, `rank-one-update`, `data-cleaning`. First needed: coding-int l17.
- [Must] C-alloc-check: a C++ harness flag `allocCheck: true` giving `alloc_count()` and `reset_alloc_count()` that survive `-O1` under the app's clang 21 and do not clash with `leakCheck`. Systems l15, P-format-price, P-handler and homework-4 use it in place of a replaced `operator new`. First needed: systems l15. Counting rule: `alloc_count()` counts calls to any global `operator new` or `operator new[]` in the whole program (harness and test setup included) since the last `reset_alloc_count()` or start-up, so tests reset after setup and read last; `malloc` is not counted.
- [Must] E13-blocked-lanes: the `interleave` canvas disables a lane whose next step is a lock held by another lane; a schedule with no steppable lane ends "deadlock"; a wait with no later notify ends "asleep forever"; conditional steps; `values.outcomes` gives each reachable final, including those end states, with its schedule count (U6 is 4 complete and 2 deadlock of 6, U7 162 and 6 of 168, U11 5 and 1 of 6). First needed: systems l8.

Raised later (coding-int L1, 4 Nov):
- [Should] `table` widget: `validate.mjs` refuses `byKey` keys that name a cell's `wrong[]` id ("the widget cannot return the key"), although `js/engine/widgets/table.js` (pass 7) returns that id and AUTHORING section 3 documents it. Wanted: the validator adds each cell's `wrong[].id` to the widget's keys. Workaround: coding-int l1 screen 10 keys its feedback on the cell ids `r2c3` and `r3c3` instead.
- [Should] C++ `types`: `validate.mjs` warns that `std::vector<std::vector<int>>` is not a supported type, yet it compiles and passes in YoWASP clang 21 as an argument (rows of any length, empty list included) and as a result compared with `equal` (coding-int l1 screen 6, 10 cases). Wanted: add it to the supported list and drop the warning (seconds the earlier "nested integer vectors" bullet). Workaround: none needed; the 12 warnings stay.
- [Could] `pair` of two `array` canvases side by side: when one side holds a single short cell, both sides are drawn at a very small scale (cells about 20 px, labels unreadable). Wanted: each side keeps the array's own cell size, or the pair stacks. Workaround: coding-int l4 screen 8 shows the two heaps as a `table` canvas with a `frames` control.
- [Could] `tree` canvas: no way to mark a single node (a trie's end-of-word mark); `shade` and `highlight` colour the whole path from the root. Wanted: `state.mark: { "<path>": "end" }` or a node fill. Workaround: coding-int l2 writes "word" beside end nodes with `state.values`.
- [Could] Starter check in the playthrough. `playthrough.mjs` runs each code widget's `solution` but never its `starter`, so "the starter fails its tests" is checked only by hand. Wanted: `--starters` runs every starter in the real runner and fails the build if one passes all its tests (with the slow-starter timing of the earlier bullet). Workaround: a scratch Playwright script in the coding-int L1 agent's scratchpad (`codecheck.mjs`), results in `docs/reports/coding-int-1.md`.


Raised later (systems L1, 4 Nov):
- [Should] `validate.mjs`, `table` widget: a cell's `wrong: [{ "id", "value" }]` (AUTHORING section 3, `table`) is not added to the keys a `byKey` may use, so `byKey.oneway` is refused as "the widget cannot return the key". Needed in systems l1 screen 5 (a one-way time typed for a round trip). Workaround: the line sits on the cell id (`r0c1`) and names both slips.
- [Should] Playthrough: `--lesson` and `--set` check that a `code` widget's `solution` passes, but never that its `starter` fails; only `--drill` does, and it prints only pass or fail, not how many tests the starter passes. Wanted: the same starter check for lesson, quiz, review and homework code widgets, with "starter passes k of n". Workaround: systems L1 copied `app/` into the scratchpad and wrapped each of its four C++ problems as a sample drill there (starters pass 4 of 10, 5 of 15, 5 of 10 and 4 of 10).
- [Could] `derive` number formatting: no function prints a whole number with thousands separators or in hex, so the l3 explore caption reads "Address 10876: page 2, offset 2684, physical 39548" while the prose writes 10,876 and 0x2A7C. Wanted: `commas(v)` and `hex(v, digits)` in the derive function set. Workaround: plain digits in the caption.
- [Could] `numeric` and `table` cells take decimals only, so address answers are typed in decimal (0x8B10 must be entered as 35,600). Wanted: an opt-in `"hex": true` that also accepts `0x...` input. Workaround: hex in the statement, decimal in every answer, as the brief says.
- [Could] Control readouts: a `scale: "log"` control over nanoseconds (l1 explore, 0.1 to 100,000,000 ns) shows the raw number, not "10 ms". `player.js` already reads `stopLabels` beside `stops` (inbox garch 1), but AUTHORING does not document it, so it was not used. Wanted: document `stopLabels` (or a unit format for log controls). Workaround: the caption counts powers of ten from 1 ns.
- [Could] Playthrough, homework parts check (`partsHomeworkChecks`): it finds the first item with three or more non-code parts but then answers from the first item of the paper and expects that item at screen 0, so it fails ("the item should show its last part (screen 1, part 2)", then a `filter` TypeError) whenever the parts item is not the first item. Wanted: step to item k before answering its parts. Workaround: systems homework-1 puts its four-part item first (the code item is second).

Raised later (research-int L1, 4 Nov):
- Validator, `table` widget: a cell's `wrong[]` ids are returned as keys by `js/engine/widgets/table.js` (AUTHORING section 3, `table`), but `tools/validate.mjs` adds only cell ids to the producible keys, so `feedback.byKey.<wrong id>` is an error ("the widget cannot return the key"). Needed on research-int l2 screen 2 (the product 0.49 typed as the floor). Workaround: no cell `wrong`; the `byKey` line sits on the cell id `r0c1` and names the likely slip generally.
- Playthrough, homework with `parts`: `partsHomeworkChecks` in `tools/playthrough.mjs` assumes the first item with three or more parts is item 1 (it expects `screenIndex === 0` and reads `ZQHomework.handles()[0]`), so a homework whose parts item is elsewhere fails with "the item should show its last part" and a TypeError. Workaround: research-int `homework-1` puts its parts item first.
- `plane` with `equal: true` and a square range (`x` and `y` both `[-1, 1]`, `height` 300) drew at about twice the usual type size at 540 px, with axis labels over the tick labels. Needed on research-int l2 (a correlation square). Workaround: no `equal`.
- `scatter` `state.lines` takes `m` and `b` only, so the vertical regression line of $x$ on $y$ when $r = 0$ cannot be drawn as a line. Wanted: a vertical line option (`x: 3`) or `vlines` on scatter. Workaround: a parametric curve `{ x: "3", y: "t" }` in `curves` (research-int l1 screen 5).
- `bars` with `bins` and a `sim`: no vertical reference line at a value on the binned axis (the exact mean 9, or 2.5) beside the dashed `showMean`; `cut` draws one but recolours the bars. Wanted: `vlines` on bars, so a sim can show where it converges. Workaround: the exact value in the explanation, and a numeric tolerance of 0.1 so a 1,000-trial reading passes (research-int l3 screens 1 and 6).
- Test hook: there is no way to set a chosen, off-target value on a widget (`ZQ.test.answerWith(value)`), so a brief's tolerance check ("c = 0.285 passes, c = 0.2 fails") needs a custom Playwright probe pressing arrow keys on the handle. Wanted: `answerWith` for drag-point, numeric and table widgets, used by `playthrough.mjs --why` too.

Raised later (coding-int L2, 4 Nov):
- [Should] Validator, `table` widget: a cell's `wrong: [{ id, value }]` entry is returned as the key by `js/engine/widgets/table.js`, but `tools/validate.mjs` refuses that id as a `feedback.byKey` key ("the widget cannot return the key"). Needed: named slips on table cells (l6 screen 10 wanted `distinct` for counting `aa` once). Workaround: `byKey` on the cell ids (`r0c1`, `r1c1`) with a line that covers the likely slip.
- [Should] `array` canvas in `mode: "stack"` inside a side-by-side `pair` draws its cells at about 20 px with unreadable text, because each side scales the full-width drawing down. Needed: two stacks (numbers and operators) side by side for the l7 parser. Workaround: horizontal arrays with a `top` pointer in a `pair` with `stack: true`.
- [Could] `array` canvas with `cells: []` draws nothing at all, not even the stack outline or an empty-queue marker, so an empty stack at the start of a `manipulate` run is blank space. Workaround: the caption says "Numbers: empty".
- [Could] `orderbook`: a level's third-entry label (`[100, 7, "#1 #2"]`) is drawn in the bid-size column even on the ask side, and the walk's "bought n" text replaces it as soon as an `order` is set, so the order ids vanish when the l10 slider moves. Workaround: the prose names the orders, and the hook pairs the book with an `array` queue.
- [Should] Playthrough: `--lesson` and `--set` check that each code solution passes but never that the starter fails; only `--drill` does. Needed: every level brief requires failing starters. Workaround: a scratchpad Playwright script that sets each starter through `ZQ.test.step().widget` (and `ZQHomework.handles()[j].step.widget`) and grades it. A `--starters` flag would make this a standard check.


Raised later (research-int L2, 4 Nov):
- [Should] `validate.mjs`, `table` widget: a cell's `wrong: [{ "id", "value", "tol" }]` id is still refused as a `byKey` key ("the widget cannot return the key"), though `js/engine/widgets/table.js` returns it (seen again after coding-int L1 and systems L1). Needed in l6 screen 4 to name "you left out the noise, 0.09". Workaround: no cell `wrong[]`; the generic line names the noise.
- [Could] Playthrough, `partsHomeworkChecks`: it finds the first item with three or more non-code parts, then expects it at screen 0 and reads `ZQHomework.handles()[0]`, so a homework whose parts item is not item 1 fails ("the item should show its last part (screen 2, part undefined)" and a `filter` TypeError). Seen again after systems L1. Workaround: H2.4 moved to item 1 of `homework-2`.
- [Could] Playthrough, starters: `--lesson` and `--set` never run a `code` widget's `starter`, so "the starter fails" is checked by hand (seen again). Workaround: a Node script that loads Pyodide 0.27.7 from the local CDN, copies the `data` files into a fresh directory, and runs each starter and solution against the hidden tests.
- [Could] `scatter` canvas has no `equal: true` (square units, as `plane` has). l9's trap draws PC1 and PC2 at slopes 1 and −1, which only look perpendicular with equal units. Workaround: symmetric ranges and a caption; the lines still look skewed at 540 px.
- [Could] Validator ladder warning: "a ladder screen usually reuses the previous canvas kind" fires on research-int's closing "The follow-up" ladder whenever the transfer before it has another canvas or none (a code screen). The brief makes that screen a ladder by design. Wanted: no warning when the previous screen is a `transfer` (or has no canvas). Workaround: two warnings left (l6, l10).
- [Could] Docs: `derive` targets on grouped `bars` (`"values.ridge.x1": "3 / (1 + lam)"`) work through the generic state path, but neither AUTHORING section 4 nor README-B "bars" says so. Wanted: one line and an example under bars `groups`. Used in l7's hook.

Raised later (systems L2, 4 Nov):
- [Should] `interleave` canvas, `wait`: a woken lane does not take its lock back (engine report deviation 4), so the condition-variable fix in l10 screen 3 cannot show the real `cv.wait`, which re-locks `m` before it returns. Wanted: an opt-in `"retake": true` on a `wait` with `lock`, so the woken lane next blocks on that lock like any `lock` step, and the enumeration counts that. Workaround: the l10 prose says the real wait takes `m` back and the canvas leaves that step out; the count (5 schedules, none asleep) is the canvas's.
- [Should] `interleave` canvas, `notify`: every notify wakes every sleeper, so there is no `notify_one`, and the two-consumer "if or while" case (one item, two waiters) cannot be stepped. Wanted: a `notify-one` op (wakes the earliest sleeper) beside `notify`. Workaround: l10 screen 4 shows that case as a `console` listing beside an `order` widget, and screen 3 says the canvas's notify acts as `notify_all`.
- [Could] `interleave` canvas, weak memory: no store buffer (by design), so the (0, 0) result of the store-buffering test is shown only in a `console` listing with a `choice` (l9 screen 3, review-2 item 7, homework-2 item 6 part b). Wanted, if ever: a `relaxed` option where a store sits in a per-lane buffer until a "drain" step. Workaround as stated.
- [Could] `interleave` canvas at 390 px: with three lanes, a step id with a hyphen (`t1-3`, from a lane id ending in a digit) breaks across two lines ("t1-" then "3 unlock B"). Wanted: no line break inside a step id (`white-space: nowrap` on the id). Workaround: the l8 ring uses lanes `p`, `q`, `r` labelled P, Q and R (ids `p1`, `q3`).
- [Could] `interleave` canvas at 390 px with two lanes: the order badge of a done step sits over the end of a long step text ("store total from" with badge 3 on the last word, l6 screen 2). Wanted: right padding on the step text equal to the badge. Workaround: none needed; the text stays readable.
- [Should] `validate.mjs`, `generalise`: a fenced code block states the rule only in a course on the code track or a lesson with a `code` widget, so `systems` lessons without code (l6, l9, l10; topic 3.5 is on the speed track) need a KaTeX line even when the rule is a C++ pattern. Wanted: count a lesson whose course has any C++ `code` widget, or a `"codeCourse": true` course flag. Workaround: each of those generalise screens has a short display formula as well as the code or table.
- [Could] Playthrough `--shots` on `interleave` screens shows only the opening state and the first viewport. Wanted: a shot after `finish()` and of the Why? sheet, as engine follow-up 4 suggested. Workaround: a scratch Playwright script (`systems-2/shots.mjs`, light and dark at 390 px, stepping each lane to the end and opening the explanation), and `probe.mjs` reading every derive caption before and after a schedule.
- [Could] Seconds two open bullets: the playthrough's homework parts check still needs the parts item first (homework-2 puts its four-part item first and the code item second, against the brief's numbering), and lesson and set code widgets still have no starter check (systems L2 adapted the coding-int L1 `codecheck.mjs`: starters pass 2 of 7, 6 of 12 and 2 of 7).

Raised later (systems level 3):
- Playthrough `partsHomeworkChecks` counts a `table` part as answered even when no cell is filled (the draft keeps an array of empty strings), so a parts item whose last part is a `table` fails with "kept 4 open parts and 4 answers, expected 4 and 3". Wanted: count a part as answered only when a cell has text. Workaround: homework-3 item 1 ends with a `numeric` part.
- `frames` control on an `array` canvas (l14 byte order) shows only the current frame's label at the right end of the slider, with no chip for the other frame, so two frames read as a one-sided slider. Wanted: label both ends, or let the `options` chips drive `frames`. Workaround: the prose names both orders and the question asks about the second.

Raised later (research-int level 3):

- [Should] `numeric` with `unit: "%"` compares fractions: the key, `tol` and every `wrong[]` value must be written as fractions (0.0458 for 4.58%) while the learner types 4.58 and `accept` reads in percent. A key written in percent (58.0) is silently accepted only as 5800, and the playthrough typed 5800. Wanted: a validator warning when a `%` numeric has an `answer` above 1, or an `answerPercent` field. Workaround: the level builder scaled every `%` numeric by 0.01; found only because the playthrough typed 5800 into one.
- [Could] `bars` with `bins` and `data`: `state.cut` draws one cut only, so a 95% bootstrap interval shows one tail (l15 screens 0 and 1). Wanted: `cut` as a list of two cuts, or an `interval: [lo, hi]` that recolours the middle bars.
