Workaround: the hook shows no cut and its explanation patch shows the lower tail; the next screen shows the upper tail.
- [Could] `code` widget `prelude`: names it defines (here `X_train`, `X_test`, `y_train`, `y_test`, `FEATURES` on the five l14 screens) are invisible to the learner, who sees only the starter and the Data files panel. Wanted: a read-only "Ready for you" panel under the editor listing names and their first values. Workaround: a two-line comment at the top of every starter naming the variables.
- [Could] Playthrough: lesson, review and homework `code` widgets are checked only with the model solution, never with the starter against the hidden tests (still open from systems L2). Wanted: `--starters` that fails a widget whose untouched starter passes every test. Workaround: a Pyodide harness (`docs/briefs/research-int-scripts/l3_codecheck.mjs`) that runs each solution against its tests and examples and each starter against its tests; no starter passed a test except the input-unchanged test of H3.2 (1 of 8).
Raised later (coding-int level 3):
- [Could] `array` canvas in `mode: "linked"` draws one arrow per cell (next only) and a chain that is always consistent, so a doubly linked list with `prev` pointers, or the half-unlinked state after one assignment (the dangling `prev` of l11 screen 2), cannot be drawn. Wanted: optional `prev` arrows and a per-link patch (`links: { "c2": { "next": "c4" } }`) so a pointer can be rewired and shown stale. Workaround: a `frames` slider over `cells` and `highlight` with the state in the caption.
- [Should] Playthrough, `partsHomeworkChecks`: it still finds the first item with three or more parts but expects it at screen 0 and reads `ZQHomework.handles()[0]`, so a homework whose parts item is not item 1 fails (raised in coding-int L2, research-int L1 and L2, systems L1 and L2). Workaround: `homework-3` puts its three-part ledger item first, against the brief's numbering (item 5).
- [Should] Playthrough: `--lesson` and `--set` never run a code widget's starter, so "the starter fails" is checked by hand (raised again). Workaround: `coding-int-3/starters.mjs`, a Playwright script that sets each starter and solution through `ZQ.test.step().widget` and `ZQHomework.handles()[j].step.widget` and grades both (starters pass 2 of 8, 1 of 7, 0 of 6, 1 of 5, 5 of 6, 1 of 3, 1 of 7, 8 of 11, 0 of 6, 1 of 6, 1 of 7 and 1 of 5). A `--starters` flag would make it standard.
- [Could] Validator ladder warning, "a ladder screen usually reuses the previous canvas kind", fires when the screen before the ladder is a code screen or a trap with a `console` and the ladder has an `array`, a `tree` or a `table` (l11 screen 8, l12 screen 7, l14 screen 6). The briefs set these screens by design. Wanted: no warning when the previous screen is a `problem`, `trap` or `transfer` (raised in research-int too). Workaround: three warnings left.
Raised later (exam mock-oa-code):
- `code` item in an exam: needs a share-of-tests credit (partial marks by passed hidden tests). A reported platform scores by test group, which l15 and l17 teach as a planning rule. Workaround: one problem is right only when every test passes, and the description says so.
- `draw` from `drills/<pack>`: needs a `language` filter, so a mixed Python and C++ pack can feed a Python-only mock. Workaround: the `oa` pack is all Python.
- Drill library: `drills/oa` is listed on `#drill`, so a learner may meet a pool problem there before the mock. No workaround; the description says the pool is in the library.
- `draw` unseen-first is applied inside one family only, and the families here are one drill each, so a resit may draw a problem seen last time while others are unseen. Needs unseen-first across the whole pool when families are singletons.
Raised later (exam mock-superday-research):
- [Should] The bank holds no statistics, machine learning or open-case families (research banks 1 to 4 are not written), so only paper 1's four probability questions are drawn; the other 14 questions repeat on a resit.
- [Could] The validator warns "more than 4 tags" on a `draw` entry's `tags`, a rule written for item tags; a draw filter of 16 families is legitimate and the warning cannot be cleared.
- [Could] The playthrough names exam screenshots by screen index only, so each paper overwrites the one before and only the last paper's items survive; it also takes no shot of the start card and shoots the results page mid-animation (13 of 18 and no stamp at 1280 px).
- [Could] The exam playthrough never runs a code starter against its hidden tests, so "the starter fails" is not checked for mock code items (it is for drills); the author ran the starters in Pyodide 0.27.7 by hand.
- [Could] A drawn bank item carries its own course skills (for example `pz-conditioning`), which appear on the results page's By skill chart under names the mock's `skills` map does not define.

Raised later (exam mock-oa-quant-data):
- [Should] There are no statistics or applied-maths bank families (only probability, brain-teasers, mental maths, market making and behavioural). Paper 1 of `mock-oa-quant-data` is fixed, so a resit repeats all ten questions; bank families for statistics, linear algebra and machine learning would let it draw.
- [Could] `draw` has no `language` filter. A mixed Python and C++ drill pack cannot feed a Python-only mock (a data task needs Python and a data file); a `language` field on the entry would fix it.
- [Could] A code item scores per task, so a learner who passes 8 of 9 hidden tests gets no credit. A share-of-tests option with a pass mark would suit data tasks.
- [Could] The generator `gen_qd_data.py` is meant to live at `app/tools/gen-qd-data.py`, but content agents may not edit `app/tools/`. An engine agent should copy it there if it is wanted.
Raised later (systems level 4):
- `bars` canvas with `xAxis: "value"` and `showMean`, two far-apart categories (10 and 1,000): the bar at 10 draws about one pixel wide at 390 px and the weighted-mean label collides with its value label (l17 hook, explore, trap). Wanted: a minimum bar width on a value axis, or a log value axis, and the mean label offset from the value labels. Workaround: value labels hidden (`showValues: false`) and the counts stated in the prose.
- `bars` canvas: no percentile marker (a line at rank ceil(p n / 100) in a sorted sample), so the p99 of the l17 explore screen is shown only in a `derive` caption. Wanted: `state.percentile` drawing the marker on a histogram. Workaround: caption text computed by a ternary.
- `graph` canvas at 390 px: three pill nodes in a row whose labels pass about 70 px touch or overlap (l18 pipeline, first try with "card to ring", "send order"). Wanted: pill nodes that wrap or shrink to fit the row. Workaround: one-word labels on the nodes and the long names on the slider frames.
- `grid` canvas with text cells (l16 cache line, 3 rows of 8 words): at 390 px the cell text is about 9 px and four-letter words ("mask", "head") are close to the edge. Wanted: a larger text minimum or fewer, wider cells on a phone. Workaround: short words only.

Raised later (research-int level 4):

- [Should] `series` canvas: a known point whose neighbours are `null` gaps draws no mark, so a series of 100, null, null, 112 shows only the label dot of the last value (l16 screens 0 and 5). Wanted: a `points: true` option that draws every non-null value as a dot. Workaround: `markers` on the known indices.
- [Could] `bars` canvas thins category labels when there are 12 named categories (every second town was shown, desktop and 390 px), and a negative bar of -0.0005 prints as 0.000 at three places, so "how many are negative" had no honest chart. Wanted: `labelEvery: 1` (rotated or abbreviated) and per-category decimals. Workaround: a two-column `table` canvas of 12 slopes to four places (l17 screen 2).
- [Could] Drill hidden tests have no `setup` line (AUTHORING section 5), so a stateful class such as `OnlineOLS` needs hidden helper functions in `prelude` (`_run_seq`, `_run_random`) that the learner never sees. Wanted: `setup` on drill tests as on `code` widgets, or a "Ready for you" panel listing prelude names. Workaround: helpers named with a leading underscore, and the names `DF`, `FRAME` and `ORDERS` stated in the brief or prose.
- [Could] `generalise` screens must state the rule in KaTeX or a fenced block (validator), but the "Say it aloud" screens of this course teach a spoken answer, not a formula. Wanted: a `generalise` that accepts a `choice` of spoken answers with no formula. Workaround: a short formula in the prose of l19 and l20 (the VWAP and the interval ends).
- [Could] `bars` with `bins` and `data`: `state.cut` marks one tail only, so the l20 bootstrap histogram shades the upper end while the question reads the interval from both ends (still open from research-int level 3). Wanted: a two-cut interval. Workaround: the prose gives both percentiles.
- [Could] A lesson cannot link to a drill: l19 names `rq-clean-frame-py` in prose for the stopwatch run, and the learner must open the Drills list and find it. Wanted: a `{{drill:rq-clean-frame-py}}` link, or a `drill` field on a screen that renders a button to `#drill/<id>`. Workaround: the id in code format in the prose.
- [Could] Python drills: the drill harness compares with a fixed relative 1e-9 and ignores a test `tol`, and it has no `compare: "raises"` (the Python code widget has both). `rq-online-ols-py` first failed 4 of 7 on a `tol` of 1e-6 and `rq-interp-py` 12 of 13 on a `raises` case. Wanted: `tol` and `raises` on Python drill tests, as on C++ drills. Workaround: the prelude helper rounds to six places, and the "nothing left" case became a missing y at the left end.
Raised later (coding-int level 4):
- [Should] Lesson prose has no link markup, so "link to `#exam/mock-oa-code`" (l15, l17) and "go to `#drill/session`" (l17, l19) are the route written in inline code, which the learner must find on Review or Drill by hand. Wanted: an inline link in prose (`[text](#exam/mock-oa-code)`) or a screen field `link: { route, label }` drawn as a button after the task. Workaround: the route in backticks in the explanation.
- [Should] Drill session and `#drill` filters take one pattern (a select), so "pattern `prefix-sums` or `hashing`" cannot be one filter, and `pickTwo` needs two drills that pass it. Wanted: a multi-pattern filter (chips) or a pattern group such as "hashing and prefix sums". Workaround: ten `ci-` drills authored so each of `prefix-sums` and `hashing` has two drills in Python and two in C++ at difficulty 3 to 4 (six more than the brief named), plus one `intervals` drill in each language.
- [Should] C++ cases are compiled with the `call` inlined in one unit, so a quadratic starter given a constant argument is optimised to linear: `count_subarrays_divisible_by_k(std::vector<int>(400000, 3), 1)` ran at once (`total % 1` is always 0) and passed the case the brief says stops a slow starter. Wanted: an `opaque: true` widget flag (or arguments read through a `volatile`) so every case sees runtime values. Workaround: the large case uses `k = 3`, which clang cannot fold, and the expected count is the same 80,000,200,000.
- [Could] `table` widget: a categorical answer (Yes or No, full, partial or skip) is a typed text cell, graded with `accept` spellings, and at 390 px four editable columns overflow when a header is long. Wanted: a cell kind `{ "select": ["Yes", "No"], "answer": "Yes" }`. Workaround: `accept: ["y"]`, and "Group 1" to "Group 3" as headers with the sizes in a console above.
- [Could] Playthrough: a `code` widget that is a part of an E11 item (homework item 1, parts 1 and 3) is not reachable by the starter check, and the check is still manual (raised again). Wanted: `--starters` that walks `parts[].widget` too, running the starter against the cumulative tests. Workaround: `coding-int-4/starters.mjs` for lessons and plain `code` items, and a native Python run for the two Leaderboard starters (fail 3 of 4 of their own rows each, and 3 of the 8 merged rows for part 3).

Raised later (exam topic-3.5):
- Validator, `table` widget: a cell's `wrong: [{ id, text }]` is graded and returns its id as the key (engine pass 7), but `validate.mjs` registers only `r{i}c{j}` keys, so a `byKey` line for a custom wrong id is an error. Workaround: `byKey` on the cell keys `r0c1` and so on, one line per cell.
- Playthrough: it runs each code widget's `solution` but never the starter, so "the starter fails" cannot be confirmed in the tool. Workaround: a Pyodide script for Python, and `g++` with sanitisers for C++, kept in `docs/briefs/exam-3.5-scripts/`. A `--starters` check would help.
- `code` editor: long lines (starter comments, 60 or more characters) scroll sideways inside the editor at 390 px and at 1280 px beside the question map. Workaround: starter comments kept short. Soft wrap for comments would help.

Raised by the coding-int fix pass (topic 3.5):
- Player: no per-screen language skip, so a learner who does not write C++ cannot skip a `ladder` C++ screen in l1, l4, l10, l11, l16 or l17. A `optional` or `language` flag on a screen would do.
- `orderbook` canvas: labels shrink with the SVG at 390 px (about 7 px in `coding-int` l10 screens[1]). A `labelSize` or `minWidth` parameter under 480 px would fix it.

Raised later (orchestrator, session H, 4 Nov):
- [Should] Mock draw pools are open to practice: the eight `oa` drills behind mock-oa-code are listed in the drill library, so a learner can solve them before the mock. Options: a drill-file flag that hides a pack from the library until its mock is sat once, or a larger pool with `language` filtering and unseen-first draws by solved state (bullets above). Raised by the exam author and the topic 3.5 exams second read.
- [Should] Playthrough: run each code widget's starter and require it to fail (every topic 3.5 author confirmed this by hand script), and run the code tests inside homework `parts` items (systems homework-4 histogram tests were not run).
- [Done, item 500] The `rq-clean-frame-py` and `rq-group-slopes-py` failures were not a flake: the drill runner counted package loading against the 5 s limit.

Raised later (topic 3.2 levels, 4 Nov), measure-prob level 1:
- [Could] `series` control on `revealAt` reads out the 0-based index and `indexFrom` only relabels the axis, so a slider over steps n = 1 to 100 cannot show n. Wanted: an offset or `format` on the readout. Workaround: a control on `reveal` shown as a percentage (l3 screen 2).
- [Could] `number-line` interval labels collide when rows are stacked tightly (the nested covers of l2 screen 8 and l3 screen 7 at 390 px). Wanted: label thinning per row or `labelPos: "right"` for rows above 0. Workaround: labels dropped and the rows explained in the prose.

Raised later (cpp L5 brief, 5 Nov):
- [Must] (numbered 518) C++ `code` harness: make a use of freed memory observable. The app's allocator (wasi dlmalloc) leaves freed blocks readable and frees a block twice without complaint; `leakCheck` counts only blocks never freed or freed with the wrong form, and its own `put` reads the header of a block that may already be freed. So a test cannot see a delete-first assignment (`delete[] data_; data_ = new double[n]; copy from other`, which misbehaves only for `a = a`), and the commonest move slip (the pointer left set, so two objects `delete[]` one block) passes unless the test adds an accessor. Wanted, in `cpp-grade.js` for `allocCheck` and `leakCheck` units: on `delete`, fill the block with a poison byte (0xDD) before it is released and keep it out of reuse until the unit ends (a quarantine), so a read of a freed block returns poison deterministically; and have `leakCheck` report a second `delete` of the same block as "Memory error: a block was deleted twice" (exit status 1). Needed by: cpp L5 P-copy-samples (the `delete-first-no-guard` slip is listed as not caught and the problem is marked as waiting on this) and, as a second line of defence, P-move-buffer, P-vec and P-unique-ptr. Workaround today: the problems test observable state instead (`data() == nullptr`, `Counted::bad`), which covers every other named slip.
- [Should] (numbered 519) C++ `allocCheck`: also expose `free_count()` (and optionally `live_blocks()`), so a test can check that an assignment frees exactly the old block. Not needed by the cpp L5 problems once the [Must] above lands.
- [Should] (numbered 520) `array` canvas: document `state.highlight.ranges` (a list of `{ range, label, colour }`) in `app/js/engine/canvas/README-B.md`; the field exists in `array.js` and cpp L5 l21 S3 wants three labelled byte ranges (`vptr`, `x`, `y`).

- [Could] `plane` curve formulas have no conditional, so one curve whose closed form changes with a slider (under-, critically and over-damped $y'' + by' + 4y = 0$) needs `exprs` with `index` set by a `derive` rule (`"params.k": "abs(b - 4) < 0.001 ? 1 : (b < 4 ? 0 : 2)"`). Worked in odes l9 screens 1 to 4. Wanted: a `trajectory` for second-order equations in time (a field in $(t, y, y')$ plotted as $y$ against $t$), or `if(c, a, b)` in curve formulas.

Raised later (odes level 3):
- [Could] Validator ladder rule (`tools/validate.mjs`, the "reuses the previous canvas kind" warning): `matrix` is not among the views that pair naturally, so a ladder that moves from a `matrix` (eigenvectors, constants) to a `plane` of the same solution warns. Workaround in odes l10: screens 4 and 6 are `problem` screens, leaving one ladder. Wanted: `matrix` beside `plane`, `table` and `vectors` counted as natural.
- [Should] `tools/o2-rules.mjs` does not scan `widget.how`, `summary.ideas`, `order` line text or longform `model`, while `docs/reviews/voice-scan.py` does: each odes level had 4 to 8 sentences over 20 words there that the validator passed. Workaround: ran the voice scan per level and split them. Wanted: the O2 long-sentence rule over the same fields as the voice scan.
Raised later (complex level 1):
- [Should] Validator refuses a `table` widget cell's `wrong[]` id as a `feedback.byKey` key ("the widget cannot return the key"), though `widgets/table.js` returns that id as the key and AUTHORING section 3 documents it. Wanted: add each cell `wrong[].id` to the table widget's keys in `validate.mjs`. Workaround: complex l2 screen 6 keys its slip lines by cell id (`r2c1`, `r2c2`) and drops the named wrong entries.
- [Should] `order` widget at 440 px: one long inline formula widens every line past the column, so all lines are clipped on the right (text and the leave-out cross hidden). The sideways-scroll check passes because the overflow is hidden. Wanted: wrap or scale wide maths inside an order line, and a playthrough check for clipped lines. Workaround: long formulas split into separate `$...$` pieces and `\tfrac` (complex l2 screen 8, l3 screen 2, homework-1 item 5).
- [Could] `expression` has no `tol`: complex answers are compared at relative 1e-9, so a learner who types the decimals a canvas caption shows (0.5981 + 4.9641i for 3 + 4i turned 30 degrees) is marked wrong. Wanted: an optional `tol` for numeric-only expression answers. Workaround: the question asks for the exact value and accepts the product $(3 + 4i)(\frac{\sqrt3}{2} + \frac12 i)$.
- [Could] `vectors` with `state.transform`: a `fixed: true` vector and its turned copy share a label position at angle 0, so the two labels overlap. Wanted: offset coincident labels. Workaround: complex l3 screen 5 opens at 10 degrees.
Raised later (complex level 2):
- [Should] `complex-map`: `hide` on a curve hides it in both panels, so a "predict the image" screen cannot show a shape in the z-plane while its image stays hidden. Wanted: hide the image only (`hide: ["<id>-image"]` for curves as for points). Workaround: complex l5 screen 5 asks the prediction on a `plane` with the unit square as a polygon, and screen 6 confirms on the `complex-map`.
- [Should] `complex-map`: a draggable point cannot be held to a curve (no `on`, as `plane` points have), so "drag $z$ once round the unit circle" leaves the learner to stay on the circle by hand (l5 screen 1, the direction dials of l6 screens 1, 2, 6 and 7). Wanted: `on: "unit"` or `on: "<curve id>"` on a complex-map point.
- [Could] `complex-map`: no marker can be placed in the w-plane alone, so a target such as $-3 + 4i$ cannot be drawn for the learner to steer $f(z)$ onto (l5 screen 4). Workaround: the caption prints $f(z)$ and the prose names the target.
- [Could] `plane` curve formulas have no `atan2` or `arg`, so a principal argument along a path is written as `x - 2*pi*floor((x + pi)/(2*pi))` (l8 screen 10). Wanted: `atan2` in curve formulas, as derive has.
Raised later (numerics level 1):
- [Should] `tools/validate.mjs` (table widget case): a cell's own `wrong: [{ id, value }]` id is refused as a `feedback.byKey` key ("the widget cannot return the key"), though AUTHORING section 3 says the key is that id. Workaround: the slip is named in the cell-id line (`r2c2`) instead (numerics l1 screens 4 and 5). Wanted: the validator adds cell `wrong` ids to the key set.
- [Should] `plane` with `xScale: "log"` and `yScale: "log"` over many decades (x from 1 to 1e20, y from 1e-16 to 1e5): x tick labels print every digit ("10,000,000,000") and overlap, y ticks below 1e-6 print "0", and points near 1e-14 are not drawn. Wanted: power-of-ten tick labels ($10^{6}$) and thinning on wide log ranges. Workaround: plot log10 values on linear axes with axis titles "log10 of ..." (l1 spacing staircase and timestamp gap, l2 V curve, l4 term sizes).
- [Could] `derive` captions have `fixed` and `money` but no scientific format, so a live caption reads "10^−14.4" in plain text (l2 explore). Wanted: `sci(v, digits)` giving "4.3 × 10⁻¹⁵". Workaround: the caption prints the log10 exponent and a digit count.
- [Could] Code widget: packages load from the learner's code imports only, so a hidden test whose `setup` imports NumPy fails with ModuleNotFoundError when the learner's code does not import it (homework-1 float32 pennies). Wanted: scan `setup`, `prelude` and test `call` text for imports too. Workaround: `import numpy as np` in the starter, with a comment saying the tests pass float32 values.
Raised later (numerics level 2):
- [Could] `docs/reviews/voice-scan.py` reads canvas `derive` strings (`let` formulas, targets) as learner prose, so a long formula such as l8's `solve(A, [...])` counts as a sentence of 25 words. Wanted: skip `derive` (except `caption`) in the scan, as the validator's O2 rules already do. Workaround: the l8 formulas are written without spaces.
- [Could] `plane` live spline (l8): a `derive` with `let` and `solve(A, rhs)` writing `params.M1`..`M8` from eight dragged points works, but it needs seven `functions` entries with hand-built formulas and a 6 by 6 matrix in `params`. Wanted: a `spline: { points: [ids], bc: "natural" }` function entry, reusable for yield curves and smiles. Workaround: the generator writes the seven piece formulas.
Raised later (numerics level 3):
- [Could] `plane` `implicit` curves take real formulas only, so the RK4 stability boundary |1 + z + z²/2 + z³/6 + z⁴/24| = 1 had to be expanded by hand into real and imaginary parts in x and y (l12). Wanted: a complex level-set entry, for example `{ "modulus": "1 + z + z^2/2 + z^3/6 + z^4/24", "level": 1 }`, which would also serve Crank-Nicolson and theta-method regions in level 4. Workaround: a script expanded the polynomial.
- [Could] Playthrough and code widget: no record of a heavy package's first-load time per item, which every SciPy `how` is asked to state. Wanted: the playthrough prints load and import seconds for each code item that imports SciPy or pandas. Workaround: measured in node Pyodide (2.1 s for `import scipy.integrate` after the wheels were fetched) and the `how` says "about 2 s once downloaded".
Raised by the main session (session S, protocol 6, third sighting):
- [Must] Author number-recompute scripts do not read the lesson JSON (second reads of odes L1 to L3, complex L1 and L2, numerics L1 to L3), so stated numbers in prose, `wrong[]` values, `why` and `explain` texts go unchecked; most errors found sat there. Wanted: a shared helper (`app/tools/numbers-lib.py` or similar) that loads a level's files and asserts each recomputed value appears verbatim where the script names it (file, path), and a validator warning when a level's script names no JSON path. LEVEL-BRIEF then requires its use.
- [Should] (session S) Six new level reviews (odes 1 to 3, numerics 1 to 3) were committed without the per-level `pass` share that item 4 put in every review; the validator does not require it. Wanted: a validator error on a level review with no `pass`, and LEVEL-BRIEF names it. Fixed by `write-review-pass.mjs` this session.
- [Should] (session S) Four `--all --shard i/4` runs started in the same second race for the "free port": shard 1's static server died with `OSError: [Errno 98] Address already in use` and the run exited 2 before any item. Wanted: the playthrough retries the server start on a new port (or binds port 0 and reads the port back). Workaround: stagger shard starts by a few seconds.
Raised later (odes level 4):
- [Should] `plane` `trajectory` integrates by RK4 only and `derive` has no integrator, so an Euler polygon for a nonlinear equation or a system cannot follow a slider on $h$ (l16 screen 5 and l17 screen 5, the centre $x' = y$, $y' = -x$). Workaround: precomputed 64-point `points` polylines for $h = 0.1$, Euler beside RK4, no step slider; linear equations use the closed-form polygon expression with a slider. Wanted: `method: "euler" | "rk4"` with an explicit `step` on `trajectory`, so a step control redraws the broken line.
- [Should] Validator refuses a `table` widget cell's `wrong[].id` as a `feedback.byKey` key, though the engine returns it (fourth sighting after odes L1, complex L1, numerics L1). l14 screen 4 keys the lines on cell ids `r1c2` and `r2c2` instead.
- [Should] `plane` with `yScale: "log"` labels every tick below $10^{-6}$ as "0" (l17 screens 3 and 4, errors from $10^{-10}$ to 0.1; second sighting after numerics L1). Workaround: plot $\log_{10}$ of the error on a linear axis with `yTicks: "integer"` and `xAxis: "bottom"`.
- [Could] `plane` function colours: a function without `colour` takes slot "list position + 1", so an authored `colour: 2` on the first curve collides with the default of the second (l16, l17: exact and Euler both orange until every curve was given a colour). Wanted: default slots that skip slots already taken.
- [Could] `app/tools/numbers_lib.py`: `expect` compares a rounded float, so a figure printed half-up from an exact half (5.53125% shown as 5.5313%) needs a hand override; the L4 script adds `show=` and `tol=` to its own `stated()` helper. Wanted: `lv.expect(ref, path, value, dp=4)` that rounds half-up and accepts either neighbour of an exact half.
- [Could] Playthrough has no per-item package-load time (second sighting after numerics L3), which every SciPy `how` must state. Workaround: a Node script loading Pyodide 0.27.7 from the local CDN folder timed NumPy at 0.6 s and SciPy at 1.5 s; the `how` lines say "about 2 seconds from a local cache here; expect 5 to 20 seconds over a home connection".
Raised later (numerics level 4):
- [Should] Validator, `table` widget: a cell's own `wrong[]` ids (documented in AUTHORING section 3, `table`) are refused as `feedback.byKey` keys ("the widget cannot return the key"), so a cell slip such as dropping the ½ in a_j cannot get its own line (l13 screen 3). Wanted: the validator adds each editable cell's `wrong[].id` to the keys. Workaround: the slips are named in `feedback.generic`.
- [Could] No tool prints what a seeded canvas shows. The `walk` and `series` streams are mulberry32 with Box-Muller, not NumPy, so an author who states a caption figure (l15's standard error of the walk's stopped payoff) must copy walk.js's draw order into Python. Wanted: `node tools/canvas-values.mjs --lesson <course>/<id> --screen <n>` printing the canvas's `values()` after `enter`. Workaround: a Python copy of the stream in `docs/reports/numerics-L4-numbers.py`, and a wide tolerance on the explore answer.
- [Could] `plane` function labels: three curves that start together (amplification factors equal 1 at ω = 0) get labels that sit nearer the wrong curve at some slider values (l14 screen 0 at ν = 0.25). Wanted: labels re-placed after each control change, or `legend: true`. Workaround: the explore opens at ν = 0.6, where the curves have separated, and the caption gives each factor by name.
- [Could] numbers_lib: a figure written as `4.5 \times 10^{30}` is three tokens (4.5, 10, 30), so a computed 4.5e30 cannot be matched by value. Wanted: `normalise` reads `a \times 10^{b}` (and `10^{-b}`) as one number. Workaround: such figures are expected as literal strings and their mantissas checked separately.
- [Could] Playthrough `--shots` captures the viewport only, so the `walk` overlay and stopped-mean panels below the fold (l15 screen 0) are not in the screenshot that the brief asks authors to check at phone width. Wanted: `--fullpage`, or one extra shot scrolled to the canvas's bottom. Workaround: the panel heights were kept small (220 px walk, 70 px overlays).
Raised later (convex level 1):
- [Should] `plane` with `equal: true` on a square range (the long-only triangle in the $(w_1, w_2)$ plane) fills the 780px column on an iPad and grows to about 700px tall, with 30px tick labels. Wanted: a default height cap for `equal` planes. Workaround: `canvas.maxHeight: 330` on every plane, space and pair canvas of the level.
- [Could] `plane` `area` shades only between two `y = f(x)` curves, so a region bounded by an `implicit` or `contour` curve (the risk budget ellipse $\mathbf{w}^T\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\mathbf{w} \le 0.04$, which is tilted) cannot be filled. Wanted: `area: { inside: "<implicit id>" }` or a filled contour level. Workaround: the discs and the axis-aligned ellipse use hidden `sqrt` halves; the tilted risk ellipses are outlines only.
- [Could] `derive` captions cannot call a plane's own curve functions, so l1's live volatility caption repeats the variance formula three times by hand (one per point) and l2's chord caption repeats $f$. Wanted: `fn("f", x)` in derive evaluating a function entry by id.
- [Could] Playthrough: no per-item record of the SciPy first-load time that a `how` is asked to state (also raised for numerics level 3). The l4 `how` says "a few seconds from cache, longer on a first visit" without a measured figure.
Raised later (convex level 2):
- [Should] `table` widget: AUTHORING section 3 says a cell may carry `wrong: [{ id, value }]` and "the key is that id", but the validator errors `feedback.byKey.<id>: the widget cannot return the key` for such an id (l6 vertex table, a swapped-rates slip on the $(5, 3)$ profit cell). Wanted: the validator collects cell `wrong` ids as keys, or the doc says they are not yet keys. Workaround: the slip is named in the hint and the generic feedback.
- [Could] `plane` state patches merge `points` and `segments` by id, but a wrong answer's canvas patch had to replace the whole `polygons` list to redraw one region (l5 trap: the reversed "at least" rows flip the hedge region). Wanted: `polygons` keyed by id like the other lists. Workaround: the patch sends the full one-polygon list.
Raised later (stat-models level 1):
- [Should] Validator refuses a `table` widget cell's `wrong[]` id as a `feedback.byKey` key (second sighting after complex level 1). Workaround: keyed the slips by cell id (`r0c2`) with "If you wrote ..." lines in l1 screen 5.
- [Should] `plane` has no compact data form for many points: D1's 300 firms are 300 `points` objects per canvas, so l1 is 100 KB and l2 and l4 about 90 and 60 KB. Wanted: `state.data: { x: [...], y: [...] }` drawn as plain dots (no ids, not tappable). Workaround: point objects with `tap: false`, rounded to 3 places.
- [Should] `docs/reviews/voice-scan.py` counts canvas formula strings (a plane `contour` of 12 terms) as sentences of 36 words. Wanted: skip `expr`, `contour`, `heat`, `implicit` and derive keys. Workaround: the l2 contour formula is written without spaces.
- [Could] Playthrough prints no package load time, so the statsmodels and scikit-learn first-load figures in every `how` (20 to 40 s, from KNOWN_ISSUES) cannot be measured in the browser here (jsDelivr is blocked, the local CDN loads from disk). Measured instead with Pyodide 0.27.7 in Node from the local wheels: NumPy + SciPy + statsmodels 3.1 to 4.5 s, plus scikit-learn 7.5 s. Wanted: the playthrough reports each code screen's package load seconds.
- [Could] `pair` prose: a pair stacks on a phone, so "on the right" and "on the left" are wrong at 440 px. Wanted: an author note in AUTHORING section 4 (or `leftLabel`/`rightLabel` shown as small titles). Workaround: l2 says "the contour plot" and "the default plot".
Raised later (stat-models level 2):
- [Should] `boxes` in `place` boxes mode at 440 px: eight short cards ("HSBA am") in three containers overflow sideways and overlap until each container gets `cols: 2`, and even then the card text is about 9 px. Wanted: cards that keep a readable minimum size and wrap the containers onto rows on a phone. Workaround: `cols: 2` on every container in l8 screen 2.
- [Should] `walk` captions read from `values` (hits, `tauMean`) and the `bars` `sim` counts cannot be read by any tool, so the counts a seeded canvas shows must be read off a screenshot. Wanted: the playthrough prints `values()` of each canvas after its `explain` patch (or a `--values` flag). Workaround: temporary copies of l9 with the walk at the alternative and the sim at `ran: 1000`, screenshotted, then reverted; the sim count was confirmed by running `js/engine/sim.js` in Node.
- [Could] A control cannot carry both `stops` and `options` (validator error), so a two-state toggle of the walk's `mu` needs `frames` with `frameLabels`. Workaround used in l9 screen 9. Wanted: `options` with `values: [...]` that binds a value rather than the index.
- [Could] `distribution` overlay `label` collides with a `shade` region's `{value}` label at the right tail (l8 power canvas). Workaround: overlay label left empty, the dashed curve named in the prose.
Raised later (stat-models level 3):
- [Should] `bars` `state.cut` counts whole bins: with bins from -1.6 in steps of 0.1, a cut at 0.562 gave `beyondShare` 0.086 (bins from 0.6) where 101 of 1,000 shuffles were at or beyond 0.562. Wanted: `beyondShare` from the raw sim outcomes, or the cut snapped to a bin edge with a warning. Workaround: bins start at -1.638 so 0.562 is an edge; the axis then shows ticks such as -1.238 and 0.362.
- [Should] Sim language has no `median` or other order statistics, so the bootstrap of the median (the robust contrast to the live bootstrap of the mean) runs only in code (brief bullet "sim order statistics", second sighting). Workaround: l13 says so and leaves it to the code screen.
- [Could] A 60-term kernel curve (`curves` on `bars`, one `normpdf` per return) and a 40-term plane function work, but the formula must be generated by script and is unreadable to a second reader; redraw time on a slider move could not be measured by any tool here (brief bullet "data arrays in formulas", second sighting). Wanted: `kde(x, data, h)` in curve formulas over a `params` array.
- [Could] `scatter` `fitLabel: "auto"` prints the equation on top of the cloud at 440 px and on the desktop (l13 beta canvas). Wanted: a label position option or a legend. Workaround: none; it stays readable.
Raised later (convex level 3):
- [Should] `numbers_lib.py` `normalise` strips braces, so `\tfrac{2243}{45000}` becomes one run of digits and neither the numerator nor the denominator can be matched as a number (about 60 checks in this level failed for that reason alone). Wanted: read `\frac`, `\tfrac`, `\dfrac` as a value a/b, or keep a separator between the two groups. Workaround: `docs/reports/convex-L3-numbers.py` checks a `Fraction` as the text `frac{n}{d}` (or `frac97` for single digits).
- [Could] Validator: the warning "the number script never uses numbers_lib (Level( and expect( )" fires on `convex-L1-numbers.py` and `convex-L2-numbers.py`, which call `lv.expect` through an alias `E = lv.expect`. Wanted: match `Level(` plus `.expect` (or any `expect` reference). Workaround: the L3 script calls `lv.expect(` literally inside its helper.
- [Could] `space` surfaces with `clip: "clamp"` draw a staircase along the top of the box where the surface leaves it (l9 saddle, $z$ range $[-0.5, 5]$). Wanted: cells cut along the level line at the box edge. Workaround: the $z$ range is widened until nothing is clipped, which flattens the interesting valley.
- [Could] `space` has no way to reveal a curve or point only after Show why except `hide` plus a patch; and a `plane` trap that hides the true curve (l13, `hide: ["v"]`) must repeat `{"hide": []}` on every `wrong[]` entry and in `explain.canvas`. Wanted: a `revealAfterGrade` list on plane and space, as grid has. Workaround: the patch on each wrong entry.

Raised later (momentum-rep level 2):

- `code` widget, `equal` and `isclose` on arrays holding NaN: NaN never equals NaN, so a test of `formation_returns` (NaN before month J + skip by design) cannot compare the array as it is. Wanted: an option such as `"nan": "equal"` that treats NaN as equal to NaN in element-wise comparisons. Workaround (l7): the call wraps the result in `np.nan_to_num(..., nan=-9.0)` and the `how` says NaN shows as −9.
- `table` canvas and control `frames`: `rows` is a param, so a frame cannot swap a whole table (no skip against skip). Workaround (l10 explore): each frame patches `state.values` with all sixteen `r{i}c{j}` cells. Wanted: `state.rows` (or `frames` accepting a rows patch) for tables that switch panels.
- Docs: `docs/LEVEL-BRIEF.md` says "look at the screenshots when you pass `--shots`", but `playthrough.mjs --shots` needs a folder argument and stops with "--shots needs a value" without one. Wanted: the brief shows `--shots <dir>`.

Raised later (momentum-rep level 3):

- `distribution` canvas, overlay labels: an `overlay` curve's label ("true 0.5%" in l13 screen 4) is drawn at the right end of the axis beside the main curve's tail, not on its own curve, so it reads as a label for the wrong curve. Wanted: overlay labels placed at the overlay's peak or with a `labelAt`. Workaround: the prose says which curve is dashed.
- `numbers_lib`: every level script that has code widgets copies its own harness to run solutions and starters against the hidden tests (momentum-rep L2 and L3, numerics, convex and others). Wanted: a shared `lv.run_code_widgets()` in `app/tools/numbers_lib.py` that handles `isclose`, `equal`, `raises`, `setup`, `prelude` and `tol`. Workaround: the harness is copied into each script.
Raised later (complex level 3):
- [Should] `table` widget grades a cell only as a number or as text, so a complex value cannot be an `expression` cell (the brief wanted l9's hook cells $f(\gamma(t))\gamma'(t) = i$ graded as expressions). Wanted: `"compare": "expression"` on a table cell, using the expression grader. Workaround: text answer `i` with an accept list (`1i`, `+i`, `0+i`, `0+1i`) and two named `wrong` texts.
- [Should] `plane` curve formulas have no complex arithmetic, so the Carr-Madan integrand $\operatorname{Re}[e^{-ivk}\psi(v)]$ had to be expanded by hand into a 220-character real formula in $v$ and $\alpha$ (l13 screens 3 and 6), checked against the complex form in the numbers script. Wanted: `re(...)`, `im(...)` and `i` in plane formulas, as `complex-map` has. Second sighting after numerics level 4.
- [Could] A control's `frameLabels` print KaTeX as raw text (`$U = 1$` showed with its dollars, l12 screen 5). Wanted: render maths in frame labels as in `options` chips. Workaround: plain `U = 1`.
- [Could] `plane` `area` label sits at the middle of the authored bounds, so an area over $[0, 40]$ on a $[0, 12]$ view puts "area 10.4506" on the axis at the right edge (l13 screen 3); the bounds must stay wide for an accurate value. Wanted: `labelAt` on an area. Workaround: none needed, the label stays readable.
- [Could] `numbers_lib` paths cannot name a hyphenated key (`feedback.byKey.test-1`), so checks on code feedback fall back to the whole `byKey` object. Wanted: `byKey["test-1"]` or hyphens in key names.
- [Could] Playthrough prints no package load time for a code screen (third sighting, after numerics 3 and stat-models 1). Measured instead in node Pyodide from the local copy: NumPy and SciPy 1.9 s.
Raised later (analysis level 5):
- [Should] `order` widget at 440 px: one line holding an inline KaTeX span wider than the card (about 45 TeX characters, such as a whole inequality chain) widens every card of the list past the screen edge, clipping their text and the cross buttons; the playthrough's "page wider than the window" check passes anyway. Workaround: split long spans into several `$...$` pieces with words between ("equals", "which is less than"). Wanted: the card scrolls its maths sideways (as display maths does), and a playthrough check for a card wider than its list.
- [Could] `playthrough.mjs --lesson` given twice plays only the last lesson, silently, while `--set` repeats. Wanted: `--lesson` repeatable, or an error. Workaround: a loop script.
- [Could] `plane` `steps` functions always draw an open dot at the right end of the last piece, so a step function on a closed $[0, 2]$ looks undefined at 2 (l21 jump screen). Wanted: a closed right end option. Workaround: last piece extended past the x range.
- [Could] `docs/reviews/voice-scan.py` scans a whole folder, so a level author cannot reach "0 and 0" while earlier levels hold long sentences (analysis levels 1 to 4 hold 28). Wanted: a file or level filter. Workaround: copied the level's seven files to a scratch folder and pointed `VOICE_BASE` there.
- [Could] `numbers_lib` paths cannot name a key with a hyphen (`feedback.byKey.right-ends` parses as `right`). Wanted: bracketed keys (`byKey["right-ends"]`). Workaround: end the path at `byKey`, which searches every line below.
Raised later (convex level 4):
- [Could] A control over whole numbers with a wide range (l15 hook: step $k$ from 0 to 760, step 1) moves about two steps per pixel on a 440 px phone, so landing on 691 needs the arrow keys. Wanted: plus and minus stepper buttons beside a control, or `fine: true` that halves the drag speed. Workaround: the caption prints the exact error at each $k$ and the hints name the crossing.
- [Could] Second sighting of the missing `sci(v, digits)` caption format (see "Raised later" bullet on `derive` captions above): l15's live caption has to print "error × 10⁶ is 1.015" instead of "1.015 × 10⁻⁶". Workaround: scale by 10⁶ in the caption text.
- [Could] `plane` with `yScale: "log"`: a function label held near the y axis (`labelAt` 6 on the κ = 2 line, l15 hook) sits on the tick labels at 440 px. Wanted: function labels that keep clear of tick labels as they do of curves. Workaround: none; the label stays readable.
Raised later (pairs-rep level 1):
- [Should] `series`: series hidden by `state.hide` (or a control frame's `hide`) still set the automatic y range, so a frames slider through six price lines drew each pair on a scale set by all six and the planted break was hard to see (l5 explore). Workaround: plot one derived series per frame (each pair's log gap) so the hidden lines share a scale. Wanted: auto y range from visible series only, or a per-frame `y`.
- [Could] `derive` cannot reduce over a series' values (a running sum of squared gaps between two series up to a window end), so l2's live SSD caption is a frames control with four precomputed captions instead of a continuous window slider.
- [Could] `bars` `state.cut` label sits on top of the value label of the bar beside the cut when `showValues` is on (l3 explore, "cut 1" over "928"). Workaround: none; minor overlap at desktop width.
Raised later (pairs-rep level 2):
- [Could] `orderbook`: no marker for a last-trade or closing print distinct from the bid and ask levels, so l9's hook ("the close printed at the bid") highlights `bid0` and says the rest in prose. Wanted: `state.last: { price, label }` drawn beside the book.
- [Could] `code` widget: hidden tests cannot share an expensive result, so homework-2's 127-cycle run sits in one test with `budget: 120000` and lessons sample one cycle in six (`step = 126`). Wanted: a per-widget memo the prelude can fill once (for example `setupOnce`) that every test reads.
Raised later (stat-models level 4):
- [Should] `bars` has no draggable bar heights: the l15 hook "drag the bars to guess the predictive, then reveal it" would need eleven controls, one per category. Workaround: one control on the plug-in rate $p$ redraws a Binomial(10, $p$) by `derive`, with the beta-binomial predictive as fixed `exact` lines. Wanted: `drag: true` on bars (a learner-set pmf, readable by `derive` and a `sliders`-style grader).
- [Should] `plane` with a long `xLabel` ("Default rate $\lambda$ per year") draws the label below the ticks, on top of the derive caption on desktop and outside the frame on the trap screen (l14). Workaround: the label shortened to `$\lambda$` with the unit in the prose. Wanted: the caption placed below a lowered x label.
- [Could] Code widget packages load from the learner's own imports only: a hidden test `setup` that imports `scipy.signal` fails with `ModuleNotFoundError` when the starter imports only NumPy (l17 `ess_ar1`). Workaround: a pure-NumPy AR(1) via `itertools.accumulate` in the setup. Wanted: the worker also scans `setup`, `prelude` and `postlude` for imports, or a widget `packages` list.
- [Could] A `derive` `caption` with no `{{...}}` is a validator error ("not an expression"), so a canvas factory that turns its live caption into a fixed one must move it to `state.caption` (l18). Wanted: plain text accepted as a constant caption, or a line in AUTHORING section 4.
- [Could] `numbers_lib.normalise` strips braces, so `$\sqrt{3.2}$` reads as `\sqrt3.2` and the 3.2 is never matched as a number (l14). Workaround: a string match on `\sqrt{3.2}`. Wanted: a space or separator left where a brace is removed after a command.
- [Could] No tool prints a canvas's derived caption, so the $\hat R$ the l17 `series` caption shows (from `ar1` and `rolling` in a `let`) had to be recomputed by a Node copy of `_base.js`'s seeded normals and confirmed on a Why? screenshot. Wanted: `playthrough.mjs --captions` printing each screen's caption after `explain.canvas`.

Raised later (vector-calc level 1):

- `plane` polyline function entries (`points: [[x, y], ...]`): `label` with `labelAt` is not placed at the given x; the three walk labels in l2 screen 11 all piled up near the left end, over the paths. Wanted: `labelAt` honoured on a polyline as on an `expr` curve. Workaround: no curve labels, and a caption naming the colours ("Blue: n = 4 ...").
- `space` `fields`: arrows are drawn only on an n by n by n grid. For work along a space curve (l3 screen 8, $\mathbf F = (y, z, x)$ on the helix) we wanted the arrows at points of the curve, for example `fields: [{ F, along: "h", n: 12 }]`, so the learner sees $\mathbf F$ against $\mathbf r'$. Workaround: a normalised 4 by 4 by 4 grid beside the bold curve.
Raised later (pairs-rep level 3):
- [Could] `bars` `hlines`: the label sits at the right end over the bars, and a long label ("247.5 a bin if no pair is cointegrated", l12 p-value histogram) overprints the bar tops at 1280 px. Wanted: hline labels placed clear of bars (above the plot, or at the left when bars are tall on the right). Workaround: a short label and the detail in the caption.
- [Could] `table` widget at 440 px: four columns with a text column ("From", "Value", "To", "Result") wrap to one word a line and push the inputs off the right edge (l15 results screen). Wanted: a stacked layout for narrow screens, one row a card. Workaround: three columns with short labels.
- [Could] Validator, number-script check: it looks for an import of `numbers_lib` and `Level(` in the script text, so the level 1 and 2 scripts, which import `Level` through `docs/reports/pairs_rep_common.py`, still warn "never uses numbers_lib". Wanted: follow one level of local imports. Workaround: the level 3 script imports `numbers_lib` directly.

Raised later (vector-calc level 3):

- `space` `curves`: no direction marker on a curve. Stokes (l12 screens 0, 2 and 9) needs the boundary's orientation shown (counterclockwise seen from above, or clockwise in the trap). Wanted: `arrow: true` (or `arrows: n`) on a curve entry, drawing small arrowheads along it in the direction of increasing `t`. Workaround: a short `vectors` entry tangent to the curve at one point, from a `points` entry.
Raised later (exam topic-3.1):
- [Could] `numbers_lib.Level` loads course levels only, so an exam number script cannot call `expect` or `check_wrongs` on an exam file. Workaround: a short `Exam(Level)` subclass that loads one exam JSON (`docs/briefs/exam-3.1-scripts/check_numbers.py`). Wanted: `numbers_lib.Exam("topic-3.1")`, and a note that a script named `numbers.py` shadows the standard library's `numbers` module (it breaks `fractions`), so exam scripts need another name.

Raised later (linear-maps level 4):
- [Should] `vectors` with `state.matrix`: the image grid, the arrows `b1` and `b2` and the unit square are always drawn, and `state.hide` cannot remove them. For a single operator acting on one arrow (l15 screens 3 and 6, l16 screens 0 and 1, l18 screen 0) `b1` lies exactly on the image of $\mathbf e_1$ (and on $\mathbf R\mathbf v$, $\mathbf R^2\mathbf v$ for the rotation), so two arrows and two labels overlap on a skewed grid. Wanted: `params.basisGrid: false` (or `hide: ["b1", "b2", "unitsq", "basis"]`) that keeps the matrix acting on the vectors but draws the plain grid. Workaround: no `state.matrix`; the image arrow is a plain vector whose `v` a `derive` formula writes (`"ax.v": "[2 * x.v[0] + x.v[1], x.v[0] + 2 * x.v[1]]"`).
- [Could] `vectors`: no `dashed` option on an arrow (the `space` canvas has one). Wanted for a target or reference arrow beside a movable one (l15 screen 1, where $a\mathbf A\mathbf v + b\mathbf v$ is steered onto $\mathbf A^2\mathbf v$). Workaround: distinct colours and labels.
- [Could] `playthrough.mjs --shots` needs a folder argument ("--shots needs a value"); `docs/LEVEL-BRIEF.md` says "when you pass `--shots`" with no value. Wanted: a default folder, or the brief to show `--shots <dir>`.
Raised later (algebra level 3):
- [Could] `complex-map` points: when several points share an image (z^4 on the 12th roots sends four points to each of three places), each image draws its own label and the last one wins, so the w-plane shows a misleading "f(9)" at 1. Wanted: images that coincide drawn once with a combined label or a count badge ("×4"). Workaround: `imageLabel: ""` on every point, with the counts asked in the widget (l10 screens 1 and 2).
- [Could] `grid` `rowHeads` and `colHeads` are params, so a `frames` control or a patch cannot reorder a Cayley table (rows by the cosets of one subgroup, then another). Wanted: heads (and the cell array) patchable in state, so one explore screen can switch the ordering. Workaround: a second screen with its own grid (l11 screens 1 and 2).

Raised later (algebra level 4):
- [Should] `plane` functions: in l17 screen 8 two curves `{ "id": "g", "expr": "x^2 + 2x + 2", "labelSide": "below" }` and `{ "id": "h", "expr": "x^2 - 2x + 2", ... }` beside `f` were not drawn at all at 440 or 1280 px, and the validator said nothing. Renaming them `q1`, `q2`, writing `2*x` and dropping `labelSide` drew both; the cause was not isolated. Wanted: the curve drawn, or a validator error naming the reason. Workaround: the renamed entries.
Raised later (algebra level 1):

- `vectors`: `segments` listed under `params` are not drawn; only `state.segments` is. The README lists `segments` beside the params-level `vectors` and `points`, so a square drawn from corner vectors showed no edges until the list moved into `state`. Wanted: seed `segments` (and `angles`) from params as `vectors` and `points` are, or say in the README that they are state only. Workaround: `state.segments` (algebra l1, all square and triangle canvases).
- `plane` in a `frames` control: a keyed `functions` patch (`{ "functions": { "f": { "expr": "..." } } }`) throws `object is not iterable` in `plane.js` `functionsOf`, although AUTHORING section 4 says a `functions` patch by id keeps the domain. Wanted: keyed patches on plane `functions` like other lists. Workaround: each frame gives the whole `functions` list (algebra l1 transfer, four payoffs and their mirrors).
- `expression` with a complex answer: parts are compared at 1e-9, so `0.5+0.866i` and the exact `\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}i` are different answers, and a learner typing 0.86603 fails. Wanted: an answer-level `tol` (absolute, per part) for decimal answers. Workaround: `answer` `0.5+0.866i` with the exact form, `e^{i\pi/3}`, `0.5+0.8660i` and the cos/sin form in `accept` (algebra l4 screen 6).

Raised later (algebra level 2):

- `manipulate`: the validator requires the target to be reachable, so an exploration whose point is that the target can never be reached (the 15-puzzle with 14 and 15 swapped) cannot be built as a slide game. Wanted: `"target": null` or `"explore": true`, graded by a following question, with each state still a canvas patch. Workaround: the target is "blank home again" after a four-slide loop, a refused "swap 14 and 15" chip states the rule, and the next screen asks whether the board can be solved (algebra l9 screens 0 and 1).
- `grid`: row and column heads are not tap targets, so "tap the four rotations" on a Cayley table cannot be asked of the heads. Wanted: head ids (`h-r2`, `h-c3`) in tap mode. Workaround: the learner taps cells inside the rotation block with the rest dimmed (algebra l5 screen 0) or a column of cells standing for the elements (l7 screen 0).

Raised later (session U orchestrator, from review-algebra-3-4):

- Validator: warn when a table screen's `feedback.byKey` key is an editable cell id (`r0c1`) and that cell has `wrong[]` entries. Since 576 the wrong id is the key; a cell-id key fires on every wrong answer except the slip, so the slip line is dead. algebra L3 and L4 had 13 such screens (fixed in their fix pass). Then scan every course for the pattern and list hits for a content fix pass.
Raised later (topology level 1):
- [Could] `space` and `plane` `frames` controls: a frame label longer than about eight characters ("octahedron") is clipped at the right edge at 440 px (l1 screen 8). Wanted: the readout wraps or the slider shortens. Workaround: labels d₁, d₂, d∞ and the names in the prose.
- [Could] `plane` `contour` entries: no way for one control to switch the formula, so "the unit ball of the chosen metric" (l2 hook) needs three contours plus three more for the small ball, and a `frames` control that hides four of six. Wanted: `exprs` with `index` on a contour entry, as `functions` already allow. Workaround: six contours and `hide` patches in frames.
- [Could] `plane` `area` with `label: "auto"` over $(1 - |x|^p)^{1/p}$: the curve has vertical tangents at $x = \pm1$, and the label's 400-strip Simpson value is not documented to 4 places there (l1 screen 4 asks for the area at $p = 4$). Wanted: the integration accuracy stated in README-B, or adaptive strips. Workaround: the caption prints the closed form `4 * exp(2 * lgamma(1 + 1/p) - lgamma(1 + 2/p))`.

Raised later (topology level 2):
- [Could] `code` widget: no course-level prelude, so the finite-space helpers `norm(tau)` and `T(*names)` are copied into the `prelude` of five widgets (l5 to l8, homework-2). Wanted: a `prelude` file per course (for example `content/courses/<course>/prelude.py`) that a widget names with `"preludeFile"`. Workaround: the same six lines in each prelude.
- [Could] `number-line` on a linear scale: no `format: "percent"` (it exists only for `scale: "log-odds"`), so the l8 return line (4% to 9%) needed a hand-written `labels` list. Wanted: `format: "percent"` for linear number lines. Workaround: `labels: [{ x, text }]`.
- [Could] `graph`: no layout helper for a Hasse diagram, so every finite topology (l5, l6, l8, three to eight sets) places each node by hand in rows by size. Wanted: `layout: "hasse"` that rows nodes by a `rank` field and centres each row. Workaround: hand-placed `x`, `y`.

Raised later (topology level 3):
- [Should] `number-line` with `tapIntervals`: an interval is a 4 px bar, so on a 440 px phone the six covering intervals of l9's hook are small tap targets, and rows 4 and 5 drew at one height. Wanted: a taller hit area round each interval bar (about 24 px) and `row` honoured for every interval. Workaround: intervals that do not overlap on a shared row, and labels I₁ to I₆.
- [Could] `derive` with no loop: the l13 cobweb is an 8-step `let` chain (9 hidden points, 16 segments), and the caption needs a further 32 `let` lines to show $x_{40}$, so a learner can read the limit to 0.001. This adds to the existing `iterate` request: `iterate(f, x0, n)` returning the list would replace both. Workaround: the long `let` chain.
- [Could] Playthrough: it types only the authored `expression` answer, so `accept` forms near a singular point (l12's $\tfrac{q^n}{1 - q}d$ at $q = 1$) are never tried. Wanted: a `--accept` pass that types each `accept` entry and expects it graded right. Workaround: answers whose `accept` forms are simple rearrangements.
- (from review-topology-1-2) Choice options are not shuffled unless `shuffle: true`, and app-wide 3,373 of 6,605 lesson `choice` widgets list the key first without it, so "pick the first option" scores about half of those. Proposed: a validator warning for a `choice` with the key at index 0 and no `shuffle`, then either a bulk `shuffle: true` pass (skipping options that are ordered or refer to position, as O2 detects) or a seeded default shuffle with `shuffle: false` as the opt-out. Needs a decision before content edits.
- (584 refinement) `[table-slip-key]` should warn only when a cell's `wrong[]` ids have no `byKey` line of their own; a cell-id line beside wrong-id lines is the deliberate generic line for that cell and fires correctly.
Raised later (exam 1.10):
- [Should] Topic exams: the validator checks skill coverage but not level coverage, so "every level has an item on a skill first met there" is checked by hand in each exam's script (`docs/briefs/exam-1.10-scripts/check_numbers.py` derives first-met skills from `course.json` and the lesson files). Wanted: a validator warning per level whose new skills no auto-graded exam item tests.
- [Should] Freshness: each exam writes its own `fresh.py` with hand-picked tokens. A clash on a key that follows from the setup (any $x^3 + kx$ on $[0, 2]$ gives $c = 2/\sqrt3 = 1.1547$, the key of limits l17) is caught only if the author lists that key. Wanted: a shared `tools/fresh.mjs` that compares every numeric key and `wrong` value of an exam with every key in the topic's lessons, reviews and homework.
- [Could] `expression` with a matrix answer in an exam: the question carries no hint of how to enter a matrix (the MathLive matrix template or `[[a, b], [c, d]]`), and the exam screen shows none. Wanted: a short format line under the field when the answer is a `pmatrix`.

Raised later (exam 0.6):
- [Could] Playthrough: `--exam` prints "25 items" for topic-0.6, which has 24 items, one of them in two parts. Wanted: count items, and give parts separately ("24 items, 25 graded answers"). Workaround: none needed; the score line (24 of 24) is right.

Raised later (exam 2.6):
- [Could] `table`: an editable numeric cell is about 70 px wide at 440 px, so a placeholder such as "number" shows as "numbe" (topic-2.6 ANOVA table, five cells in a row of five columns). Wanted: the placeholder is clipped with an ellipsis or the cell grows to fit it. Workaround: leave `placeholder` out, so the cell shows "?".
Raised later (exam 3.4):
- [Should] Validator and voice scan disagree on sentence length: a 22-word sentence in `topic-3.4.json` `items[28].prose` (fixed) raised no `O2-long-sentence` warning, while `docs/reviews/voice-scan.py` flagged it. Wanted: one shared word counter (how `$...$`, `|y - y'|` and a full stop inside maths count), used by both.
- [Could] `app/tools/numbers_lib.py` `normalise` joins cycle notation `$(1\,2\,3)$` into the number 123, so the every-number pass fails on permutations. Wanted: split digits at `\,` in `normalise` (or a documented option). Workaround: the exam script replaces `\,` between digits with a space before reading tokens.
- [Could] Freshness: all six clashes found here were setups in prose (a formula, an interval, a cycle), not numeric keys, and two came from the analysis course, which shares metric spaces with topology. The shared `tools/fresh.mjs` asked for under exam 1.10 should also compare formula strings in item prose, and should read the courses that share a topic's skills, not only the topic's own courses.
- [Could] Process: the scratchpad named in `docs/LEVEL-BRIEF.md` is shared by parallel agents, and a scratch patch file of this task (`patch2.py`) was overwritten mid-task by the exam 1.11 agent. Wanted: the brief asks each agent to work in a subfolder named after its task.

Raised later (session U orchestrator, protocol 6):

- Validator warning: a `choice` or `multi` with `shuffle: true` whose `explain`, `why`, hint or feedback names an option by place ("the first", "the second option", "option B", "the last one"). Third sighting: review-exam-3.2, review-exam-1.10, review-exam-3.4. Run it over lessons, reviews, homework, exams, drills and bank; list hits in `docs/reports/scan-positional-options.md`.
- Optional (two sightings, exams 1.10 and 3.4): warning when a choice key is more than 1.5 times the length of every distractor; and when every `multi` in an exam has the same number of keys.
- (Must, protocol 6: third sighting in review-exam-1.10, review-exam-0.6, review-exam-2.6) A shared freshness tool, `app/tools/fresh-check.mjs` or `.py`: per exam (or drill, bank file), flag any item whose prose and question share a long word n-gram (8 or more words after normalising numbers to a placeholder) or high shingle overlap with any lesson screen, review, homework or other exam item. The per-exam `fresh.py` scripts only compare keyed numbers, so word-for-word setups with a changed constant pass. Output: hits with both paths; exit 1 on any hit above the threshold. The 1.10 author asked for the same.
Raised later (exam 1.11):
- [Should] `expression` `wrong[]`: nothing checks that a wrong form differs from the key. The validator does not grade `wrong[].tex` against `answer`, and the playthrough types only `answer`. So a wrong form equal to the key (easy with complex or trigonometric forms) would never fire, and an `accept` form that is not equal would fail learners silently. Wanted: a validator or playthrough pass that grades every `accept` form (must pass) and every `wrong[].tex` (must fail) with the real grader. Workaround: each exam's `check_numbers.py` evaluates the LaTeX at sample points (`docs/briefs/exam-1.11-scripts/check_numbers.py`, `expr_check`).
- [Could] `numbers_lib.check_wrongs()`: its "check by hand" lines fire whenever a `byKey` line states the right intermediate numbers rather than the wrong value (30 lines on topic-1.11, all correct on reading). Wanted: silence the line when the byKey text states a number that matches any value the script has already checked for that item, or an opt-out per wrong id. Workaround: read the list by hand.
Raised later (exam 2.5):
- [Could] `numeric` with `tol: {"rel": r}`: the O2 explain-answer rule reads only `tol.abs`, so a tiny key such as $2^{-31}$ is matched within 0.6% only, and an explanation that writes $4.66 \times 10^{-10}$ is not read as a number at all (`numbersIn` takes 4.66 and 10 apart). Wanted: `numbersIn` reads `a \times 10^{b}` as one number, and the rule uses a relative tolerance when the widget has one. Workaround: the topic-2.5 explanation also prints the key as Python does, 4.656612873077393e-10.
Raised later (stage exams):
- [Should] `draw` takes at most four `tags` per entry, so a stage paper drawing from eight probability families needs two entries on the same pool (`stage-1` paper B). Allow a longer family list on a draw entry, or a `families` field, since the first-tag rule does not apply there.
- [Should] The 1.1 risk drills (`daily-returns`, `log-returns`, `annualised-vol`, `max-drawdown`, `sharpe-ratio`, `cumulative-pnl`, `rolling-mean`) carry no `pack` or shared id prefix, so a stage exam cannot draw them without a one-drill pool and a warning. A `pack: "py-risk"` on each (content) would let `stage-1` paper C draw its 1.1 task fresh per sitting.
- [Should] `tools/readiness-sums.mjs` fails on `block generated:catalogue is out of date` in this worktree before any exam change (the block counts courses and lessons); the main session should rerun it with `--write` after checking the tables.
- [Could] Stage exam result: `passed` needs every check ticked, and checks are ticked by position, so adding a check to an existing stage exam changes what a pass means for earlier attempts' readers. A note on the result page ("this test gained a check since your attempt") or ids on ticks would make extensions safe.
- [Could] A stage-exam `check` has no way to be optional, so `stage-0`'s `getting-hired` (0.7, which `docs/curriculum.md` keeps out of the exit test) gates the pass. An `"optional": true` on a check, shown but not required for `passed`, would match the curriculum.
- (from review-exam-1.11, engine item) An `expression` widget whose answer is a constant (no free symbols) grades by value, so retyping the prompt's unsimplified form (for example $\frac{4+7i}{2-3i}$) scores. Proposed: a `form` option (`rectangular` for a + bi, `simplified` for a single rational or surd) that rejects unevaluated operations, and a validator warning on a symbol-free expression answer with no `form`. Content workaround meanwhile: ask for the real and imaginary parts as two numeric parts.
- (588 refinement) `fresh-check.mjs` hits are dominated by LaTeX skeletons once numbers become placeholders (`x # x # mathbf x`, `tfrac langle mathbf v`). Count only word tokens in `run` (drop LaTeX command names, braces and `#` placeholders), or require at least 6 distinct English words in the run. Then re-measure the gate; today's `--min-run 12` still gives 7 to 23 hits on exams that passed a second read.
- (from review-exam-stage-1) A `required: true` flag on a paper item: the paper fails if that item is wrong, whatever the share. Each Stage 1 coding exit is one item in a paper passed at 0.7, so it can be failed while the stage passes. Also two validator warnings: a float `example` in a code widget with no `compare` (exact equality on floats), and a thousands comma inside `$...$` (KaTeX spacing).

Raised later (stage-3):
- [Should] A stage-exam `check` cannot be scoped to a role. The 3.5 exit asks researchers for an open case aloud in 45 minutes and developers for a feed handler defence; as plain checks they would block every other role, so `stage-3` folds them into the mock-interview check's detail. A `"roles": ["research"]` field, required only when the learner's target role matches, would let the stage gate follow the curriculum.
- [Could] Generated `arith` items are four-option choices, while Zetamac is typed. A `"typed": true` mode (numeric entry, Enter to submit) would let Paper B of `stage-3` model Zetamac's pace exactly instead of standing beside a Zetamac check.
- [Could] `playthrough.mjs --shots` names exam screenshots `<exam>-item-NN` by index within a paper, so a multi-paper exam overwrites earlier papers' shots. Prefix the paper id (`stage-3-probability-item-05`).
- [Could] `fresh-check.mjs` flags standard instruction lines ("Put the steps in order. Leave out the two that do not belong"). A stop list of the AUTHORING instruction sentences would drop these from every exam's hit list.
- [Could] Content: the Python medium pool for a two-problem paper is 7 drills (`oa` 4, coding-int 3). More Python difficulty-3 drills would keep resits of `stage-3` and `mock-oa-code` unseen.
- (from review-exam-stage-3) (a) Count a drill or bank item drawn in any exam or mock as "seen" for unseen-first draws: stage-3 paper D's small pools are shared with stage-1 paper D and `mock-oa-code`. (b) `arith` generator near misses should keep the units digit, so a distractor cannot be ruled out by the last digit alone. (c) Let the Zetamac-style check tick itself from a logged Today warm-up score. (d) Validator warnings: a float artefact in shown text (`4.499999999999999`), and a numeric wrong value whose band is narrower than the rounding the question asks for (12 `byKey` lines in stage-3 never fire).
## Raised later (mock mock-oa-strats):

- Bank: no calculus, linear algebra or differential-equation families exist, so `mock-oa-strats` paper 2 (8 maths `choice` items) is fixed and a resat repeats it with shuffled options. Families such as `chain-rule`, `limit-series`, `by-parts`, `linear-ode`, `eigenvalues-2x2` and `correlation-bound` (role `strats`) would let it `draw` four of its eight afresh, as `mock-superday-research` does for probability.
- Playthrough `--shots`: exam screenshots are named by item index only (`<id>-item-00`, `<id>-start--1`), so on a mock with two papers the second paper's items and the check page overwrite the first paper's items and the start card. Workaround: a separate Playwright script for the start card and the first item of each paper. Proposed: put the paper index in the name (`<id>-p0-item-00`, `<id>-start`, `<id>-checks`).

## Raised later (mock mock-oa-code-hard):

- `code` widget: one `language` per item, but the reported developer tests let the candidate choose Python or C++ for each problem. `mock-oa-code-hard` fixes H1 and H2 in Python and H3 in C++. Proposed: an item that offers both languages (a starter, solution and tests for each, one graded), so a learner can sit the whole paper in either.
- `draw`: `mock-oa-code-hard` is three fixed problems, so a resat repeats them. A harder drill pack drawn by difficulty, with the `language` filter already asked for under `mock-oa-quant-data`, would give a fresh sitting and allow one C++ draw beside two Python draws. Workaround: fixed items, with large inputs built in hidden `setup` lines so the file stays small.
Raised later (stage-2):
- [Should] Python `code` widget: packages load from the learner's own imports only, so a hidden test whose `setup` imports pandas fails with `ModuleNotFoundError` when the code imports only numpy (seen on five `stage-2` items before their solutions gained `import pandas as pd`). A learner who deletes the starter's import gets the same confusing failure. Wanted: also scan `setup`, `prelude` and `postlude` for imports. Workaround: every starter and solution imports what the setups use.
- [Should] `fresh-check.mjs` compares prose only, so a code task that repeats a lesson's task passes: the first `stage-2` build reused `walk_forward_mse` (validation l6), `irls_logistic`/`irls_poisson` (stat-models l4), `metropolis` (stat-models l16) and `implied_vol` with bounds (opt-dash l9) without a hit; only `fresh.py`'s name grep found them. Wanted: compare code `starter` function names and signatures, and the `question` of code widgets, against every lesson, review and homework code task.
- [Could] `playthrough.mjs --shots` on a multi-paper exam names item shots by the index within the paper (`stage-2-item-03`), so each paper overwrites the one before and only the last paper survives. Wanted: put the paper index in the name. Workaround here: a scratch copy of the exam with one paper at a time.
- [Could] `playthrough.mjs`: when several code items fail in one exam, the failure line of a later item quoted an earlier item's first failing test (`item 19 part 2` showed `irls_logistic`'s test 1), so the text under each failure is not reliably its own. Wanted: read the failing row from that item's own results panel.
Raised later (mock mock-superday-strats):
- [Should] `playthrough.mjs --exam <id> --shots`: an exam item's screenshot is named `<id>-item-<screenIndex>`, and the index restarts in each paper, so every later paper overwrites the earlier papers' shots and only the last paper survives. Workaround: a scratch Playwright script (`scratchpad/exam-superday/shots.mjs`) that steps through the papers and names shots `p<paper>-i<index>`. Wanted: the paper index in the file name.
- [Could] Mock results page at 390 and 440 px: the page is wider than the window (the playthrough notes it on the time-up result; `mock-superday-research` does the same at 390 px), and the score trend prints an unrounded net score ("Latest 3.333333") after a time-up with part credit. Wanted: no sideways overflow, and the trend line rounded as the headline is (3.33).
- [Could] Bank families for strats: `bank/` has no derivatives, Greeks, stochastic calculus or numerics family, so `mock-superday-strats` draws nothing and repeats its 20 items on a resit. Wanted: a `bank/derivatives-1` to `-4` set with `role: ["strats"]` so a strats paper can draw.

Raised later (mock mock-superday-developer):
- [Could] C++ `code` widget, per-case time: a price-store test of 200,000 `std::map` inserts and 200,000 queries ran past the 5 s case limit in the playthrough, while the same code natively takes about 0.1 s, so an author cannot size a large test from a native run. Workaround: the test was halved to 100,000 ticks and 100,000 queries, which passes. Wanted: the playthrough prints the slowest case time of each code widget, so a large test keeps a stated margin under its limit.
- [Could] C++ `code` widget, threads: the runtime has no threads, so the concurrency paper of `mock-superday-developer` asks choices about code the learner reads (a lost update, a relaxed flag, a lock order) and never runs it; the check script runs that code natively with `-pthread`. Wanted: a deterministic interleaving harness (two coroutines stepped by a seeded scheduler) so a learner's lock or atomic can be tested in the browser.

- [Could] Seen again (fifth time): `fresh-check.mjs --file content/courses/<c>/<set>.json` exits ENOENT unless `--no-code`, and with `--no-code` every item matches itself (share 1, Jaccard 1). Wanted: skip same-file, same-item pairs and read code from the given file.

Strip a command name before its braced argument when tokenising.

Compare `pmatrix` bodies and tuple literals as whole tokens.

Add the rule to AUTHORING's pytest notes, and warn on the pytest 9 spellings (review findings 2 and 10).

Raised later (review sql L1 to L3):
- [Should] Playthrough or validator: an SQL code test can expect a tie order its query does not fix. In sql homework-2 `items[2]` test 2, HSBA and SHEL tie on `COUNT(*)` and the test wants SHEL first, so a learner who adds `, ticker` fails, while every SQLite build agreed and the playthrough passed. Rerun each SQL solution with its `ORDER BY` list extended by every selected column, ascending and descending, and flag a test whose expected rows change.
- [Should] AUTHORING code section: name the SQL engine. The app runs SQLite 3.39.0 (Pyodide 0.27.7), while author scripts use local SQLite 3.45 or newer. In 3.39.0 `SUM` and `AVG` of floats can differ in the last digit (`2.1500000000000004` against `2.15`), so SQL tests should `ROUND` float aggregates or use `isclose`. 3.39.0 has `FULL` and `RIGHT JOIN`, `NULLS FIRST`/`LAST`, `IS DISTINCT FROM` and the math functions, but not `string_agg`. Note SQLite's documented quirks that content must not deny: a bare column beside a lone `MIN` or `MAX` comes from that row, and a text `PRIMARY KEY` accepts `NULL` (review-sql-1-3 findings 3, 5, 7).
- [Could] Validator `key-length`: exempt a choice whose options are all single code spans and whose key starts with a distractor's text (an SQL `ON` with one more condition: sql l10 `screens[5]`, homework-3 `items[3]`). Such a key is long by construction.

- [Should, numbered 619] Expression grader: a correct formula typed with a lone letter on one side (`C = 3 + 2n`, `\text{cost} = 3 + 2n`, `3 + 2n = C`) is graded wrong with the generic line when the authored `answer` has no `=` (checked on the real `grade.js` with Compute Engine 0.30.2 against equations l1 `screens[3]`). GCSE learners write formulas this way. When the answer has no `=` and the input is `<one symbol or \text{...}> = <expr>` on either side, grade `<expr>` and, if equal, return a key `formula` with the line "That is right as a formula.
